# Design System — design.md

## Overview

This design system is a themeable component library and documentation website. The atmosphere is built from roles, not colours: a **page floor** (`--color-bg-page-primary`) carrying **containers** (`--color-bg-container-primary`) that step through a neutral hierarchy, where the point is the contrast between floor and container, not its direction (a theme may set light containers on a deeper floor or the reverse), and a single **action colour** (`--color-action-primary-bg`) that carries all of the brand's voltage. The action colour is deliberately split between light and dark mode: one colour rarely clears 3:1 against both a light page and a near-black one while also carrying a 4.5:1 label, so each mode pairs its own fill with its own label, and the build holds every pairing to contrast in every theme.

**This spec is theme-agnostic.** The library ships several complete themes (see Preset themes below), and every statement here is meant to stay true under all of them. Rules are therefore written in token roles: "the action colour", "the page floor", "the primary family", never the colour or face one theme gives them. Two looks have a special standing, and neither is named here because each has one authoritative home: the **base theme** is what the raw token files in `src/tokens/` carry, which a package consumer gets with no `data-brand` attribute (`DEFAULT_THEME_LABEL` in `website/src/lib/theme/presets.ts` names it), and the **served theme** is the `data-brand` the website's root layout renders on `<html>`. Colour values are never stated in this document: `/foundations/colour-mode` lists every colour token with a chip that paints the live value under whichever theme is applied, beside the base theme's wiring, and `/foundations/themes` shows each theme whole, with a specimen page per theme beside its generated DESIGN.md. Where a dimension appears — a px radius, a ms duration, a font weight, a type size — it is the base theme's position, given for orientation, and the density, type, motion and radius levers can move it per theme. "Theme" in this document means a preset; light and dark are **modes** of every theme.

The type system runs **one face per family role** — a heading role and a body role, both resolving to `--font-family-primary` unless a theme splits them — set at weight 300 (display/hero) → 600 (headings) → 500/400 (body/UI) in the base theme, with one site-chrome exception — the header wordmark sits at 700, one step above title-body's 600, so the name reads heavier than the nav pills beside it (the Site header pattern owns the detail) — and one library exception, the iOS and Android variants of BottomNav and TopAppBar, which use each platform's own face so a mock reads as the real system chrome (the BottomNav spec owns it). Monospace appears only in sanctioned code contexts through `--font-family-code`. Which faces fill the roles is a theme's choice, and so is whether they are one face or two: the scale chains through `--font-family-heading` for the display and heading tiers and `--font-family-body` for the body tiers, both resolving to `--font-family-primary` by default, so a theme or a consumer splits heading and body faces by overriding the roles (several presets do; see Font Family below).

The **three-tier token architecture** is the defining structural rule:
1. **Primitives** (`--primitive-*`) — raw hex/px values. Source of truth. Never used directly in components.
2. **Semantic tokens** (`--color-*`, `--radius-*`, `--gap-*`, `--padding-*`, `--border-*`, `--font-*`, `--motion-*`, `--icon-size-*`, `--shadow-*` — `CATEGORY_PREFIXES` in `scripts/generate-token-registry.mjs` is the authoritative list) — usage-intent variables consumed by components. Always use these.
3. **Component CSS classes** (`.ds-button`, `.ds-badge`, etc.) — per-component scope, referencing semantic tokens.

The system is **light/dark-first**: every semantic color token has a light-mode value and a dark-mode override. The switch is driven by `data-theme="dark"` on the root element. Status border keys stay perceptually stable across modes; surfaces and text invert.

**Key Characteristics:**
- Contrast between the page floor (`--color-bg-page-primary`) and the primary container (`--color-bg-container-primary`) is what separates a surface from the page. The direction is the theme's call: containers can sit lighter than the floor or deeper than it, and the two modes can differ. Primary text (`--color-text-primary`) sits at the far end of the neutral ramp from the floor in both modes.
- One action colour (`--color-action-primary-bg`). Used exclusively on primary CTA buttons, focus rings, active input borders, the checked or on state of a form control (Checkbox, Slider's fill, ToggleSwitch, the selected-option check in a menu), and the selected item of a mutually exclusive set (the action selection convention: SegmentedControl's active segment, Pagination's current page, Stepper's active step, the site header's current-section pill), and the hover and focus signal of a surface that is itself a control (an expandable BentoTile's border), with one sanctioned data-viz exception: the action colour leads the chart series palette. Never decorative elsewhere.
- One face per family role; the base theme uses one face for both roles, and a theme may split them. In the base theme: weight 300 for Mega/Display (marketing), 600 for headings, 500/400 for body and UI labels; 700 appears once, on the site header's wordmark. Tier weights are the theme's call, the same way the faces are: a theme may set the display and heading tiers to any weight, including the same one, and shift the body tier as a unit.
- Container hierarchy as depth signal — standard containers carry no drop shadows. Depth is conveyed by stepping through `--color-bg-container-primary` → `secondary` → `tertiary`; the only shadows are the `--shadow-floating`/`--shadow-modal` tokens on floating surfaces, `--shadow-table-pinned` on a pinned table column's scrolling edge, the interactive-card hover lift, and the iOS glass of the mobile navigation components, which carries the platform's own shadow.
- Five semantic status variants running through every feedback component: `info`, `positive`, `warning`, `error`, `neutral`.
- Border radius is hierarchical and assigned per element type: `--radius-100` for badges, `--radius-300` for inputs and standard containers, `--radius-600` for the sanctioned soft-container set the Border Radius Scale table below enumerates (navigation tiles, chat surfaces, Panel, the floating sidebar), `--radius-pill` for buttons. A theme moves the values; the assignments hold.
- Material Symbols Rounded as the bundled icon set (a library choice, not a structural limit — see the icon style hooks and the node-accepting icon props), on a four-step size scale — `--icon-size-500` (20px) / `600` (24px, default) / `800` (32px) / `1200` (48px). Optical size tracks the step automatically.
- One GPU-rendered surface: **ShaderField**, an ambient field of soft light sources that sample the colour tokens at runtime, so it re-themes with everything else. It is scenery rather than interface, it reports how it resolved so a caller can keep a fallback painted, and its `prefers-reduced-motion` check lives in JavaScript, where the CSS guard cannot reach (Carousel's autoplay gate makes the same move).
- Accessibility-first: ARIA roles, semantic HTML, and keyboard navigation in every interactive component.

### Preset themes

The playground's saved looks are shippable build artifacts, not demos. `THEME_PRESETS` in `website/src/lib/theme/presets.ts` is the registry of complete themes — every lever holds a position, never inherits one: the action brand (with an optional theme-dependent `brandDark`), neutral tint, radius scale and pill switch, spacing `density`, `typeScale`, `motionScale`, the `elevation` variant, the three tier weights (`displayWeight`, `headingWeight`, and `bodyWeight`, which shifts the body tier rather than setting it), a `tracking` offset, the icon font's `iconWeight` and `iconFill`, the body and heading typefaces, the ambient accent sextet (`accents`), the optional background-field colours (`ambient`) and AI gradient shape (`aiGradient`), hand-tuned ramp re-keys (`advanced`), and per-preset extras — colour corrections with dark-only variants where the derived ramp's shape inverts, but any token is fair game (Forest and Zest step Sub Display a notch under their display tier). The ambient accent sextet is part of every theme's declaration: the six `--color-core-accent-*` roles ship as hexes per preset and drive the background blobs and chart series 2–7 together. One pure composer, `presetOverrides(preset, theme)`, turns a preset into its exact override map, and the generated stylesheets are written from it; the playground's live preview composes the same lever functions in a memo of its own that mirrors it. The neutral tint is the one lever whose primitives differ by theme: light mode's two surface steps, the container white and the page floor, take only a share of the tint (`LIGHT_SURFACE_TINT_RATIO` in `website/src/lib/theme/theme-overrides.ts`), because a full-strength tint greys a light floor far enough to cost body copy its contrast, while dark mode reads the same steps as ink and keeps the tint whole. A tinted preset's two lightest neutrals therefore differ between its light and dark blocks, and a hand-written tint override needs both.

**The base sheet.** `tokens/base.css` is optional and imported once after `tokens.css`, which does not pull it in: the reset every component is measured against (border-box sizing, no margin or padding an element did not ask for), `color-scheme` per mode so native controls and scrollbars follow dark mode, the body's font, colour and background, and headings and paragraphs on the type tokens. Every rule sits inside `:where()`, so it carries no specificity and a consumer's own class always wins. It is the sheet the documentation site has always run under, published so a consumer's first screen measures as the site does.

`scripts/generate-preset-stylesheets.mjs` writes each preset to `src/tokens/presets/<id>.css` — an `html[data-brand="<id>"]` block carrying the light declarations and an `html[data-brand="<id>"][data-theme="dark"]` block carrying the dark ones, emitted even when identical so the cascade is self-evident — plus a `presets.css` aggregate that only imports them all. The files ship in the npm package under the `tokens/presets/` subpath. The contract: import `tokens/presets/presets.css` after `tokens/tokens.css`, set one `data-brand` attribute on `<html>`, and the whole app rethemes end to end — light and dark included, zero runtime JavaScript. Specificity does the work: `html[data-brand]` outranks the token files' `:root` and `[data-theme]` blocks, and the preset's dark block outranks its light one. The documentation website loads the bundle from its root layout, so any page rethemes the moment the attribute is set.

Two build gates guard the surface. `scripts/validate-preset-stylesheets.mjs` regenerates in memory and byte-compares every file, holds the folder to `THEME_PRESETS` in both directions, and parses one preset's CSS back into declarations to prove it equals the composer's output. `scripts/validate-theme-presets.mjs` is the completeness gate — the "one attribute, full theme" guarantee: every override name must resolve to a real token, a primitive, or one of the icon font's axis hooks, the action family must be overridden or provably intended-default (the brand equals `DEFAULT_BRAND`, which the action lever short-circuits to the base token files), every action fill must hold contrast with the label or icon components draw on it, in every state (resting, hover and pressed; labels at WCAG AA 4.5:1, icons and strokes at 3:1) and in both themes, for every preset and for the base token files (the script's `ACTION_PAIRINGS` table is authoritative, and every action fill token must appear in it; a cell that deliberately cannot hold would be pinned in `SANCTIONED_AA_GAPS` with its ratio and reason, never silently passed — the map is empty today, every cell holds), and every feel lever must be present on every preset (the script's lever list is authoritative) — as must all six ambient accent roles, in both themes, with no intended-default exemption.

---

## Colors

### Token Tiers

Never reference `--primitive-*` tokens inside components. Always use the semantic layer (`--color-*`). The chain is enforced in the other direction too: every semantic colour token's value must be a `var(--primitive-*)` reference — never a raw hex/rgba literal — so that overriding a primitive re-themes everything built on it (`scripts/validate-token-references.mjs` fails the build otherwise). That chain is also what makes the themes possible: a preset re-keys the primitive ramps and re-points semantic roles, and every component follows. This section therefore describes what each colour role is for and how it relates to its neighbours, never what colour it is; where a role's wiring is described in primitive steps, that is the base token files' wiring.

The primitive colour scales share one shape. The neutral scale and the seven chromatic ramps (red, orange, yellow, green, teal, blue, purple) all run **00–11**, light to dark — relative luminance decreases strictly down every chromatic ramp, build-enforced by `scripts/validate-theme-mirrors.mjs`. Step 07 is each chromatic ramp's hand-drawn key colour; the 00 and 11 end caps are generated tint/shade extensions (the blend method and factors are documented above the ramps in `tokens-primitives.css`). Translucent neutral variants follow the **`-aNN` grammar** — NN is the alpha × 100, so `--primitive-neutral-09-a80` is neutral-09 at 80% opacity — and the suffix is held to the rgba() value's actual alpha by `scripts/validate-token-references.mjs`.

### Action / Brand

The action roles are the one token family that splits per mode by design (see Overview): each mode pairs its own primary fill with its own label (the neutral roles stay neutral-on-neutral in each mode), and by default hover/active walk away from the page — deepening in light, brightening in dark. The rule underneath is that state fills walk away from their **label**, so every state keeps the label's contrast. For a deep light-mode key and a light dark-mode key the two rules agree. For other keys they can differ: a mid-tone key in dark mode carries a light label, so its states deepen, and a light key in light mode carries a dark label, so its states lighten. The action lever (`actionPointerOverrides` in `website/src/lib/theme/theme-overrides.ts`) applies the label rule, and `scripts/validate-theme-presets.mjs` checks every state of every preset against it.

- **Primary bg** (`--color-action-primary-bg`): The action colour. Every primary CTA button fill, focus rings, active input borders, and selected-state fills under the action selection convention. The most-recognized brand color.
- **Primary bg hover** (`--color-action-primary-bg-hover`): Hover shift on primary buttons.
- **Primary bg active** (`--color-action-primary-bg-active`): Press/active shift on primary buttons.
- **Primary text** (`--color-action-primary-text`): Text/icon color on the primary fill — a tint of the action key on a deep fill, a shade of it on a light one.
- **Primary text active** (`--color-action-primary-text-active`): Text on hovered/active primary button.
- **Icon active** (`--color-action-icon-active` — an alias of `--color-action-primary-text-active` in both themes): The icon on a hovered/active primary fill, including the outlined CircularButton's. It is an alias rather than its own step because it sits on exactly the fills the active label does, so a theme that repaints those fills repaints labels and icons together and the two can never disagree.
- **Primary border** (`--color-action-primary-border`): Outline on secondary (outlined) Buttons and CircularButtons.
- **Secondary border** (`--color-action-primary-border-secondary`): Brighter border step — the completed-step border and connector in Stepper.
- **Tertiary border** (`--color-action-primary-border-tertiary`): Hover ring on RadioButton.
- **Tertiary text** (`--color-action-primary-text-tertiary`): Action-coloured text for tertiary/ghost button labels.
- **Passive bg** (`--color-action-passive-bg`): Near-transparent ghost button fill.
- **Passive bg hover** (`--color-action-passive-bg-hover`): Translucent neutral hover on ghost/tertiary buttons.
- **Passive text** (`--color-action-passive-text`): Ghost button label color.
- **Neutral bg** (`--color-action-neutral-bg`): Solid neutral fill for primary-emphasis buttons in the `neutral` tone — unlike passive, the background is always shown at rest.
- **Neutral bg hover** (`--color-action-neutral-bg-hover`): Hover shift on neutral buttons — deepening in light, brightening in dark.
- **Neutral bg active** (`--color-action-neutral-bg-active`): Press/active shift on neutral buttons.
- **Neutral text** (`--color-action-neutral-text`): Text/icon color on the neutral fill.
- **Neutral border** (`--color-action-neutral-border`): The outline of secondary-emphasis buttons in the `neutral` tone — the neutral counterpart of `--color-action-primary-border`.
- **Destructive bg** (`--color-action-destructive-bg`): The danger fill — what outlined destructive controls take on hover and press, and filled destructive buttons wear at rest. Keyed to the red ramp the error status uses, never to an accent, so danger reads as danger in every theme (a preset whose action colour sits on the red ramp rebases it, and danger follows that red like its error status does).
- **Destructive bg hover / active** (`--color-action-destructive-bg-hover`, `--color-action-destructive-bg-active`): The filled destructive button's hover and press steps, walking away from the page like the primary family.
- **Destructive text** (`--color-action-destructive-text`): Label and icon on the destructive fill, AA on every step in every theme.
- **Destructive text secondary** (`--color-action-destructive-text-secondary`): Label and icon of a destructive control on the page — outlined destructive buttons, destructive menu and command rows — one step further from the page than the fill, so it holds AA on the page and on the error tint behind a highlighted menu row.
- **Destructive border** (`--color-action-destructive-border`): The outline of destructive controls, on the fill's step.

### Surfaces
- **Page primary** (`--color-bg-page-primary`): The floor of every screen.
- **Container primary** (`--color-bg-container-primary`): First elevation above page — sidebars, section bands, card fills. What makes it read as a surface is its contrast with the floor, not which of the two is lighter: a theme may set it lighter or deeper than the page, and the two modes may go opposite ways.
- **Container secondary** (`--color-bg-container-secondary`): Second elevation — nested containers, divider fills.
- **Container tertiary** (`--color-bg-container-tertiary`): Third elevation — pressed states, control tracks (Slider, ProgressBar, Meter, Spinner, Gauge), deepest nesting.
- **Container border** (`--color-bg-container-border`): Hairline borders on containers.
- **Container inverse** (`--color-bg-container-inverse`): High-contrast inverted surface — ImageCompare's handle label, the calendar and Gantt "today" markers. Always paired with `--color-text-on-inverse`.
- **Glass** (`--color-bg-glass`): The floating-surface fill — deliberately translucent in both modes so the page's colours reach the surface. Always paired with `backdrop-filter: blur(24px)`, which is what keeps content readable through it; the blur radius is a documented constant, not a token. Used by the site chat panel, AppSidebar's floating variant, CommandPalette's panel, and the chart family's tooltips.
- **Divider** (`--color-divider`): Horizontal/vertical rule between sections.

### Table surfaces
Every table reads one family, so a markdown table in a doc page or a chat reply and a data table in a product UI are drawn alike: the library `Table` (plain and `bordered`), `DataTable`, `Prose`'s markdown tables and the site's blueprints tables. A table is a surface of its own and stands off what it sits on, which takes a different move per mode: in light it is brighter than the page, the card fill with a header a touch off it; in dark it is a little darker than its surface, the page floor with the header one container step up, so on a panel or a card it reads as an inset. Each role aliases a container or page role, so a theme that retunes the ramp retunes its tables, and `scripts/validate-table-styles.mjs` fails the build when any other stylesheet colours a table part or a drawing file colours one outside the family.
- **Body** (`--color-table-body-bg`): light `--color-bg-container-primary`, dark `--color-bg-page-primary`.
- **Header** (`--color-table-header-bg`): a half step off the body in both modes, between the container steps either side of it: light `--primitive-neutral-02-a50`, dark `--primitive-neutral-08-a50` (the scale's 02 and 08 greys at half strength, which every preset re-tints with its neutral).
- **Row hover** (`--color-table-row-hover-bg`): light `--color-bg-container-secondary`, dark `--color-bg-container-primary`: lighter than the header, so a hovered row never reads as a second header.
- **Row stripe** (`--color-table-row-stripe-bg`): light `--color-bg-container-secondary`, dark `--color-bg-container-primary-semi`.
- **Row selected** (`--color-table-row-selected-bg`): light `--color-bg-container-tertiary`, dark `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`; held while the checkbox is on.
- **Divider** (`--color-table-divider` → `--color-divider`): The line between rows and under the header.
- **Border** (`--color-table-border` → `--color-divider`): The bordered table's outline.

### Chat surfaces
Chat surfaces get their own colour roles so they can be re-themed without touching the container ramp. The four bubble roles currently alias the container-secondary / text-primary pair — deliberately identical today, separable later; the context fill is its own tint.
- **Sent bg** (`--color-chat-bubble-sent-bg` → `--color-bg-container-secondary`): Fill of the person's outgoing bubble.
- **Sent text** (`--color-chat-bubble-sent-text` → `--color-text-primary`): Text inside the sent bubble.
- **Received bg** (`--color-chat-bubble-received-bg` → `--color-bg-container-secondary`): Fill of a received bubble, when the agent's turn renders as a bubble at all.
- **Received text** (`--color-chat-bubble-received-text` → `--color-text-primary`): Text inside the received bubble.
- **Context bg** (`--color-chat-context-bg` → `--primitive-neutral-01` light / `--primitive-neutral-09` dark): Fill of the Composer's context note — the quietest tint per theme, the nearest neutral to each shell that still separates from it. Its own role rather than a container alias because it deliberately sits off the container ramp.

ChatMessage consumes only the four bubble roles above — never the container tokens directly. The action colour is never a bubble fill; it keeps its CTA meaning.

### Calendar surfaces
EventCalendar's day cells and event pills get their own colour roles, for the same reason chat surfaces do — tunable without touching the container ramp. The family exists because the dark container ramp's steps are too far apart for calendar density: a cell field on the secondary container step glares against the near-black calendar container, and a hover that lands between the cell and pill fills swallows both. Cells rest one quiet step off the container and step one gentle notch away from the pill fill on hover — lighter in dark, darker in light; a hovered cell keeps its hover while the pointer is on a pill inside it, so the pill's own hover fill and hover-only border ring are what separate it.
- **Cell bg** (`--color-calendar-cell-bg` — `--primitive-neutral-01` light / `--primitive-neutral-08-a80` dark): Resting fill of an in-month day cell.
- **Cell bg hover** (`--color-calendar-cell-bg-hover` — `--primitive-neutral-02-a80` light / `--primitive-neutral-07-a80` dark): The clickable cell's hover — one gentle step lighter in dark, one gentle step darker in light, always away from the pill fill.
- **Event bg** (`--color-calendar-event-bg` → `--color-bg-container-primary`): Resting fill of an event pill — the calendar container's own surface, so pills read as windows down to it.
- **Event bg hover** (`--color-calendar-event-bg-hover` → `--color-calendar-cell-bg`): A hovered pill takes the *resting* cell tint — always one step off the hovered cell it sits on (darker than that cell in dark, lighter in light). The "+N more" overflow row's hover shares it, so every interactive row inside a cell separates the same way.
- **Event border** (`--color-calendar-event-border` — `--primitive-neutral-04` light / `--primitive-neutral-06` dark): The hover-only hairline ring on the pill under the pointer — an obvious mid-grey that reads against both cell states, while resting pills carry a transparent border so the ring appears with no layout shift. Its own role because the container border colour sinks into the dark cell fill.

### Overlay & Controls
- **Scrim** (`--color-scrim`): Modal backdrop behind Dialog, AlertDialog, Drawer and CommandPalette. Darker in dark mode so the modal still separates from the near-black floor.
- **Control thumb** (`--color-control-thumb` — chains to `--color-action-primary-text` in both modes): The circular thumb inside toggle switches (SwitchIndicator, the track ToggleSwitch, InspectorToggleSwitch and SelectionCard's toggle mode all draw). Riding the on-action ink means every theme's AA-held action pairing keeps the thumb legible on its track — a light dark-mode action fill (Smoke, Volt) gets a dark thumb automatically, where a fixed near-white value vanished into it.

### Text
- **Primary** (`--color-text-primary`): Headlines and primary content — the far end of the neutral ramp from the page floor.
- **Secondary** (`--color-text-secondary`): Emphasized body, card subheadings.
- **Tertiary** (`--color-text-tertiary`): Labels, helper text, captions.
- **Inverse** (`--color-text-inverse`): De-emphasized secondary labels; inverts in dark mode.
- **On inverse** (`--color-text-on-inverse`): Text sitting on `--color-bg-container-inverse` surfaces (ImageCompare's handle label, the "today" markers).

### Icons
- **Primary** (`--color-icon-primary`): Default icon fill — tertiary buttons, input icons, nav icons.
- **Secondary** (`--color-icon-secondary`): De-emphasized icons.

### Status (backgrounds and text invert between modes; borders hold their key)
Each of the five variants (`positive`, `warning`, `error`, `info`, `neutral`) carries the same steps, drawn from one ramp per variant (green, orange, red and blue in the base wiring; `neutral` from the neutral ramp):

| Step | Light mode | Dark mode |
|---|---|---|
| `--color-status-<role>-bg` | A pale tint of the ramp | A deep shade of the ramp |
| `--color-status-<role>-border` | The ramp's key colour | The same key (`neutral` steps one notch lighter) |
| `--color-status-<role>-text` | A deep shade, readable on the pale tint | A pale tint, readable on the deep shade |

A theme that re-keys the chromatic ramps re-keys its status colours with them; what holds is the role-to-ramp wiring and the contrast between each background and its text.

Each role also carries an **icon step** (`--color-status-<role>-icon`): the glyph colour for status indicators sitting on plain container surfaces (AgentPlan's step indicators, ToolCall's state glyph, SourceTrail's rows). The text step is tuned for its tinted status background and washes out on a bare container — in dark mode the near-white text tints all read as grey. Light mode uses the deep inks, around step 08 of each ramp, and dark mode brightens to mid-ramp steps that hold at least 3:1 on the dark container floor.

### Trend (mode-split — the "number went up / went down" role)
- Up: `--color-trend-up` — a deep green-ramp ink in light mode, the ramp's vivid key in dark
- Down: `--color-trend-down` — a deep red-ramp ink in light mode, the ramp's vivid key in dark

Stat deltas and any table mover colour through these, never through the accents directly: on light surfaces the vivid keys fall below readable contrast, so the light half uses the deep status inks, while the dark half keeps the vivid keys, which read well on dark fills. The role exists so that "went up" has one colour everywhere it appears.

### File type (the "what kind of document" role)
- PDF: `--color-file-pdf`, red
- Spreadsheet: `--color-file-spreadsheet`, green (a CSV shares it)
- Document: `--color-file-document`, blue
- Presentation: `--color-file-presentation`, yellow

The type mark on an attachment colours through these and nothing else does. They sit beside the status and trend sets rather than inside them: status says what state something is in, the chart series says which data series it is, and a file colour says what format a document is. One role per well-known format is the whole family; every other file wears a neutral extension badge, so colour stays a signal for the formats a reader recognises at a glance.

This is the one colour family a theme does not own. Each role points at a fixed file-format primitive (`--primitive-file-*`), the hue that format wears everywhere else a person meets it, and it holds the same value in every preset and in both modes, because recognition is the entire job and a re-tinted PDF red would stop being recognised. Two rules keep that safe. The colours only ever fill the mark's silhouette, a graphic beside a file name that already states the extension; and nothing is ever read against them, because the mark's lettering is drawn in a text colour. A consumer who wants house colours overrides the four primitives.

### Core Accents (data-viz, decorative — not for semantic status)
Six named slots: `--color-core-accent-coral`, `--color-core-accent-amber`, `--color-core-accent-gold`, `--color-core-accent-mint`, `--color-core-accent-cobalt`, `--color-core-accent-violet`. The names are slot names, not promises of a hue: every theme declares its own sextet (a monochrome theme fills all six with greys), and the sextet drives chart series 2–7 and EventCalendar's event accents together, and is what the ambient background follows by default.

### Ambient (the background shader's blob colours)
Six numbered slots, `--color-ambient-1` to `--color-ambient-6`, one per coloured blob in the ambient background. In the base wiring each points at a core accent (gold, mint, violet, cobalt, coral, amber, in that order), so the field and the chart palette share their colours. They are separate tokens so a theme can tune the field alone: a preset that declares `ambient` in `THEME_PRESETS` states all six, and the shipped presets that do keep four on their accents and move the coral and amber slots to lighter tones of their key colour, so the field loses its warm patch while the chart series keep the contrast between hues that data needs. The ambient background reads them, and so does BentoTile's shader wash by default, so a tile's wash and the page's field are the same light.

### AI (reserved for AI entry points and surfaces)
The three-stop AI gradient is the system's "a model answers here" signal, the AI counterpart to the action colour: ordinary actions keep the flat `--color-action-primary-bg`, AI affordances carry the gradient, and the gradient is never used for an ordinary action's job — or decoratively. Inside shared list chrome the reservation changes form rather than lapsing: an AI affordance in a uniform list never restyles its own row (a one-off row treatment would break the list before it signalled anything), but it may wear the gradient as a **passive mark** — a non-interactive ringed badge at the row's trailing edge, signalling the destination without touching the row's shape. The site palette's ask-the-chat row is the reference: its label stays the visitor's own query in the list's plain face, and the AI-ring chip rides the CommandPalette `trailing` slot (see that spec for the slot's contract). Always used as gradient stops (AiButton's ring and glow are the reference), never as flat fills for ordinary chrome. One sanctioned departure: the landing hero's rotating subtitle changes its line behind a passing band of the three stops. Nothing there is interactive and no model answers, so it is the one place the gradient marks a subject (the page's own AI claim) rather than an affordance; it is not a precedent for decorating other headings. A second sanctioned departure: the playground's theme-source dropzone, while it waits for a file, passes a band of the three stops across its headline and one sweep of the ring round its edge every six seconds, resting as an ordinary card between passes. It marks the tool's headline feature, exists only until a file is loaded, and has no passes at all under reduced motion; it is not a precedent for decorating other cards.

| Token | Base wiring | Under a preset |
|---|---|---|
| `--color-ai-gradient-start` | `red-05`, both modes | A tonal preset: the action key's ramp, four steps lighter than the key. A fade theme: the key's ramp, two steps toward the page. Otherwise the same step, on the red ramp as the preset re-keys it |
| `--color-ai-gradient-mid` | `blue-05`, both modes | A tonal or fade theme: the action key's own step. Otherwise the same step, on the blue ramp as the preset re-keys it |
| `--color-ai-gradient-end` | `teal-07`, both modes — two steps deeper than the other stops | A tonal preset: the action key's ramp, two steps deeper than the key. A fade theme: the key's ramp, four steps toward the page. Otherwise the same ramp split by mode (step 07 in light and 08 in dark, following where the key sits) |

A **fade** theme is one whose source states no gradient of its own: the playground's imports, from a DESIGN.md or a photo. Its ring is the action colour fading toward the page, lighter tints in light mode and deeper shades in dark, so it dissolves into the surface rather than carrying hues the source never named.
| `--color-ai-button-bg` | `neutral-00` light / `true-black` dark | Unchanged |

A preset chooses between two shapes (`aiGradient` in `THEME_PRESETS`). The base look and any preset that leaves the field unset keep the red and blue stops, which suit a blue or teal key. A **tonal** preset draws all three stops from its action key's ramp, so the ring is the key colour at three depths and carries no hue the theme does not own; a key too near the light end of its ramp hands the missing light steps to the deep side, so the spread holds.

In the base shape, the start and mid stops are bright enough to stay luminous on dark surfaces in both modes, while deeper inks would read as a heavy dark border on a light floor. The end stop follows the action colour, so a preset that moves the action key moves it too, and it sits deeper than the other two so the gradient ends on the same weight the action colour carries.

The family carries one solid fill alongside the stops: `--color-ai-button-bg`, the AI button's field. It is the theme's extreme surface — brighter than the light page floor, deeper than the dark one — so the AI entry point punches out of whatever it floats over rather than frosting into it. Reserved with the rest of the family: it fills AiButton's pill and the passive marks that wear its face (the site palette's ask chip), nothing else.

---

### Pixel ink (mode-split — the "which generated character" role)
- Ink 1 to Ink 6: `--color-pixel-ink-1` → `-6` — one ink per chromatic ramp outside the brand's (red, orange, yellow, green, blue, purple), each a deep step of its ramp in light mode and a high step in dark

A PixelAvatar is drawn in one of these, picked by its name. They are a set of their own rather than the chart series because the job is different: a series colour fills a large mark on a chart and is tuned as a mid-tone that works on both grounds, while a sprite is a few small solid shapes on the page and needs a text-like contrast, so each ink splits by mode. Yellow and green sit a step deeper than their neighbours in light mode, because those ramps stay bright for longer. PromptSuggestions' card covers borrow the same inks for their tint, cycled by position. The brand's own ramp is left out on purpose: a theme re-points it, so an ink there would become a second copy of whichever ink the theme's colour resembles, and two neighbours in a list would match. Nothing else uses them: they identify, they never signal a state.

### Avatar (mode-split — the "whose initials" role)
- Fill 1 to Fill 6: `--color-avatar-bg-1` → `-6`, with Initials 1 to 6: `--color-avatar-text-1` → `-6` — the pixel inks' six ramps in their order, a pale step under a deep one in light mode and the reverse in dark

An initials Avatar takes one pair, picked by its name. The fill is large and the initials are text, so each pair is a fill and an ink together, held to a text contrast in both modes, and a stack of people reads apart rather than as one grey run. The brand's ramp is left out for the pixel inks' reason. They identify, they never signal a state.

## Typography

### Font Family
Every family role resolves to one face, `--font-family-primary`, unless a theme splits them. Which face that is belongs to the theme: the base token files set Nunito Sans, and each preset declares its own body and heading faces. No monospace face is bundled — code contexts (CodeBlock, ToolCall names, Prose inline code) use the system mono stack codified as `--font-family-code`, in every theme.

Whatever face a theme picks needs the weights that theme's tiers ask for. The base theme asks for 300 through 600 and a light weight that still reads at display size; a theme whose face has no light cut sets its display tier heavier instead (Velvet's display serif starts at regular).

**Family roles.** No type-scale step references `--font-family-primary` directly. The scale chains through two role aliases in `tokens-typography.css`, split along the tier boundary the Hierarchy table below draws:

| Role token | Resolves to | Feeds |
|---|---|---|
| `--font-family-heading` | `var(--font-family-primary)` | The display and heading tiers — Mega 1/2, Display 1/2, Sub Display, Heading 1–3 |
| `--font-family-body` | `var(--font-family-primary)` | The body tiers — Title Body, Paragraph Em, Paragraph, Paragraph SM (+ Em), Overline, Caption |

Both roles default to the primary family, which is what keeps the three levers coherent: override `--font-family-primary` and the whole system re-fonts as one face (the base theme's position); override `--font-family-heading` alone and headings take a display face while running text keeps the primary; override both roles and the primary token stops mattering. Title Body sits on the body role deliberately — it is a bold label inside working UI (card titles, table column heads), and a consumer pairing a serif display face with a sans text face wants that label in the text face, not the serif.

**Sourcing.** The base theme's face is deliberately not bundled with the npm package. The website self-hosts it via `next/font/google` (fetched from Google Fonts at build time and served first-party), Storybook loads it with a Google Fonts `<link>`, and package consumers load it however their stack prefers — then swap the whole system to another face by overriding `--font-family-primary`, or split faces through the family roles above. The presets' faces are the opposite case: they ship self-hosted inside the package, declared by each preset's generated stylesheet, so a theme arrives complete with its type. Material Symbols Rounded, by contrast, ships inside the package as a self-hosted woff2, so icons need no external setup.

### Type scale ladders

Every style bundle's `-size` (and pixel `-line-height`) resolves through two shared ladders in `tokens-typography.css`, named by the same rule as the spacing scale (rank = px / 4 x 100): `--font-size-300` (12px) up to `--font-size-3300` (132px), and `--font-line-height-400` (16px) up to `--font-line-height-1100` (44px). This is the type-size unlock: override a ladder step and every tier that reads it rescales together, without touching the tier bundles. The display tiers' unitless leadings (0.92-1.15) are ratios, not lengths, and stay in their bundles.

### Hierarchy

| Token prefix | Size | ≤768px | Weight | Line Height | Letter Spacing | Use |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--font-mega-1-*` | 132px | 64px | 300 | 0.92 | +2% | Marketing hero — never in app UI |
| `--font-mega-2-*` | 116px | 56px | 300 | 0.92 | +2% | Marketing hero — never in app UI |
| `--font-display-1-*` | 96px | 48px | 300 | 1.05 | +2% | Large marketing section heads |
| `--font-display-2-*` | 64px | 40px | 300 | 1.1 (1.15 ≤768px) | +1.5% | Marketing section heads |
| `--font-sub-display-*` | 30px | 24px | 300 | 44px (36px ≤768px) | +1.5% | Sub-section heads on marketing pages |
| `--font-heading-1-*` | 30px | — | 600 | 44px | +1.5% | Page-level h1 in app/docs |
| `--font-heading-2-*` | 26px | — | 600 | 32px | +1.5% | Section headings in app/docs |
| `--font-heading-3-*` | 22px | — | 600 | 28px | +1.5% | Sub-section headings in app/docs |
| `--font-title-body-*` | 16px | — | 600 | 24px | −1% | Card titles, table column heads, bold labels |
| `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` | 16px | — | 500 | 24px | −1% | Emphasized body, button labels, input labels |
| `--font-paragraph-*` | 16px | — | 400 | 24px | 0 | Default running text |
| `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` | 14px | — | 500 | 20px | 0 | Compact button labels, badge text, form helpers (emphasized) |
| `--font-paragraph-sm-*` | 14px | — | 400 | 20px | 0 | Secondary body, helper text |
| `--font-overline-*` | 14px | — | 600 | 20px | +8% | Uppercase labels — eyebrows, map callout titles, code-language chips |
| `--font-caption-*` | 12px | — | 400 | 16px | 0 | Footnotes and disclaimers — the floor of the scale |

The ≤768px column is not a separate token set: the same `--font-*-size` tokens re-resolve inside a single `@media (max-width: 768px)` block at the bottom of `tokens-typography.css`. Anything set in display-tier tokens collapses automatically on mobile; the heading and body tiers never step (marked —). The Size and Weight columns, like everything token-owned, are the base theme's positions — the presets' `typeScale`, tier-weight and `tracking` levers move them per theme.

### Principles
- Mega and Display sizes (Mega 1/2, Display 1/2, Sub Display) are **marketing-only** — they belong on the documentation homepage and landing pages, never inside application UI or component pages. One sanctioned exception: the site chat's welcome ask line is set in `--font-sub-display-*`, because the empty widget is a landing moment, not working UI — the pair reads as a small heading-weight greeting over a large display-weight invitation, the base theme's weight contrast at welcome scale. The exception ends where the conversation starts: nothing in a live thread uses the display tier.
- Headings 1–3 use weight 600 and display styles weight 300 in the base theme, and **never bold a display size within a theme** — a theme sets its tier weights once, and the use site never overrides them. Weight is a per-preset position like size: the `displayWeight` and `headingWeight` levers set their tiers (Forest sets its display tier to 600 to suit its heavier display face) and the `bodyWeight` lever shifts the body tier as a unit, but nothing inside a theme bolds a display token at the use site.
- **Display leading is unitless, and loosens as the size drops**: 0.92 on Mega, 1.05 on Display 1, 1.1 on Display 2 (1.15 on mobile, where the same headline wraps to more lines). Set solid, a display line reads as a poster on one line and closes up on two — and these headlines wrap, because a page title is a sentence. The heading and body tiers state leading in px instead, because at those sizes the line box is a rhythm the whole page snaps to.
- Positive letter-spacing (+1.5–2%) applies to all display and heading sizes. Negative letter-spacing (−1%) applies to title-body and paragraph-em — this tightening is what makes UI labels feel crisp.
- Buttons use `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` (default) and `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` (compact). Weight 500 on interactive controls in the base theme, one step above the paragraph; a theme's body weight moves it with the tier.
- Overline is the uppercase label face, and always pairs with `text-transform: uppercase` at the use site — the tokens set the metrics, never the casing. Its +8% tracking is the one positive letter-spacing outside the display and heading tiers, because capitals at label size need air to stay legible. A component may keep the metrics and swap the family (MapCallout sets its title in the code face).
- Caption (12px) is the floor, and it is for text the reader can find but never has to read to use the screen: the site chat's disclaimer line, a footnote under a chart. Never on a control, never for helper text (that is Paragraph SM), never for running copy.

---

## Page Layout & Heading Hierarchy

This section documents how to apply the typographic scale when building new pages in the documentation website. Following these rules ensures every page reads with the same visual hierarchy.

### Heading Roles

| Level | Token | Size | Weight | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Page title (`h1`) | `--font-display-2-*` | 64px | 300 | Route title — rendered once per page at the top, outside the markdown body |
| Major section (`h2`) | `--font-sub-display-*` | 30px | 300 | Top-level content sections (e.g. Colors, Typography) |
| Sub-section (`h3`) | `--font-heading-3-*` | 22px | 600 | Named groupings within a section (e.g. Token Tiers, Action / Brand) |
| Minor heading (`h4`) | `--font-title-body-*` | 16px | 600 | Inline labels or additional sub-groups |

### Weight Contrast

**The base theme separates the major → minor transition by weight contrast, not size alone. That is the base theme's choice, not a rule of the system.**

- `h2` sits on the display tier and `h3` on the heading tier. In the base theme that is 30px / 300 (light) over 22px / 600 (bold): the weight jump makes the hierarchy unmistakable at a glance.
- Tier weights belong to the theme, like the faces do. A theme may move them, invert them (Zest sets its display tier heavier than its headings), or set both tiers to one weight. A theme that closes the gap leans on size alone, so it should check that a heading still reads as a sub-item of the one above it.
- Within a tier, levels step by size and share a weight: `h1` and `h2` are both display-tier, `h3` and `h4` both heading-weight. A theme sets a weight per tier, never per page. A preset may specialise a single style through its `extraOverrides` (Forest and Zest step Sub Display down a notch from their display tier); nothing else departs from the tier. The body tier moves as a unit rather than to one weight: a theme sets the paragraph's weight, and emphasis, overline and caption shift by the same amount, so emphasis stays a step above the paragraph whatever the theme's body weight is.

### Section Dividers

`h2` elements carry a `border-bottom: var(--border-025) solid var(--color-divider)` by default to visually close the preceding section. Do not insert `<hr>` elements between sections — they duplicate the divider. The border on `h2` is the only section separator needed.

**The header-divider rhythm is fixed**: heading, then `--padding-500` (20px) of clearance, then the rule, then `--gap-1000` (40px) before the section's content. The rule belongs to the heading, so the tight side is above it and the generous side is below — inverted spacing (a rule floating far under its heading, or content hugging the rule) reads as the rule labelling the content instead. SectionTitle owns the heading-to-rule half; the page's section layout owns the 40px below, usually as the section container's gap. A page whose sections run a tighter internal gap must make up the difference on the divider itself (margin-bottom on the title — /overview and /docs/get-started are the precedent) rather than letting the rule sit closer to the content than everywhere else. The hand-rolled equivalents (the case-study and journal `resumeSectionHeader`-style headers) follow the same geometry. The one deliberate exception is markdown article bodies (the /blueprints renderers — see Applying to Markdown Pages below): their h2s head running article copy, not page sections, so they run a tighter internal rhythm (8px above the rule, 16px below) that would read as page-section spacing nowhere else.

### Applying to Markdown Pages

When a page renders markdown (via `react-markdown` or similar), apply these heading styles using `:global()` selectors scoped to the markdown body container:

```css
/* h2 — major section, light and airy */
.markdownBody :global(h2) {
  font-size: var(--font-sub-display-size);      /* 30px */
  font-weight: var(--font-sub-display-weight);  /* 300 */
  padding-bottom: var(--primitive-padding-200);  /* 8px above the rule */
  border-bottom: var(--border-025) solid var(--color-divider);
  margin-top: var(--gap-1500);                   /* 60px above, 40px on mobile — semantic, so the section rhythm collapses with the token */
  margin-bottom: var(--primitive-gap-400);       /* 16px below */
}

/* h3 — sub-section, bold contrast */
.markdownBody :global(h3) {
  font-size: var(--font-heading-3-size);        /* 22px */
  font-weight: var(--font-heading-3-weight);    /* 600 */
  margin-top: var(--primitive-gap-1000);          /* 40px above */
  margin-bottom: var(--primitive-gap-200);       /* 8px below */
}

/* h4 — minor heading, same weight as h3, smaller size */
.markdownBody :global(h4) {
  font-size: var(--font-title-body-size);       /* 16px */
  font-weight: var(--font-title-body-weight);   /* 600 */
  margin-top: var(--primitive-gap-400);
  margin-bottom: var(--primitive-gap-100);
}
```

### Responsive Collapse

The display tier collapses **in the token layer**, once, at 768px (see the Hierarchy table): page titles (`--font-display-2-*`) go 64px → 40px and section heads (`--font-sub-display-*`) go 30px → 24px with no per-page CSS. Pages that reference the tokens are responsive by default — never re-hardcode a mobile font size in a page module. One sanctioned exception, marked with a `ds-allow(typography)` directive in the package: the chat widget's welcome ask line repeats the sub-display step as a *container* query on the panel's own width (`src/chat/ChatWidget.css`), because the docked panel is phone-width on desktop windows the viewport media query calls large — the panel wears the phone scale below a 768px container, and only the fullscreen takeover on a wide window keeps the 30px size. It restates the same 24px/36px pair, and the two must move together.

- Headings 1–3 and the body tiers do **not** step; 22–30px headings stay readable on phones.
- Weights do not change at any breakpoint.
- The one sanctioned exception: a mega-1 hero (home page, 404) may keep a hand-tuned two-stage ramp (132 → 64 → 40) in its own module, commented as a deliberate override.

---

## Spacing & Layout

### Base Units
- **Gap (flex/grid spacing):** 4px-based scale via `--gap-*` tokens.
- **Padding (internal element padding):** 2px-based micro scale via `--padding-*` tokens.
- **Borders:** 1px hairline (`--border-025`) and 2px emphasis (`--border-050`).

### Gap Scale
| Token | Value | Typical Use |
|---|---|---|
| `--gap-050` | 2px | Tightest inline spacing (icon + label micro-gap) |
| `--gap-100` | 4px | Compact component internals |
| `--gap-200` | 8px | Standard icon-to-label gap, button icon spacing |
| `--gap-300` | 12px | Form field stack spacing |
| `--gap-400` | 16px | Card internal element spacing |
| `--gap-500` | 20px | Section element spacing |
| `--gap-1000` | 40px | Card-to-card spacing, major internal gaps |
| `--gap-1500` | 60px (40px ≤768px) | Between component groups |
| `--gap-2000` | 80px (60px ≤768px) | Between major page sections |
| `--gap-3000` | 120px (80px ≤768px) | Page-level section breaks |

The three section-rhythm steps (`xxl`–`xxxxl`) compress one notch down the primitive scale below 768px, in a `@media` block at the bottom of `tokens-light.css` — the same single token-layer breakpoint typography uses. Steps `xl` and below never change.

### Padding Scale
| Token | Value | Typical Use |
|---|---|---|
| `--padding-050` | 2px | Badge vertical padding |
| `--padding-100` | 4px | Micro insets |
| `--padding-150` | 6px | Compact button/input vertical |
| `--padding-200` | 8px | Standard button vertical, badge horizontal |
| `--padding-300` | 12px | Compact button horizontal, compact input horizontal |
| `--padding-400` | 16px | Standard input horizontal, card inner padding baseline |
| `--padding-500` | 20px | Standard button horizontal |
| `--padding-1000` | 40px | Large card insets |
| `--padding-1500` | 60px (40px ≤768px) | Section-level insets |

### Layout Philosophy
Whitespace communicates hierarchy. Dense elements use micro-gaps (2–8px); comfortable reading areas use md/lg gaps (16–20px); major layout regions use xl–xxxxl (40–120px). The system relies on spacing contrast rather than dividers — overuse of `--color-divider` is a design smell.

---

## Composition

Tokens constrain values; this section constrains choices. Element-level rules (colors, radii, type) make components consistent — these rules make **pages** consistent. Every rule here is stated so that a page assembled from the components lands right on the first pass, without per-page taste decisions.

### The rhythm ladder

Page-level vertical space is a statement of relatedness: the gap between two things says how related they are. The ladder is exhaustive — these five roles are the *only* page-level gaps. A spacing value that is not on the ladder is component-internal spacing, and belongs to a component.

| Role | Token | Value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| Section break | `--gap-2000` | 80px (60px ≤768px) | Separates page regions. The only gap between sections. |
| Heading bind | `--gap-1000` | 40px, flat | Ties a heading to the content it introduces. |
| Intro bind | `--gap-500` | 20px | Ties a tagline or lede to its body copy. |
| Group bind | `--gap-200` | 8px | Ties a label to the items it names. |
| Column gutters | `--layout-*` | presets | Gutters and rail widths are facts of the layout, set once at the shell — never per-page choices. |

Because the ladder's meanings differ by a full visual step (80 / 40 / 20 / 8), a reader can recover the page structure from spacing alone. Never use an off-ladder gap to "fine-tune" a page-level relationship: if 40px feels wrong between a heading and its content, the content is wrong, not the gap.

### The dashboard rung set

Rule 7 below says tiling is a template's decision; a dashboard is that template. When a page is an app shell laying Panels in a grid, the ladder gains one inner rung set, worked out on the labs marketing rebuild and stated here so every dashboard lands on the same density:

| Role | Token | Value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| Card gutter | `--gap-500` | 20px | Between Panels, in every direction. |
| Card inset | `--padding-500` | 20px | A Panel's own padding — the gutter and the inset match, so the grid reads as one fabric. |
| Region gap | `--gap-400` | 16px | Between a Panel's regions: header to chart, chart to legend row. |
| Cluster gap | `--gap-200` / `--gap-300` | 8 / 12px | Within a cluster: icon to text, tiles in a legend row, buttons in a toolbar. |

The recurring mistake this prevents: reaching for the 2px and 4px tokens (`--gap-050`, `--gap-100`) for cluster spacing — both spacing audits on the labs rebuild found the same error, and at cluster scale those values read as touching, not spaced.

### Composition rules

1. **Parent owns spacing.** Siblings are spaced by their container's `gap`, never by margins on the children. A child that carries its own outer margin breaks in every context except the one it was tuned for.
2. **One level of chrome.** Components that carry their own container (charts, tables, calendars) sit bare under a heading — never wrapped in a card. A card wrapper is only for content with no chrome of its own. Two levels of container on one element is always a bug — and so is the over-correction: bare means without a wrapper, never with the component's own borders and background stripped to fake flatness. The component keeps exactly the chrome it ships with.
3. **Dividers are the last resort.** Separation comes from whitespace (the ladder) and containers first; a divider is only for where spacing contrast cannot do the job — long uniform lists, table rows. A divider under a heading that whitespace already separates is decoration, and decoration is not a divider's job. Next to content that draws its own lines (bordered tables, calendars), a section heading always separates by whitespace alone — on a line-heavy page, every added line is noise.
4. **Constrain the column, not the content.** A page has one content max-width; anything wider than its column (tables, diagrams, code) scrolls inside its own container. Never cap the width of a paragraph — cap the column it sits in.
5. **A header is one region.** A page title and its tagline bind at the heading tier (40px), never separated by a section break. The first section break on a page sits *below* the complete header group.
6. **Page rhythm stops at a component's edge.** The ladder governs the space *between* components and headings; a component's internal spacing is its own spec's business. Composing a page never involves reaching into a component to adjust its insides.
7. **Sections stack.** A page is one column of full-width sections; side-by-side placement is for the items *inside* a band (a metrics row of Stats), not for sections themselves. Tiling sections into a grid is a template's decision to make, never a page-by-page one — tested both ways on a dense dashboard, the stack reads calmer and the grid reads cramped.

### Template screens

A template rebuilds a whole product screen from the system alone, and the family has conventions beyond the page rules above — worked out across the marketing dashboard, sales pipeline, roadmap planner, and relay console rounds of review, and stated here so the next screen lands right without re-learning them:

**The product mark is the generic disc.** Every template's fictional product wears the same mark beside its name: `ProductMark` (`website/src/components/templates/ProductMark/`), a `--radius-pill` disc at `--icon-size-600` in `--color-core-ui-primary`, so it follows the theme's accent through every preset without borrowing the action colour, which stays reserved for controls. No template draws a bespoke logo for its own product, and the site's own brand mark never stands in for a product's (the mobile dashboard's is a link back to the templates index, not the product's logo). Marks a screen shows as content are the exception, in three kinds. A real publisher or identity provider a screen names as data gets its brand-colour mark through the shared `PublisherMarks` table (the marketing dashboard's channel column, the sign-in template's provider buttons). Fictional companies a marketing screen names as customers get small invented glyphs drawn in `currentColor` (the landing page's customer strip), so the row reads as several brands in one quiet tone and never as a real company's mark. Language flags keep their own fixed colours, the file-format colours' reasoning: recognition is their whole job (the landing page's language menu).

1. **A reference product is direction, never a blueprint.** A template may start from another product's screen, but it is composed from this system's idioms, and the standard for every choice is the rendered siblings, not the reference. Furniture the reference has and the family lacks — a status badge beside the title, an avatar cluster in the header, drawn imitation browser chrome, a bespoke rollup strip — does not come along: a piece with no sibling precedent is left out by default, not invented.
2. **One job per control species, one size per row.** A data view's control bar is the library's `TableToolbar` in the DataTable's `toolbar` slot (the sales pipeline and the marketing dashboard's campaigns), so its search, filter chips, sort, More panel and bulk bar are the table's own, and a filter the screen opens on (the pipeline's last 90 days of activity) is a default filter the reader can change, never a hand-built select beside the table. Never a mix of segmented controls, filter chips, and inputs doing the same job in one row. A *stage toolbar* — the strip floating over an immersive stage, the relay console's precedent — may pair one view-flip `SegmentedControl` with the filter cluster, at the default size with wider selects, because it is read at arm's length over a map rather than at table density; the filters themselves stay one species. What survives every toolbar: controls sharing a row share one size, and no job is done by two species in the same row.
3. **The stage earns the viewport.** The screen's point — the table, the board, the chart — takes the room. A KPI band is a choice, not template furniture: both the sales pipeline and the roadmap planner shipped without one, because the stage deserved the screen. When metric tiles do appear, they follow the dashboard rung set above.
4. **People are initials.** Demo humans render through the Avatar's initials fallback — never generated portrait imagery or photographs. The fiction stays legible as fiction, and the rows stay quiet.
5. **The content column caps at 1680px — unless the stage is the content, or the screen is a phone (rule 6).** The family's shell centres a 1680px column inside the viewport-tall shell. A screen whose stage *is* the content — a map, a projection instrument — may run the full viewport width instead (the relay console's call); it is a stage decision, made once per screen, never a per-section one.
6. **A phone-only screen is the phone and nothing else.** At a phone's width the app is the page, edge to edge; from 600px it is drawn as a framed device on the dotted stage, with the status bar and home indicator as `aria-hidden` set dressing overlaying the app, and the platform chrome (TopAppBar, BottomNav) riding the app area rather than the viewport so the frame carries it. The way back to /templates and the theme switchers sit on the stage, moving into the menu drawer at phone width. Its filter is one full-width `SegmentedControl` per view, the phone form of rule 2, and its sidebar entry sets `mobileOnly` so the templates carousel previews it in the phone frame at every device size. The mobile dashboard is the precedent (its entry also sets `hideFromShowcase`, which keeps a template out of the carousel while its page, sidebar entry and sitemap row stay).
8. **In a Mobile preview, an app shell previews as an app.** The templates index draws every preview in a phone bezel at Mobile, and a template with a sidebar shell swaps its web chrome for the mobile dashboard's there: an iOS large-title `TopAppBar` in place of the top bar and page title, and a glass `BottomNav` built from the sidebar's first destinations, the assistant on its search circle. The templates carousel's Mobile mode and the playground's Mobile stage both ask for it with a `data-app-frame` attribute on the preview frame's root (a Desktop stage dragged narrow never sets it: a narrow window is not a phone), and the shared `AppFrame` pieces in `website/src/components/templates/AppFrame/` do the dressing, so the template's own page is untouched at every width. A screen with no tab-bar shape on a real phone (a chat session, a sign-in) keeps its own narrow layout inside the bezel. A screen with no sidebar may read the same flag for phone chrome of its own: the landing page shows its bottom tab bar for a Mobile preview and on a real touch phone, never for a narrow desktop window.
7. **The assistant follows the site chat's responsive modes.** A template's chat is the shared `TemplateAssistant` (or, on a screen that is itself a chat surface, a restatement of the launcher's contract over the package's `ChatWidget`, as the chat home does), and it behaves as the site panel does: docked at `DOCK_QUERY` with the shell's main column reserving the width, a modal overlay over a scrim below that, a full-viewport takeover at phone widths. The component owns the modes; the host's only job is gating its `.mainChatOpen` padding on `@media (min-width: 1440px)`, because a width reservation below the dock threshold squeezes the stage into a sliver. A phone-framed screen passes `placement="contained"` and places the panel inside its frame. The ChatWidget spec owns the rule for every chat surface, not only templates. A screen where the chat is the page (the chat home) is the one shape outside this: it hosts the real `ChatWidget` as the whole viewport, with no close and no full-screen toggle, in the takeover form at every width; the ChatWidget spec records the exception.


### Blocks

A block is a whole starting page a consumer copies into their app with `npx rift-ds init block <name>` and owns from then on, so it never updates under them: the template family's idea cut to what a new app needs on day one. It is source, never a component: no prop is published, so nothing joins the API compatibility gate, and a piece that proves itself across blocks is promoted into the library rather than grown in place. The files live at their target paths under `src/cli/templates/blocks/`, listed in that folder's `registry.json`, and the site renders each one at `/docs/blocks/<name>` from the same file, so the preview and the shipped page cannot differ. `scripts/validate-blocks.mjs` holds the registry, the file tree, the imports between the files and the site's previews to one another.

1. **Composed from the library alone, styled with tokens alone.** A block's CSS modules name semantic tokens for every colour, gap, padding, radius and type style; the only literals are a component's own geometry the block must clear (AppSidebar's rail widths, its mobile menu button) and a content width, each said in a comment.
2. **Responsive at every width.** Every block is laid out for a desktop, a laptop, a tablet and a phone, on the library's breakpoints: the app shell's sidebar floats beside the page above the mobile step and becomes AppSidebar's drawer below it, the top bar's search folds to its icon, the gutter tightens, and grids step down to one column. A block that only holds at one width is not finished.
3. **One shell, shared.** The app shell (`components/blocks/AppShell.tsx`) is the floating sidebar, the top bar (CommandPaletteTrigger, AiButton, ThemeToggle, every control compact so the row holds one height) and the page header (Breadcrumb, an h1 at Heading 1, compact actions), around a content column capped at 1440px. Blocks that need it list it, and `init block` never overwrites, so the first writes it and the rest reuse it.
4. **Placeholder content reads as finished.** The product is "Acme", the people are plain names, the numbers are plausible, and every comment tells the consumer what to replace, so a block looks right the moment it lands and is obvious to make their own.

**Adding a block** touches five places, and the build fails on any one missed: the files at their target paths under `src/cli/templates/blocks/` with an entry in its `registry.json` (`validate-blocks.mjs`), the block's page in `BLOCK_PREVIEWS` (`website/src/data/block-previews.tsx`), a preview route folder under `website/src/app/docs/blocks/<name>/` copied from a sibling, that route in `CHROMELESS_ROUTES`, and a reason in the corpus generator's `EXCLUDED_ROUTES` (`validate-chat-coverage.mjs`). The index page, the bin's list and the consumer smoke read the registry and need nothing.
---

## Shapes

### Border Radius Scale
**Concentric containers.** A container wrapping a radiused element derives its own radius as the inner radius plus the inset between them, never an independent value — parallel curves are what make the nesting read deliberate, and a theme that squares the pill token squares both at once. The Composer's shell is the founding case (its spec section owns the derivation); SegmentedControl's track (`calc(var(--radius-pill) + var(--padding-100) + var(--border-025))`) follows it. At the base theme's 999px pill the sum saturates and nothing changes.

**A row of controls shares one curve, and the deepest nesting sets it.** Concentricity answers what a container's corner should be when something sits inside it; it says nothing about the control next to it, and the two can disagree. The site header's right slot is where that showed: the search pill, the theme switcher's trigger and the light/dark toggle are all 40px tall and wear the same fill and hairline border, but each derived its corner from its own contents, so a theme that squared the pill token gave the row three curves — 14px, 10px and 6px in Mono. The toggle's track is the constrained one, because its segments genuinely nest inside it, so the row matches the track: every control in it takes `calc(var(--radius-pill) + var(--padding-100))` (CommandPaletteTrigger and ThemePicker's trigger both wear it), and the theme picker's trigger does not derive from the swatch it carries. A chip inset 11px inside a 40px button is a mark, not a nested track, and reading a curve off it bowed the trigger rounder than its neighbour in every squared theme. The row shares a height for the same reason: `--control-height-default`, the height every row control takes (see the Control Height Scale), which SegmentedControl's track reaches by shrinking its segments inside it rather than padding them.

| Token | Value | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `--radius-050` | 2px | Reserved — rare micro elements |
| `--radius-100` | 4px | Badges (`ds-badge`) |
| `--radius-200` | 8px | Small sub-elements, inner nested surfaces |
| `--radius-300` | 12px | Inputs, cards (standard), modals |
| `--radius-400` | 16px | Large feature cards, hero containers |
| `--radius-600` | 24px | Card/EntityCard navigation tiles, Panel, chat bubbles and chat card furniture (MessageCard, ToolCall, AgentPlan, InterruptCard), AppSidebar's floating variant, CommandPalette's panel, oversized hero containers, page-level sections |
| `--radius-1200` | 48px | Pill containers, oversized decorative elements |
| `--radius-pill` | 999px | All buttons, every variant, toggle thumbs |

The Value column is the base theme's scale; the radius lever rescales every step per theme, and its pill switch can square `--radius-pill` down to the input's corner (the scaled `--radius-300`), so in a squared theme a button and the field beside it share one corner.

**Key rule:** Buttons are always `--radius-pill`. Inputs are always `--radius-300`, with one sanctioned departure: the Composer shell holds its own component-local `--ds-composer-radius` for concentric geometry with its send button (see the Composer spec). Card and EntityCard — the navigational tiles — use the larger `--radius-600` to read as destinations rather than form surfaces, and the chat's card furniture (MessageCard, ToolCall, AgentPlan, InterruptCard) shares that shell with the bubbles around it, so a conversation's surfaces read as one family; a choice offered as a row (SelectionCard, QuestionCard) is a form control and keeps the input radius, and a row inside an overlay takes `--radius-200`, the inner step of its panel. The assignment is what is fixed, not the curve: in the base theme it reads as pill CTAs vs softer-cornered inputs vs generously rounded tiles, and a squared theme keeps the same ordering at tighter values.

### Icon Size Scale

| Token | Value | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `--icon-size-500` | 20px | Compact controls, inline affordances, chips, list chevrons |
| `--icon-size-600` | 24px | Default — the size an icon is unless told otherwise |
| `--icon-size-800` | 32px | Feature icons — EntityCard, section headers |
| `--icon-size-1200` | 48px | Marketing and empty-state illustration icons |

### Control Height Scale

Every control that can share a row takes one height at each size, border included, so a search field, a picker, a button, a circular button and a segmented control set side by side line up exactly, with no per-seat patch.

| Token | Value | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `--control-height-default` | 40px | Default controls: fields, pickers, Button, CircularButton, AiButton, SegmentedControl's track, ToggleGroup, FilterBar's chips |
| `--control-height-compact` | 32px | The same controls at `size="compact"` |

A control sets the token as its height with `box-sizing: border-box` and keeps only its horizontal padding, so a bordered control (a field, the secondary Button) and a borderless one (the primary Button) measure the same. SegmentedControl keeps its 4px track padding and sizes its segments to fit inside the height. Combobox takes the token as a minimum, with its border taken out of the vertical padding, because selected chips may wrap it onto a second line. A tag (Chip) and a keyboard hint (Kbd) are content, not row controls, and keep their own heights. A control of your own that sits in a row takes the same token.

**The icon tile pattern** — an icon on a filled circle, for KPI cards and table cells: a `--radius-pill` disc in `--color-bg-container-tertiary` holding a `--icon-size-500` glyph in `--color-text-primary`, at 40px (stat and KPI headers) or 32px (table cells and list rows). It is a page pattern, not a component: four declarations in module CSS, worked out on the labs marketing rebuild.

Components set **`--icon-size`**, never `font-size`:

```css
.ds-thing__icon { --icon-size: var(--icon-size-500); }
```

**Node-accepting icon props** — every `icon`-shaped prop in the library (item-level entries like a tab's or a menu row's included) is typed `string | React.ReactNode`: a string renders as a Material Symbol glyph, and any other node renders as-is inside the same wrapper span, minus the `material-symbols-rounded` class. That contract is the bring-your-own-icons paved path — the repo imports no icon set, and a consumer passes Lucide (or any other) elements directly; a custom SVG should draw with `currentColor` so it inherits text colour the way the bundled glyphs do. Props with a string default (TreeView's `folder`/`draft`, Toast's variant glyphs, ThreadPanel's `edit_square`) keep resolving those defaults when the prop is omitted. New components must ship icon props on this contract, never string-only.

**Icon style hooks** — the bundled woff2 is the full Material Symbols variable font, and `src/fonts/material-symbols.css` exposes every Google axis as a consumed custom property, settable at any scope: `--material-symbols-fill` (0 line / 1 filled), `--material-symbols-weight` (100–700 stroke), `--material-symbols-grade` (−50–200 contrast). `opsz` is deliberately absent from `font-variation-settings` so `font-optical-sizing: auto` feeds the rendered size into the axis (an explicit value would pin it). Three shipped applications of the hooks: `[data-theme="dark"]` sets grade −25 so light-on-dark glyphs do not bloom, `.icon-filled` is the one-icon shorthand for the fill axis, and a theme preset may set the weight and fill for the whole look through its `iconWeight` and `iconFill` levers. These are consumer-override hooks with fallbacks, not registry tokens — the fallback values in that file are their defaults.

`.material-symbols-rounded` reads that one property for `font-size`, `width`, and `height` together, so the glyph and its layout box can never disagree. It *consumes* the variable and never declares it — which is what keeps a component rule from colliding with the icon font's own styles. Setting `font-size` directly on an icon is a bug: it changes the glyph without changing the box.

**The scale starts at 20px, and that floor is not arbitrary.** Material Symbols is a variable font whose `opsz` (optical size) axis ranges 20–48. Optical sizing thickens strokes as an icon gets smaller; below 20 the axis clamps, so a 14px icon would be drawn with 20px-tuned strokes and scaled down, reading thin and fragile. Every step therefore sits inside the axis range, and `font-optical-sizing: auto` lets the browser track the step automatically — `opsz` is deliberately omitted from `font-variation-settings`, because an explicit axis value there would override the automatic behaviour.

**Documented exceptions.** Glyphs that live *inside* a control's geometry rather than acting as icons stay off the scale: the check inside SwitchIndicator's 20×20 thumb (14px, 12px compact), which ToggleSwitch, InspectorToggleSwitch and SelectionCard's toggle mode all draw, and the miniature component mock-ups in the website's component index cards. Each is commented in place.

**Dark mode** applies `GRAD: -25` to counter the optical bloom of light glyphs on dark surfaces — it thins strokes without changing glyph width, which is what `wght` would do.

---

## Elevation & Depth

The system uses **color-block first, shadow rare** philosophy. Depth is communicated through contrast between the floor and the container steps, not box shadows. Each step only has to separate from the one it sits on; whether it reads lighter or deeper is the theme's call.

| Level | Treatment | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Page floor | `--color-bg-page-primary` — no border, no shadow | Body background, app root |
| Container primary | `--color-bg-container-primary` — no border | Sidebars, card fills, section bands |
| Container secondary | `--color-bg-container-secondary` — optional `--color-bg-container-border` border | Nested cards, inner panels |
| Container tertiary | `--color-bg-container-tertiary` — no shadow | Pressed/active states, deepest UI surfaces |
| Input | `--color-input-bg-primary` with `--color-input-border-primary` hairline | Text inputs, textareas, dropdowns |
| Status containers | Colored bg + colored border per variant | Alerts, toasts, badges |

Standard containers never carry shadows. The only shadows in the system are three semantic elevation tokens, defined per mode (stronger opacity in dark mode so they read against the near-black floor) and per theme: the elevation lever can deepen them, soften them, or take them to `none`, in which case the hairline borders carry the depth alone. The values below are the base theme's:

- **`--shadow-floating`** (`0 4px 16px rgba(0,0,0,0.12)` light / `0.55` dark) — anchored floating surfaces: menus, popovers, pickers, floating panels, chart tooltips, toasts. Any component that floats over the page without a scrim takes this token; grep `--shadow-floating` for the current set rather than trusting a list here.
- **`--shadow-modal`** (`0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.2)` light / `0.6` dark) — modal surfaces: Dialog, AlertDialog, and Drawer panels and Lightbox's media, paired with the `--color-scrim` backdrop.
- **`--shadow-table-pinned`** (`0 0 12px rgba(0,0,0,0.16)` light / `0.6` dark) — the edge of a pinned DataTable column while content scrolls under it, cast by the whole column and clipped to the strip past its inner edge, so it falls on the cells passing beneath and never on the pinned column. The pinned edge always carries a hairline; the shadow only says something is under it.

Never write a literal *elevation* shadow in component CSS — use one of these tokens or no shadow at all.
