Hairline inset rings and focus halos built with `box-shadow` are not elevation and are permitted: Swatch and ColorPicker use inset rings to keep pale swatches legible, and Slider's thumb carries a `box-shadow` focus halo. (Two documented elevation exceptions: the interactive Card hover lift — see Do's and Don'ts — and the iOS glass of BottomNav and TopAppBar, which restates the platform's own shadow under the `platform` directive category; the BottomNav spec owns it.)

### Stacking

Depth on the page has a second axis: which surface sits over which. Every surface that rises out of its component into the page's layers takes a `--layer-*` token, declared lowest to highest in `tokens-light.css` with what sits on each layer beside it, and rendered on `/foundations/layout`. From the bottom: `--layer-dropdown` (menus and panels anchored to a control) and `--layer-sticky` (bars pinned while the page scrolls) share a step, since the two never compete for the same space; `--layer-floating` (tooltips, hover cards, context menus); the mobile navigation's `--layer-nav`, `--layer-nav-scrim` and `--layer-nav-rail`; `--layer-modal` (dialogs, drawers and viewers), level with the app rail it covers by DOM order; `--layer-command` for the command palette, which can open over a modal; `--layer-toast`, above every modal, because feedback has to be seen; then the chat launcher's `--layer-chat-launcher`, `--layer-chat-scrim` and `--layer-chat-panel`. A surface that must sit exactly one step off a layer (AppSidebar's panel under its rail, the dock grip over the chat panel) writes `calc()` off the token rather than a new number.

Order inside a single component (a label over a sliding pill, a handle over its track) stays a small local integer under 10. A larger local number takes a `ds-allow(stacking)` directive saying why; `scripts/validate-stacking.mjs` fails the build on anything else. The site adds `--layer-site-*` layers above the library's in its own stylesheet, for its header, inspect mode, and the overlays that must cover the chat panel.

---

## Motion

Motion is quiet and functional — it confirms an interaction, reveals structure, or signals loading. Interface motion never decorates. The one sanctioned exception is the **ambient background**, which is scenery rather than interface: it belongs to no control, communicates no state, and sits behind everything at `z-index: -1` (see the Ambient background section below). The whole vocabulary is defined in `tokens-motion.css` (theme-agnostic, single `:root` like typography): ten `--motion-duration-*` tokens and five `--motion-ease-*` curves. Compose a duration with an easing instead of writing literal values — never hardcode `0.2s ease` in component CSS.

**Durations — core scale** (day-to-day UI):

| Token | Value | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `--motion-duration-fast` | 150ms | Quick feedback: hovers, icons, tooltips, nav links |
| `--motion-duration-base` | 200ms | The default — color/opacity/border transitions |
| `--motion-duration-slow` | 300ms | Structural change: accordion, sidebar width, toast enter |
| `--motion-duration-slower` | 600ms | Deliberate page-entrance reveals |

**Durations — extended** (special-purpose; named so they stop being magic numbers, but reach for the core four first): `--motion-duration-instant` (75ms, below the threshold where a change reads as motion — it takes the edge off the Composer growing a line, nothing more), `--motion-duration-deliberate` (400ms, carousel slide), `--motion-duration-orbit` (120000ms, one full turn of an auto-rotating surface — Globe reads it from its computed style and divides the shipped value, mirrored as `MOTION_ORBIT_REFERENCE_MS` in `src/tokens/motion.ts` and build-held to the token, by it to pace `autoRotate`, so unlike the loop periods it is a tempo, and the playground's Motion lever scales it with the schedule durations), `--motion-duration-loop-spin` (1000ms, spinner rotation), `--motion-duration-loop-shimmer` (1800ms, skeleton shimmer and the agent label sweep), `--motion-duration-loop-matrix` (1400ms, the twelve-slot cycle every AgentStatus dot pattern shares).

**Easings:**

| Token | Value | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `--motion-ease-standard` | `ease` | General default for color/opacity transitions |
| `--motion-ease-emphasized` | `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)` | Size/layout changes (sidebar width, accordion height) |
| `--motion-ease-entrance` | `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` | Expressive decelerate for enter animations (modals, dropdowns, toasts) |
| `--motion-ease-linear` | `linear` | Continuous motion (spinner, progress bars) |
| `--motion-ease-spring` | `cubic-bezier(0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1)` | Playful overshoot (toggle switch thumb) |

**Reduced motion contract:** `tokens-motion.css` collapses every duration token to 0.01ms under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, and a universal guard flattens remaining hardcoded transitions/animations. Components that consume the tokens respect the preference automatically, so a component writes its own `prefers-reduced-motion` query for exactly two jobs: parking a looping animation, which the collapsed duration would strobe rather than stop (an infinite keyframe loop at 0.01ms still runs), and dropping an entrance outright. Each such query carries a `ds-allow(motion)` comment directly above it naming which job it does, and `scripts/validate-raw-values.mjs` fails the build on one without.

**Migration status:** all component and website CSS composes the tokens — a literal duration or easing survives only where a `ds-allow(motion)` directive sanctions it in place (Skeleton's shimmer curve, the ambient AI glows, the decorative background floats; grep `ds-allow(motion)` for the current set — a count here would only rot). JS-driven timings (hover show/hide delays and the hover exit grace, toast auto-dismiss and the exit-animation unmount sync, carousel autoplay, transient feedback resets, scroll settle, the streaming reveal's rate floor and drain window, the summary panel's think beat and reveal budget…) have their own single home: the shared constants in `src/tokens/motion.ts`, published as `rift-ds/tokens/motion` — the module is the authoritative list. Most are schedule timings rather than animation durations, which the reduced-motion guard deliberately does not touch (the summary panel's think beat is one: it still stages under the preference, because it schedules a swap rather than pacing motion); components use them as defaults, overridable per instance through props. A component that animates from a JavaScript timer or frame loop checks the preference itself, since the CSS guard cannot see JS — or rides a library default that does: the recharts-backed charts animate their marks in under recharts' own `isAnimationActive: 'auto'` guard, which the chart-set spec owns (a literal `true` would override it). A grep for `prefers-reduced-motion` in `src/components` is therefore the inventory of *self-checking* components only (Carousel never starts autoplay under it, and StreamingText skips its reveal so each chunk appears whole; they are examples, not the set) — the delegated recharts set never contains the string.

### Ambient background

The website's background is **ShaderField** — the published component, not a bespoke site surface. Its rendering contract is specified in the Components chapter; what follows is only why an animated ambient surface is allowed at all, and how the site composes it: a document-top absolute band (540px standard, 900px under the home page's extended marker, the full viewport only under the full-bleed one) that scrolls away with the page opening rather than following the viewport.

Three properties make this an exception that does not erode the rule:

- **It is scenery, not interface.** Nothing about it is a signal, so its motion cannot be mistaken for feedback.
- **It degrades to the CSS blobs.** The site keeps eight blurred CSS discs painted underneath on every page — ported 1:1 to the shader's coordinate space, so the field and its fallback are the same picture, give or take the framing `crop` gives the field on a narrow viewport — and drives their visibility from the status ShaderField reports. No WebGL2, a lost GPU context, a renderer that stalls before its first frame, or the config's kill switch all land on the same quiet fallback. The blobs are hidden while the renderer is still resolving, rather than painted and then swapped. The deliberate cost is that a machine which cannot run the shader shows bare page floor for that first moment before the blobs fade in.
- **It honours reduced motion in JavaScript.** This is the part worth knowing, and it is the component's job rather than the site's: the guard in `tokens-motion.css` is CSS-only and cannot see a `requestAnimationFrame` loop. The limits on component-level `prefers-reduced-motion` queries still hold for everything that consumes the duration tokens; a JS animation loop is outside that contract by construction.

Each blob carries a 14–22s drift period inherited from the CSS blobs it replaced, scaled by the config's `speed` parameter — so the cycles you actually see are those periods divided by `speed`, not the raw numbers. Every tuneable value of the field itself — the eight field parameters and the eight blob definitions — lives in `website/src/data/shader-background.json`, validated against the token registry so the field can never sample a token that does not exist. One control sits outside it: in light mode the site runs the whole layer, the field and its CSS fallback together, at reduced opacity, because the field is composed against the dark floor and the same alpha over a light floor reads as a grey cast behind the page opening. That value lives on the `.blur-container` rule in `website/src/app/globals.css`.

---

## Components

**Emphasis and colour are two props.** `variant` carries emphasis — `primary` (filled), `secondary` (outlined), `tertiary` (ghost) — and `tone` carries colour: `default` (the action colour), `neutral` (the greys), `destructive` (the danger colour, the `--color-action-destructive-*` family). Every action control that draws in more than one colour takes both: Button and CircularButton across all three emphases, SplitButton across primary and secondary, and the controls with only one shape take `tone` alone (SegmentedControl, ToggleGroup and Chip for their selection, AlertDialog for its whole confirmation, Spinner for its arc). Some cases sit outside the pair on purpose. Status-bearing components (Alert, Badge, Banner, Toast, StatusDot, Meter, UsageCard) keep the five status roles on `variant`, because their colour *is* their meaning. A data entry a component renders (a DropdownMenu or ContextMenu item, a CommandPalette command, a MessageActions item) marks danger with `destructive: true`, because an entry has no emphasis to pair a tone with; InterruptCard's options are the exception, because each is drawn as a Button with an emphasis of its own, so they take `variant` and `tone` like one. Gauge and Sparkline colour a data reading by status role, so their `tone` speaks that vocabulary (with the chart accent as its default), and Spinner's `tone` adds `inherit` to follow the control it sits in; the validator's `SANCTIONED_TONES` table records those three. Where a single-axis value predates the split, it stays as a deprecated alias that renders unchanged (`variant="neutral"` is primary × neutral), and `scripts/validate-prop-vocabulary.mjs` keeps the repo's own code on the current spelling. A component with no emphasis to express may use `variant` for its form instead (EmptyState's `plain` or `bordered`, AnchorNav's `list` or `floating`), and the validator's `SANCTIONED_FORM_VARIANTS` table records each, so a new one is a decision. `size` is the density axis, `default` and `compact`, the pair the theme's density lever moves; a component with a real scale of its own (Avatar's ladder, Dialog's panel widths, TopAppBar's platform sizes) keeps it, recorded in `SANCTIONED_SIZES`.

**Overlay behaviors are shared, not per-component.** The modal overlays (Dialog, AlertDialog, Drawer, CommandPalette, Lightbox, AttachmentViewer, BentoTile's overlay, and AppSidebar's mobile drawer) delegate their invisible machinery to the internal hooks in `src/behaviors/` — one implementation of each behavior, so an edge-case fix lands once. The six standalone modal components (Dialog, AlertDialog, Drawer, CommandPalette, Lightbox, AttachmentViewer) are controlled by design: `open` is required and owned by the host, and the overlay never changes it. It reports every dismissal (Escape, a backdrop press, the close control) by calling `onOpenChange(false)`, CommandPalette's hotkey asks to open the same way, and the host decides whether to pass the new `open` back. The contract: Escape routes through a layer stack and dismisses only the topmost open overlay (a non-dismissible top layer swallows it — nothing beneath can close through a blocking modal), and an Escape a component has already handled (`event.preventDefault()`) is left alone, which is how a listbox closing its own list keeps the Dialog around it open; focus is trapped over the *visible* focusable elements, and restored on close or unmount to the previously focused element when it is still in the document and visible, or else to the first visible control of its nearest visible ancestor (a button in a popover that closed underneath hands focus to the popover's trigger); everything outside the open overlay is made `inert`, so a screen reader's virtual cursor cannot browse the page behind a modal, except a live region such as the toast region, which keeps announcing and stays pressable; and the page scroll lock has two strategies — `pin` (default: the body is fixed in place at its scroll offset, because iOS Safari ignores `overflow: hidden` on the body — a touch, or a field focusing under the soft keyboard, still scrolls a hidden-overflow page out from under an overlay; counted, so stacked modals release it only when the last one closes) and `cancel-events` (CommandPalette's and BentoTile's overlay's: the scrollbar stays rendered and scroll input is cancelled outside the panel's own content, so the page cannot shift sideways under the overlay). The pin's counter is one of the layer's two published subpaths (`rift-ds/behaviors/useScrollLock`; the other is the link provider below, and everything else in `src/behaviors/` stays internal): host chrome with overlays of its own joins the same counter through `acquireScrollLock`/`releaseScrollLock`, because two body locks that cannot see each other unlock the page under whichever overlay closes last — a site nav drawer closing beneath an open Dialog is the canonical case, and the website's `lib/scroll-lock.ts` is that consumer. The stack owns the document-level *dismissal* listeners for every overlay, modal and non-modal: every component that imports `useLayer` registers on it, and `/foundations/accessibility` prints that roster from the imports, so no list of them is kept here. The non-modal layers use light dismiss (`dismissOnOutsidePress` plus the elements that count as inside). An outside press walks down from the top and closes every light-dismiss layer it lands outside of, stopping at the first layer that contains it or at a modal, so a press on a Dialog's body closes the menus above it and leaves the Dialog. A modal's backdrop click asks the stack whether the press was its own (`ownsPress()`), because a press that began while a menu was on top was the menu's, and closing both on one press was the bug the stack exists to prevent. A visible tooltip is a layer like any other: inside a Dialog the first Escape hides it and the second closes the Dialog (WCAG 1.4.13 asks that the tooltip be dismissible without moving focus). Three components keep a document key listener of their own, each sanctioned in `scripts/validate-overlay-listeners.mjs`, which fails the build on any other: CommandPalette for the global Cmd+K hotkey, which must hear the keyboard while the palette is closed, and Lightbox and AttachmentViewer for their arrow keys, gated on open (their specs own them).

**Popups are placed by one behaviour.** Every panel anchored to a control (Dropdown's, Combobox's, RichDropdown's and TimePicker's lists, DropdownMenu's panel and its sub-menus, so SplitButton's too, Popover, HoverCard, Tooltip, ModelPicker, ColorPicker, FilterBar's popovers, SourceChip's preview, AiButton's summary and request panels, and ThemeToggle's icon-strip tooltips) is placed by `src/behaviors/useAnchoredPosition.ts`, not by its own CSS. The panel keeps its place in the DOM, so it stacks inside the trigger's context and keeps any theme scoped above it, but is `position: fixed`, placed from the anchor's rectangle: a fixed panel is not clipped by an ancestor's `overflow`, so a menu in a table's scroll wrapper, a rail or a card shows whole. It takes the component's preferred side (each component's existing `position`, `placement` or `align` prop), flips to the opposite side when that one has too little room and the other has more, shifts along the edge to stay `--gap-200` clear of the box (measuring the panel's layout size, so an entrance that scales it in cannot push it past the edge), caps a list's height to the room on its side so it scrolls inside, and holds a listbox at least as wide as its control; the box is the viewport, or a transformed or blurred ancestor (a Dialog panel, the chat panel) that becomes the fixed panel's containing block. It follows scroll and resize once per frame by writing to the DOM, never through a React render, and hides the panel while its anchor is scrolled out of view. The side it lands on is the panel's `data-side`, which every entrance and slide keys on, so a flipped panel grows from its control. Portals would escape the clip too, but out of the theme scope, and a positioning library would add the package's first UI dependency; this was Tooltip's approach first, now shared. ContextMenu stays its own: it is anchored to a pointer, not a control, so it clamps to the viewport and closes on scroll. `src/stories/AnchoredPopups.stories.tsx` opens every one inside a box that clips its overflow and fails unless the panel reaches outside the box and is what is drawn there.

**Links go through the app's router.** Every component that takes an `href` renders its links through a link component rather than a fixed `<a>`, so in a routed app (Next.js, React Router) they navigate without reloading the page. The link component comes from the component's own `linkComponent` prop, else from the nearest `LinkProvider` (`rift-ds/behaviors/LinkProvider`, the layer's second published subpath), else a plain `<a>`, so nothing changes for a consumer who sets neither. It receives every anchor attribute, the ref, and a string `href`, which Next.js's `Link` takes as it is; a router whose link takes `to` needs a one-line adapter. A provider is React context, which only a client component can read, so the server-renderable link components (Breadcrumb, CircularButton, LinkList, ThreadPanel and WidgetRow) take the prop alone: a Server Component that renders them passes it on the instance. The chat widget's answer links follow the same provider through its default `renderLink`. AnchorNav keeps a plain `<a>`, because its links are in-page jumps it scrolls itself. `scripts/validate-component-contract.mjs` fails the build on any other raw `<a href>` in a component, and on a server-renderable component reading the provider.

**Shared primitives, never redrawn.** A few small things recur across many components, and each has exactly one drawing. The three form-control indicators are presentational companions of the real controls: `CheckboxIndicator` (Checkbox's box, with the one SVG check and minus, `CheckIcon` and `MinusIcon`, exported beside it), `RadioIndicator` (RadioButton's circle and dot) and `SwitchIndicator` (ToggleSwitch's track and thumb, whose check is the same SVG). Each is `aria-hidden` and driven by a `checked` prop, with its states keyed on its own modifier classes, so a host that is itself the control (SelectionCard, QuestionCard, InspectorToggleSwitch's bar, FilterBar's multi-select rows) draws the indicator and carries the role and state on its own root; a host that adds `ds-control-host` to that root lights the indicator on hover exactly as the standalone control does. The standalone controls ring their indicator on focus (2px `--color-action-primary-bg`, offset 2px); a host that is itself the target rings its own edge. The chosen mark, `ChosenMark` (exported from the Dropdown module), is the trailing check a listbox or menu row shows on its selected item: one glyph at `--icon-size-500` in the action colour, drawn by Dropdown, Combobox, RichDropdown, TimePicker, ModelPicker, DropdownMenu, ContextMenu and FilterBar's single-select rows, rendered only on the selected row, whose `aria-selected` or `aria-checked` carries the state. A mark is not a control: a row whose items are picked independently shows the checkbox indicator instead. Text that is read but not seen takes the one `ds-visually-hidden` class from `src/behaviors/visually-hidden.css`; a hidden native input that must stay focusable keeps its own rule with the reason beside it (FileInput). A close button in a header is a compact tertiary CircularButton (Dialog, Drawer, CommandPalette; ThreadTabs' 24px tab close, Lightbox's media controls and AppSidebar's mobile drawer close, which is its collapse toggle doing a second job, are the three sanctioned exceptions, each with its own geometry), and a mutually exclusive set of pills is a SegmentedControl, never a hand-built radiogroup (ModelPicker's effort row). `scripts/validate-shared-primitives.mjs` fails the build on a second drawing of any of these: a font glyph standing in for a checkbox or radio, an indicator class written outside its owning module, a copied switch, a per-component check rule, or a second visually-hidden recipe; its `SANCTIONED` table is the list of deliberate exceptions. The `component-consistency-audit` skill is the review for what the validator cannot see.

**Surfaces by family.** The same shape of rule holds the surfaces together: a component takes its family's treatment rather than its own. A choice offered as a row (SelectionCard's cards, QuestionCard's option rows) is a form control and wears Input's surface: `--color-input-bg-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-input-border-primary` hairline at `--radius-300`, `--color-input-border-hover` on hover and `--color-input-border-selected` when picked. A card that holds content (Panel, the chat furniture, Accordion, NotificationCenter, the foundations swatches) sits on `--color-bg-container-primary` behind `--color-bg-container-border`, hovering its header to `--color-bg-container-secondary`; the semi fill with a backdrop blur (`--color-bg-container-primary-semi`) is reserved for tiles that float over the ambient background (Card, Widget, PromptSuggestions). A row inside an overlay (a menu item, a listbox option, a command, a tree row) has no fill at rest, hovers and focuses on `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` at `--radius-200`, the inner step of its `--radius-300` panel. The current item of a navigation list (AppSidebar, SidebarPanel, ThreadPanel, ThreadTabs, NotificationCenter's tabs) holds `--color-action-passive-bg-active`, one step past the hover wash so current and hovered never read the same. Disabled is `opacity: 0.4` with `cursor: not-allowed` everywhere.

### Button

**`ds-button`** — The primary interactive element. Always on `--radius-pill` (a full pill in the base theme; a squared theme takes it to the input's corner, so a button and an input always match), and the label never wraps (`white-space: nowrap`, shared with AiButton) — a squeezed layout shortens around the button rather than folding the pill into two lines. Text uses `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` (default) or `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` (compact). Icon size: 24px (default), 20px (compact). Transitions: background-color, border-color, and opacity at `--motion-duration-base` / `--motion-ease-standard`. A trailing icon (`iconRight`) nudges 2px right on hover at `--motion-duration-fast` / `--motion-ease-entrance` — a small confirmation that the button leads somewhere; never on disabled buttons. Disabled state: `opacity: 0.4`, `cursor: not-allowed` — never hidden. The text is `label`; when it is absent, plain-text children stand in for it (`<Button>Save</Button>` reads "Save"), with a development-only warning naming `label` as the documented prop, and anything richer than text is ignored, since icons ride `iconLeft` and `iconRight`. The warning comes from `warnOnce` in `src/behaviors/devWarning.ts`, the library's one development-warning helper, which a production bundle drops.

Two independent axes set the look. **`variant` is the emphasis** — `primary` (filled), `secondary` (outlined), `tertiary` (ghost) — and **`tone` is the colour** — `default` (the action colour), `neutral` (the greys), `destructive` (the danger colour). Each cell renders one modifier class:

| `variant` × `tone` | Class | Fill | Border | Text |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| primary × default | `ds-button--primary` | `--color-action-primary-bg` | none | `--color-action-primary-text` |
| secondary × default | `ds-button--secondary` | transparent | `--color-action-primary-border` 1px | `--color-text-primary` |
| tertiary × default or neutral | `ds-button--tertiary` | `--color-action-passive-bg` (near-transparent) | none | `--color-action-passive-text` |
| primary × neutral | `ds-button--neutral` | `--color-action-neutral-bg` | none | `--color-action-neutral-text` |
| secondary × neutral | `ds-button--secondary-neutral` | transparent | `--color-action-neutral-border` 1px | `--color-text-primary` |
| primary × destructive | `ds-button--primary-destructive` | `--color-action-destructive-bg` | none | `--color-action-destructive-text` |
| secondary or tertiary × destructive | `ds-button--destructive` | transparent | `--color-action-destructive-border` 1px | `--color-action-destructive-text-secondary` |

Hover: primary and secondary → `--color-action-primary-bg-hover`; both neutral cells → `--color-action-neutral-bg-hover` with `--color-action-neutral-text`; outlined destructive → fills with `--color-action-destructive-bg` under `--color-action-destructive-text`; filled destructive → `--color-action-destructive-bg-hover`, then `--color-action-destructive-bg-active` when pressed. Active: primary and secondary → `--color-action-primary-bg-active`; both neutral cells → `--color-action-neutral-bg-active`. Hover and active walk away from the page — deepening in light mode, brightening in dark — so the pressed fill never sinks toward the floor behind it. The neutral tone is the quiet option: a button's full silhouette without the action colour's weight. Tertiary is already grey, so the neutral tone leaves it unchanged, and a tertiary destructive takes the outlined look, because a danger action needs a visible edge. The filled destructive is for the one irreversible action a surface leads with. The single-axis values from before the split stay as deprecated aliases: `variant="neutral"` is primary × neutral and `variant="destructive"` is secondary × destructive, which is why those two cells keep their original class names.

Sizes: `default` (padding 8px × 20px), `compact` (padding 6px × 12px).

**A tertiary button never stands alone in an action row.** Its transparent resting field has no visible edge, so a lone tertiary reads as misaligned text rather than as an action. An action row leads with a primary or secondary button, and tertiary follows it — nav lists and icon rows, where every entry is deliberately passive, are the exception, not action rows.

Loading state: `loading` puts a `tone="inherit"` Spinner in the left icon slot (24px default / 16px compact), keeps the variant's full-colour appearance (no disabled dim), blocks interaction (`pointer-events: none`, `cursor: progress`, `disabled` attribute), and sets `aria-busy`. A loading `href` button renders the `<button>` branch so the link is truly inert.

### ButtonGroup

**`ds-button-group`** — Inline container that composes Buttons for nav and subnav contexts. Two orientations: `horizontal` (default — `--gap-500` between buttons, used in top navigation) and `vertical` (`--gap-050`, left-aligned hug-content column for subnav/mobile). Purely compositional: each entry is a full `ButtonProps` config passed straight through to Button, so variants and disabled states mix freely — except that an entry with no `variant` defaults to `tertiary`, not Button's own `primary`, so nav groups read as passive by default. `role="group"`, named through the native `aria-label` (`ariaLabel` is its deprecated alias).

### CircularButton

**`ds-circular-button`** — Round icon-only button, 40px (default) or 32px (compact), always `--radius-pill`. The same two axes as Button — `variant` for the emphasis, `primary` (`--color-action-primary-bg` fill), `secondary` (outlined, `--border-025` + action border tokens) or `tertiary` (ghost, `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` on hover), and `tone` for the colour, `default`, `neutral` or `destructive`: primary × neutral is the solid `--color-action-neutral-bg` fill (`ds-circular-button--neutral`, always shown at rest), secondary × neutral the `--color-action-neutral-border` outline filling with the neutral greys on hover and press (`ds-circular-button--secondary-neutral`), tertiary is unchanged by the neutral tone, and the destructive tone follows Button: primary × destructive is the filled circle (`ds-circular-button--primary-destructive`, the `--color-action-destructive-bg` steps), secondary and tertiary the outlined danger look (`ds-circular-button--destructive`: `--color-action-destructive-border` and `--color-action-destructive-text-secondary`, filling with `--color-action-destructive-bg` on hover and press). The legacy `variant="neutral"` stays as a deprecated alias for primary × neutral. Same state set (`default`/`hover`/`active`/`disabled`). Icon is a single Material Symbol at 24px default / 20px compact. Renders as `<a>` when `href` is set. `ariaLabel` is required — there is no visible label. Follows the published API contract like Button: `disabled` is the real boolean (`state` stays as the documentation-only affordance), the ref forwards to whichever element renders, and unrecognised props — `data-*`, `aria-*`, native event handlers — spread onto it. `loading` swaps the icon for a `tone="inherit"` Spinner, keeps full-colour appearance, blocks interaction, and sets `aria-busy` — same contract as Button. **Self-labelling tooltip**: because there is no visible label, the button wears a Tooltip carrying `ariaLabel` by default, on hover and focus — the same convention MessageActions established for its icon row. `tooltip` accepts a string for different wording or `false` to opt out (a host that labels the control another way, or owns the button's box directly — SplitButton's trigger); `tooltipPosition` picks the side, for hosts whose edge would clip the default top placement (the chat panel's header opens its tooltips downward).

### SegmentedControl

**`ds-segmented-control`** — Horizontal set of mutually exclusive options on a `--color-bg-container-primary` track with a `--radius-pill` pill silhouette. The active segment takes `--color-action-primary-bg` with `--color-action-primary-text`, drawn by a single sliding pill: one absolutely positioned indicator element slides and resizes between segments (`transform` + `width` at `--motion-duration-base` / `--motion-ease-entrance`), its box written from the active segment's measurements on every change and re-measured without animation on resize. A move that lands before the pill's first painted frame snaps rather than slides, so a post-hydration correction (the header theme toggle hydrates on the server's segment, then jumps to the visitor's) never animates across the control on page load. Until the first client-side measurement lands (SSR, first paint) the active segment paints its own background, so the selection is never missing; once the pill takes over, hovering the active segment darkens the pill itself (`--color-action-primary-bg-hover`). Idle segments use `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` in `--color-text-secondary` with `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` on hover, and an idle segment's icon shares the label's `--color-text-secondary` — never a dimmer icon tier — so icon-only segments keep label-strength contrast. `tone="neutral"` swaps the active fill to `--color-action-neutral-bg`/`--color-action-neutral-text`, for sets where the selection should not carry the action selection convention's weight (the deprecated `variant="neutral"` still works). Segments accept an optional Material Symbol icon and per-segment `disabled`. The track carries a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, and its corner is concentric with the pill: `--radius-pill` plus the track padding plus that hairline, so a theme that squares the pill token keeps the two curves parallel. Sizes: `default`, `compact`; `fullWidth` stretches segments across the container. `collapse` makes the strip shed parts rather than overflow when its container is too narrow for it: the control measures its own three natural widths once from the full render — everything, labels alone, icons alone — and picks the widest that fits on every later resize, so the ladder never oscillates and a container that grows back re-reads the same numbers. **Decoration goes before meaning**: the icon is shed first and the label last, it only falls to icons when every segment has one, and a hidden label is clipped rather than removed, so the segment keeps its accessible name. Off by default — the control overflows as it always did. AgentRail is the first consumer, where it is what makes four tabs fit a 360px rail. `showLabels={false}` asks for the icon rung outright, for a host that knows it is in a small mode rather than measuring for it (the playground's bottom bar on a compact stage): the same clipped labels, so each segment keeps its name, and a segment with no icon keeps its label on screen. Announces as a tablist (`role="tablist"`, segments are `role="tab"` with `aria-selected`). Keyboard: Arrow keys cycle enabled segments, Home/End jump to the ends. The selection follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`, with `activeSegment` and `onSegmentChange` kept as deprecated aliases).

### ThemeToggle

**`ds-theme-toggle`** — The colour-mode control: System, Light and Dark (the `routine`, `light_mode` and `dark_mode` glyphs) on a SegmentedControl, which draws every pixel, so it reads as the system's one mutually exclusive set of pills; the root class only keeps the strip at its own width in a squeezed row. `compact` by default, the size a header or a settings row wants; `showLabels={false}` makes it an icon strip: each label becomes its segment's tooltip on hover and keyboard focus (Tooltip's surface, after the same hover-intent delay), placed by the shared anchored-position behaviour so a clipping seat such as a sidebar's floor cannot cut it off, and it stays the segment's name, and the three labels and the strip's name (`aria-label`, "Colour mode") are props with English defaults. It runs on `useColorMode` (`useColorMode.tsx` beside it), the setting and its setter: two attributes on `<html>` carry the mode, `data-theme-setting` (the choice) and `data-theme` (what it resolves to, which every token reads), every instance subscribes to the attribute, so no two controls disagree, across tabs too, and the choice is stored under `storageKey` (default `theme`). `colorModeScript()` (`colorMode.tsx`, safe to import from a Server Component) returns the script that applies the stored choice before first paint and keeps `system` following the operating system; render it in the document head. The documentation site's header toggle and its pre-paint script are these, so the site and a consumer's app cannot apply the mode differently.

### ThemePicker

**`ds-theme-picker`** — The theme switcher: a circular trigger the row height across (`--control-height-*`), on the track recipe the theme toggle and the command palette trigger wear (`--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, SegmentedControl's track curve), wearing the active theme's swatch as a chip on the icon scale, in the theme's own colour and corner. It opens a menu of every theme on offer, DropdownMenu's surface, each row a `menuitemradio` previewing the theme's swatch, its name in its heading face and a line in its body face, the chosen one holding the passive wash with the shared ChosenMark. The host supplies `themes` and applies the choice through `onValueChange` (a `data-brand` attribute for the shipped presets), so the picker is the control and never the theme engine; `value` and `open` follow the pair convention. The menu is placed by the shared anchored-position behaviour, preferring `placement` and lining up with the trigger's `align` edge, and dismisses through the layer stack, Escape returning focus to the trigger; the arrow keys, Home and End move through the rows and into an optional `footer`, which sits after the themes below a divider (the site puts a link to the playground there). The trigger's and the menu's names are `triggerLabel` and `menuLabel`.

### ToggleGroup

**`ds-toggle-group`** — A row of pressable buttons sharing a bordered `--radius-300` container (`--border-025` on `--color-bg-container-border`, filled `--color-bg-container-primary` like SegmentedControl's track, so the group reads as one control); items hover on `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`. Single-select by default (pressing the active item keeps it active); `multiple` allows any combination. Active items fill with `--color-action-primary-bg` and `--color-action-primary-text` (`tone="neutral"`, or the deprecated `variant="neutral"`, swaps them to the `--color-action-neutral-*` greys); items can be text labels or Material Symbol icons (`icon: true`). Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Group-level `disabled` applies the standard `opacity: 0.4` and `cursor: not-allowed` treatment. `role="group"` + `aria-pressed` per item. The selection follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`), a string when single and an array when `multiple`.

### SplitButton

**`ds-split-button`** — A primary action with an attached menu of alternatives ("Save" beside "Save as draft"), composed from Button, CircularButton and DropdownMenu inside one pill silhouette. The outer corners stay `--radius-pill` and the meeting edges square to `--radius-050`; the main segment's inner-edge padding tightens one step (`--padding-300` default, `--padding-200` compact), since the squared edge has no pill cap to clear; and the chevron is optically centred, nudged 2px (1px compact) toward the flat edge, because the flat-plus-round-cap shape carries its visual mass left of the geometric centre. The split is drawn per variant: on primary emphasis a `--border-025`-wide gap of bare page between the filled segments, on secondary emphasis the gap collapses and the trigger drops its leading border, so the main segment's own outline is the single divider line rather than two hairlines side by side. Like Button, `variant` sets the emphasis (`primary` or `secondary`) and `tone` the colour (`default`, `neutral` or `destructive`), passed to both segments so they always match; the seam follows the emphasis, so a filled destructive split keeps the gap and an outlined one collapses it; the legacy `variant="neutral"` is a deprecated alias for primary × neutral. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. The label segment fires `onClick`; the chevron segment is a CircularButton (accessible name from `menuLabel`) opening a DropdownMenu with the standard entry tree, keyboard behaviour, and `align` options. `loading` runs Button's loading contract on the label segment and holds the trigger inert at full colour with a progress cursor — the loading contract's no-dim appearance, because a disabled dim on one half would split the pill into two unrelated controls; `disabled` takes both segments down together, and when both flags are set the disabled dim wins across the whole control, matching Button's own precedence.

### Badge

**`ds-badge`** — Inline status label. Radius `--radius-100` (4px) — notably tighter than buttons and inputs. Text: `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` (14px/500). Padding: 2px vertical × 8px horizontal. Each of the five status variants (`info`, `positive`, `warning`, `error`, `neutral`) maps directly to its `--color-status-*-bg` and `--color-status-*-text` tokens. Badges are borderless by decision (2026-08-26): the tinted fill carries the status on its own, and the old per-variant borders sat below 3:1 against their fills — a transparent `--border-025` border remains for stable geometry. Renders with `role="status"` for accessibility.

### Chip

**`ds-chip`** — Compact pill for attributes, filters, and inline metadata. Always on `--radius-pill`, like Button. Neutral by default: `--color-bg-page-primary` fill, `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, `--color-text-secondary` text in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` (14px/500). Icon size: 24px (large), 20px (default), 16px (compact) — below `large`, smaller than Button's because the chip is a tighter control. `removeLabel` also takes a function of the chip's label text, so each chip in a group gets its own name.

Three sizes, matching the Button ladder one rung down: `compact` (24px tall), `default` (32px), and `large` (40px). `large` is the exception to "tighter than a Button" — it takes the default Button's `--padding-200`/`--padding-500` box, `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` (16px/500) text, and 24px icons, so a pill that is a primary tap target rather than metadata reads at the same weight as the body text around it. PromptSuggestions is its intended consumer.

Distinct from Badge: a Badge communicates *status* through the five status colours and is never interactive; a Chip is neutral and optionally interactive.

A `selected` chip takes the action selection fill (`--color-action-primary-bg` under `--color-action-primary-text`); `tone="neutral"` moves it to the `--color-action-neutral-*` greys (`ds-chip--selected-neutral`), for filter rows whose selection should read quietly, the call SegmentedControl's neutral tone makes. Renders as a `<span>` when non-clickable, a `<button>` when given `onClick` (with `aria-pressed` when `selected` is set). `onRemove` adds a trailing close button (Material Symbol `close`) with its own accessible label; when a chip is both clickable and removable the container stays a `<span>` and the main region becomes an inner button so buttons never nest.

States:
- **Hover** (clickable): `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` fill; **Active**: `--color-action-passive-bg-active`
- **Selected**: `--color-action-primary-bg` fill with `--color-action-primary-text` — same action selection convention as SegmentedControl
- **Disabled**: `opacity: 0.4`, `cursor: not-allowed`
- **Focus**: standard 2px `--color-action-primary-bg` focus ring

Sizes: `large` (the default Button box, 40px tall), `default` (padding 6px × 12px — 32px tall), `compact` (padding 2px × 8px — 24px tall).

### Alert

**`ds-alert`** — Container for inline status messages. Same five variants as Badge. Wider form — carries an icon, title, and description block. References same `--color-status-*` tokens. Use when message needs to persist in the layout (vs Toast which is ephemeral). Sizes: `default`, and `compact`, which tightens the padding and sets the title and description in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` with a smaller icon. `dismissible` adds a close control at the trailing edge (named by `dismissLabel`); it only reports the click through `onDismiss`, and removing the alert is the consumer's render decision, the same as Banner.

### Banner

**`ds-banner`** — Full-width page-level status strip: the third sibling in the feedback family. Alert sits inline in the content, Toast interrupts and leaves, Banner spans the page and stays until dismissed. Same five status variants on the `--color-status-*-bg`/`-text` pairs, square-cornered and edge-to-edge by design — it belongs to the page frame, not the content column. One `--font-paragraph-sm-*` line with an emphasised leading `title` in the `-em` weight, a per-variant Material Symbol (neutral takes `campaign`, the announcement horn, rather than Alert's `info`), an `action` slot for a compact Button, and an optional dismiss control; `align="center"` centres the content for short environment notices. Stateless like Alert: `dismissible` shows the close control, `onDismiss` reports the click, and removal is the consumer's render decision. `role="status"`. The dismiss button is named by `dismissLabel`.

### Input / Textarea

**`ds-input`** — Label + field + helper text stack with 8px (`--gap-200`) vertical gap. Field: `--radius-300`, `--color-input-bg-primary` fill, `--color-input-border-primary` hairline. Label: `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*`. Body: `--font-paragraph-*`. Helper: `--font-paragraph-sm-*` in `--color-text-tertiary`.

States:
- **Default**: `--color-input-border-primary`
- **Hover**: `--color-input-border-hover` (a mid step of the action colour's ramp, clearing 3:1 against each page)
- **Focus**: `--color-input-border-selected` (the active border, on the action colour's weight)
- **Error**: `--color-status-error-border` on border and helper text
- **Disabled**: `--color-input-bg-disabled` fill, `--color-input-border-disabled`, `--color-input-text-disabled` on label/text

Icon slots (Material Symbols Rounded): left icon at 16px from edge, right icon at 16px from edge. Compact size: padding 6px × 12px, icon 20px.

Field-level error state is where the system's responsibility ends, by design: form-level validation — inline error summaries, field grouping, when to validate — is orchestration, and it belongs to the consumer's form layer, not to a component library. Every form control carries the same error vocabulary — an `error` flag that recolours the helper line and marks the control invalid, plus a status border wherever the control has one — which is exactly the surface a form library needs to drive.

### Checkbox

**`ds-checkbox`** — Custom checkbox with an inline SVG check (and minus for `indeterminate`) instead of font glyphs, for pixel-perfect centring; the glyphs are `CheckIcon` and `MinusIcon`, exported beside the component as the one home of the check every control draws. The box is `CheckboxIndicator`, exported beside the component for a host that is itself the control (see the Components intro): `--radius-200` with a `--border-050` `--color-input-border-primary` hairline on a `--color-input-bg-primary` fill, Input's field surface, so it stands off a container as well as the page; checked state fills `--color-action-primary-bg` with `--color-action-primary-text-active` stroke, hover shifts to `--color-action-primary-bg-hover` (through the `ds-control-host` class, so a host's hover lights it the same way), and focus rings the box (2px `--color-action-primary-bg`, offset 2px). Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Disabled: `opacity: 0.4`, `cursor: not-allowed`. `helperText` renders a `--font-paragraph-sm-*` line under the label (stacked in a text column beside the box, the box aligned to the first line); `error` recolours it to `--color-status-error-border` and sets `aria-invalid`, both announced via `aria-describedby`. A `CheckboxGroup` wrapper renders a labelled list of checkboxes bound to a value array — it composes `<Field group>`, so the group label, required marker, helper/error line and the `aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby` wiring are Field's, with group-level `error` carrying `aria-invalid` on the `role="group"` node. `checked` follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`checked`/`defaultChecked`/`onCheckedChange`), and CheckboxGroup's value array does too (`values`/`defaultValues`/`onValuesChange`).

### RadioButton

**`ds-radio`** — Single-select control: a `--radius-pill` circle with a `--border-050` `--color-input-border-primary` ring on a `--color-input-bg-primary` fill (Input's field surface, so it stands off a container as well as the page) and an inner dot that fills `--color-action-primary-bg` when checked; hover shifts the ring to `--color-action-primary-bg` and focus rings the circle with Checkbox's 2px `--color-action-primary-bg` ring. The circle is `RadioIndicator`, exported beside the component for a host that is itself the control (see the Components intro). Implemented as a `role="radio"` element with `aria-checked`, Space/Enter activation, and managed `tabIndex`; grouping is React state through `RadioGroup` (`value`/`onValueChange` — the `name` prop is a deprecated no-op, since nothing renders a native `<input type="radio">` for it to group). Label uses `--font-paragraph-*`; `helperText` adds a `--font-paragraph-sm-*` line under it, announced via `aria-describedby` — `error` recolours it, deliberately without `aria-invalid`, which ARIA does not allow on `role="radio"`; the group form carries it instead. `RadioGroup` composes `<Field group>` (label, required marker, helper/error, `aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby`/`aria-invalid` on the `role="radiogroup"` node). Disabled: `opacity: 0.4`. RadioGroup follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`); a lone RadioButton is fully controlled by design, because its checked state belongs to the group (an uncontrolled radio could only ever turn on).

### ToggleSwitch

**`ds-toggle-switch`** — Binary on/off switch: a 42×24px `--radius-pill` track filled with `--color-action-primary-bg` when on, holding a 20px `--color-control-thumb` circle that slides with a spring-curve transition and shows the same SVG check as Checkbox. The track is `SwitchIndicator`, exported beside the component for a host that is itself the control (see the Components intro); focus rings the track, not the label. Rendered as a `<button role="switch" aria-checked>`; the optional inline label uses `--font-paragraph-*`, and `helperText` stacks a `--font-paragraph-sm-*` line under it (announced via `aria-describedby`; `error` recolours it and sets `aria-invalid`). Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Used for binary settings — an on/off preference with immediate effect. (The site's own theme control outgrew it: three states made it a SegmentedControl.) SelectionCard's `toggle` mode and InspectorToggleSwitch's bar draw this exact anatomy through `SwitchIndicator`. `checked` follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`checked`/`defaultChecked`/`onCheckedChange`); uncontrolled, the switch starts on unless `defaultChecked` is `false`.

### Slider

**`ds-slider`** — Range input styled with a two-tone track: `--color-action-primary-bg` fill up to the current value, `--color-bg-container-tertiary` beyond it, with a `--radius-pill` thumb that gains a `--color-action-primary-bg-hover` focus ring. Native `<input type="range">` underneath, so keyboard and screen-reader behaviour come for free (`min`/`max`/`step` props). Composes inside Field: `label` (a real `<label htmlFor>` — the range input is labelable), `helperText`, `error`, `required`, and `showValue`, which prints the live value in Field's `aside` slot opposite the helper; with any of them present the root stacks as a column (`--fielded`), and a bare slider keeps its original row untouched. Unlabelled sliders fall back to an `aria-label` (native attribute first, then the deprecated `ariaLabel` prop, then "Slider"). Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Disabled: `opacity: 0.4`. The value follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`), starting at 50 when uncontrolled.

### InspectorSection / InspectorSlider / InspectorInput / InspectorToggleSwitch / InspectorSegmentedControl / InspectorDropdown / InspectorColorPicker

**The Inspector family** — one-row controls for tool panels and settings rails (the playground's rails are the first consumer). Each control is one bar with its name inside on the left and its state on the right, so a setting costs one row with no label above it. They are counterparts, not replacements: Slider, Input, ToggleSwitch, SegmentedControl, Dropdown and ColorPicker stay the controls for forms, where a label above and helper text below earn their room; an Inspector control is for a dense panel of many settings. The family lives in one shared folder, `src/components/Inspector/`, with one stylesheet (`Inspector.css`) every member imports, the way the chart set shares `Chart.css`.

**`ds-inspector-bar`** — the shared surface. Shaped and sized like Button, not Input: corners on `--radius-pill`, so the theme's pill and radius levers reshape the bars with every button, and a `size` prop matching Button's whose height, side inset and type are computed from Button's own tokens, so a bar and a Button of one size sit level: `default` is `--padding-200` twice plus `--font-paragraph-emphasis-line-height` (40px) with `--padding-500` sides and `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` type; `compact` (`ds-inspector-bar--compact`) is `--padding-150` twice plus `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-line-height` (32px) with `--padding-300` sides and `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*`. The name takes the emphasis weight in `--color-text-primary`; the reading takes the regular weight in `--color-text-secondary`. Colour climbs the neutral Button's ladder one step per state, which holds in both themes: the bar rests on `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`, a hovered bar and a slider's fill take `--color-action-neutral-bg-hover`, and a pressed one `--color-action-neutral-bg-active`. **The action colour appears where the library's own controls put it, and nowhere else**: the focus ring (the system's 2px `--color-action-primary-bg` outline, outside the bar), an on switch (ToggleSwitch's own track) and a selected segment (SegmentedControl's sliding pill), so a setting reads the same in a panel as in a form. A slider's fill and a hovered or pressed bar stay on the neutral ladder. Disabled is the system's 0.4 opacity and `not-allowed` on the whole bar. Wherever the platform has the control, it is the platform's: a native range, text field, checkbox or radio sits under or over the paint, so pointer, keyboard, form submission and screen-reader semantics come for free.

**`ds-inspector-slider`** — the whole bar is the slider and its fill (`__fill`) is the value. A native range input covers the bar at zero opacity, its thumb drawn `--border-050` wide so the value-to-position mapping stays within a pixel of the painted fill, so dragging anywhere or clicking to jump both work. A short `__grip` line in `--color-text-secondary` sits just inside the fill's edge, held `--padding-200` off both ends so it never rides the rounded corners; hover darkens the fill a step and turns the grip `--color-text-primary`, and holding it stands the grip taller with a `grabbing` cursor. The reading shows the step's precision (0.02 reads "0.50") unless `format` says otherwise, and the same string is the range's `aria-valuetext`. The fill position reaches the stylesheet through `--ds-inspector-pct`, never an inline declaration.

**`ds-inspector-input`** — the name is the field's real `<label>` (a click on it focuses the field), and the field takes the rest of the bar with its text right-aligned and its placeholder in `--color-text-tertiary`. The field is `width: 0; flex: 1`, so it never asks for its intrinsic character width and push its container. Hover and focus both lift the bar a step.

**`ds-inspector-toggle-switch`** — the whole bar is the switch: a native checkbox with `role="switch"` covers it, and ToggleSwitch's own track and thumb sit at the bar's end (`__switch`, drawn by `ToggleSwitch.css`), so the on state, the check in the thumb and the spring all match the form switch: the default switch in a default bar, the compact one in a compact bar. The component mirrors the checked state (the prop when controlled, the last change when not), because ToggleSwitch's paint keys off its `--off` class.

**`ds-inspector-segmented-control`** — the name on the left and the options as `__segment` chips in the bar's end, the bar's right inset shrunk to `--padding-100` so the chips sit inset rather than touching the edge. Chips share the bar's `--radius-pill` shape; idle chips are the reading's colour and weight and hover takes `--color-action-passive-bg-active`. The selection is SegmentedControl's: `--color-action-primary-bg` under `--color-action-primary-text` at the name's weight, darkening to `-hover` under the pointer, drawn as one `__pill` that slides between chips on `--motion-duration-base` / `--motion-ease-entrance`. The component mirrors the selection and measures the selected chip (again on resize, since a web font can change the widths), and until that first measurement lands the chip paints its own fill, so the server render is already right. Each option is a native radio (one radiogroup named by the bar's label, arrow keys moving the selection, per-option `disabled`), and focus rings the chip rather than the whole bar.

**`ds-inspector-dropdown`** — the library Dropdown with its trigger dressed as a bar, so the menu, its keyboard model and its per-option font previews (`font` on an option) are Dropdown's own, and the selected option's face carries into the bar. The name is an absolutely positioned overlay that passes clicks through, and the trigger reserves room for it through `--ds-inspector-label-width`, measured (with a ResizeObserver, since a web font can change a name's width after load) rather than guessed. The Dropdown is named by the same words through `aria-label`. It passes `value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange` through to Dropdown, so it works controlled or uncontrolled.

**`ds-inspector-color-picker`** — the library ColorPicker with its trigger dressed as a bar, so the panel (saturation area, hue and optional alpha sliders, hex field), its keyboard model and its controlled/uncontrolled contract are ColorPicker's own. The trigger fills the bar and right-aligns its contents: the hex reading in the reading's colour and weight, then a round swatch one line tall (`--ds-inspector-line-height`, `--radius-pill`) whose end inset is computed to centre it in the bar, so it sits concentric with the pill's end cap. The name is an overlay that passes clicks through, and needs no measured reservation the way the dropdown's does, since the reading sits at the far end. The trigger is named by `aria-labelledby` pointing at the name and then at the hex reading (ColorPicker gives the reading an id for exactly this), so it reads as the setting followed by its value ("Tint colour #163300"). The panel opens under the bar and spans its width. Disabled dims the whole bar, name included.

**`ds-inspector-section`** — a collapsible, titled run of controls: a full-width header button (`--font-paragraph-emphasis-*`, an `expand_more` chevron at `--icon-size-500` turning 180 degrees) over a body that stacks its controls `--gap-200` apart, and stacked sections are divided by a `--border-025` `--color-divider` hairline (none under the last). The header's `--padding-300` sits above and below the title, and the body's foot is sized so the last control sits the same distance from the divider. The body opens through `grid-template-rows: 0fr` to `1fr` on `--motion-duration-slow` / `--motion-ease-emphasized`, in a `minmax(0, 1fr)` column so a wide control can never push the panel. Two behaviours set it apart from Accordion: the body clips only while closed or animating and goes `overflow: visible` once settled open (`--settled`), so a Dropdown menu inside can open past the section's edge; and a closed body is `inert`, so its controls leave the tab order. `open` / `onOpenChange` control it, `defaultOpen` seeds it.

### Swatch

**`ds-swatch`** — Clickable colour tile: a 24px `<button>` (20px `compact`) whose background is the `value` colour, passed through the `--ds-swatch-color` custom property so the stylesheet stays data-free. Shapes: `circle` (default, `--radius-pill`) and `square` (`--radius-200`). A hairline `--color-bg-container-border` inset keeps light colours visible on light surfaces; `selected` draws the theme-aware ring (`--color-bg-page-primary` gap + `--color-text-primary` outline) and sets `aria-pressed`. Purely presentational — no `'use client'`, so it renders from Server Components. Disabled: `opacity: 0.4`.

### ColorPicker

**`ds-colorpicker`** — Colour picker composed in `Field`: a bordered trigger (`--radius-300`, input border tokens) holding a checkerboard-backed swatch and optional hex text (`showText`), opening a `--shadow-floating` panel with a saturation/brightness area (2D `role="slider"`: pointer, arrow keys, and Home/End on the saturation axis), a hue slider, an optional alpha slider (`showAlpha`, emits 8-digit hex below 100%), and a hex field. Works controlled (`value` + `onValueChange`, fires live while dragging) or uncontrolled (`defaultValue`); `name` renders a hidden input for native form submission. The white/black overlay gradients and hue spectrum are colour-space constants written as `hsl()` literals — the one sanctioned departure from hex-free component CSS, since no theme token can represent them. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Disabled: `opacity: 0.4`. The panel and its controls are named by `panelLabel`, `saturationLabel`, `saturationValueText`, `hueLabel`, `alphaLabel` and `hexLabel`. With no visible `label`, the trigger is named by `triggerLabel(hex)` ("Choose colour, #hex"); a passed `aria-label` still wins.

### DateInput

**`ds-date-input`** — Form field wrapping the native date input with the standard input anatomy: label (`--font-paragraph-emphasis-*`), `--radius-300` field on `--color-input-bg-primary` with `--color-input-border-primary`, hover/selected borders (`--color-input-border-hover` / `--color-input-border-selected`), calendar icon in `--color-icon-primary`, and helper/error text below (`--font-paragraph-sm-*`, error via `--color-status-error-border`). Supports `min`/`max`, `required`, `disabled` (input disabled tokens), and sizes `default`/`compact`. Value format is `YYYY-MM-DD`.

### DatePicker

**`ds-date-picker`** — Inline calendar: month header with `--radius-pill` chevron nav buttons, a weekday header row (`weekdayLabels`, default `Su–Sa`, with `weekdayNames` spoken in their place), and a grid of day cells. The selected day fills `--color-action-primary-bg` with `--color-action-primary-text` at `--radius-pill`; today is highlighted; out-of-range days (`min`/`max`) and the `disabled` state get the standard muted treatment. Container: `--radius-300` on `--color-input-bg-primary` with `--color-input-border-primary`. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Emits `onDateSelect` with `YYYY-MM-DD`. Selection is controlled by design: the picker never sets `value` itself, so the host stores the date `onDateSelect` reports and passes it back as `value`; only the month in view and the focused day are the picker's own state. The month heading comes from `monthLabel`, the arrows from `previousLabel`/`nextLabel`, and each day's name from `dayLabel`. The days are a WAI-ARIA date grid (`role="grid"`, rows of `columnheader`s and `gridcell`s around the day buttons, laid out as subgrids of the one CSS grid) with one roving tab stop: the last-focused day, else the selected day, else today, else the month's first enabled day. Arrows move a day or a week, Home and End go to the ends of the week, Page Up and Page Down a month (with Shift, a year); a move past the month in view turns the page with focus on the new day, and a move past `min`/`max` is held at the bound, so focus never lands on a disabled day.

### TimePicker

**`ds-timepicker`** — Time-of-day form field, the clock sibling of Dropdown. The trigger is a real `<button>` styled with the input anatomy (label via Field, `--radius-300`, input border/bg tokens, helper/error text), led by a `schedule` icon at `--icon-size-500` and closed by a rotating chevron. Open state reveals a `--radius-300` listbox on `--color-bg-page-primary` elevated with `--shadow-floating`, max-height 240px with scroll, which opens scrolled to the selected or first option. Options are generated from `minTime`/`maxTime`/`stepMinutes` (defaults 00:00, 23:30, 30) and rendered with `tabular-nums`; `hourFormat` switches the display between "2:30 PM" and "14:30" while the value stays a 24-hour `HH:MM` string (controlled `value` or uncontrolled `defaultValue`, `onValueChange`). The selected option is marked with `--color-action-primary-text-tertiary` and the shared ChosenMark (see the Components intro); hover and keyboard focus use `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`; rows are `--radius-200`. Listbox keyboard pattern (arrows, Home/End, Enter, Escape) with `aria-expanded`, `aria-activedescendant` and `aria-haspopup="listbox"` wiring. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. The trigger carries `role="combobox"` (a select-only combobox, Dropdown's pattern), which is what makes its `aria-activedescendant` valid while the list is open. In the 12-hour format the period words come from `periodLabels` (`am` "AM", `pm` "PM").

### EventCalendar

**`ds-event-calendar`** — Month-at-a-glance schedule: a full month grid with event pills on their days, an overflow row once a day is full, and prev/next month navigation. DatePicker selects a date; this one shows a schedule. The container is `--color-bg-container-primary` at `--radius-300` with a hairline border and `--padding-400`; the header pairs the month name (`--font-title-body-*`) with two `--radius-pill` passive nav buttons and an optional `actions` slot (a "New event" Button). Day cells are `--color-calendar-cell-bg` at `--radius-200` in a 7-column grid with `grid-auto-rows: 1fr`, so each week row shares the height of its fullest day instead of fixing a cell height; outside-month cells fade to the transparent container fill. Today's day number takes the inverse chip (`--color-bg-page-inverse` / `--color-text-on-inverse`) — the filled version of DatePicker's today ring, because a ring disappears at calendar density. The `selectedDate` day takes the action chip instead (`--color-action-primary-bg` / `--color-action-primary-text`, DatePicker's selected convention — the selected item of a mutually exclusive set), winning over the today chip when both land on one day. The month heading comes from `monthLabel`, the arrows from `previousLabel`/`nextLabel`, and the overflow row's name from `overflowLabel`.

Event pills are `--color-calendar-event-bg` chips at `--radius-100`: an accent dot, a truncating `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` title (`.ds-event-calendar__event-title` — a host squeezing the calendar into a miniature column may hide it, reducing pills to their dots; the titles survive as the pills' accessible names), and a `tabular-nums` time that hides under 768px so titles keep at least a few characters. The dot's colour routes through `--ds-event-accent` and resolves to the `--color-core-accent-*` roles (`coral`/`violet`/`cobalt`/`amber`/`gold`/`mint`; default neutral via `--color-status-neutral-border`) — never the action colour, which stays reserved for actions. Within a day, untimed events sort first, then by time text. Days past `maxEventsPerDay` (default 3) collapse into a "+N more" row. Pills, day numbers, and the overflow row render as buttons only when their callback (`onEventClick`, `onDateClick`) is provided — otherwise they are inert spans, so a read-only calendar contains no fake affordances. With `onDateClick` set, the whole cell is a pointer target too (`cursor: pointer`, hover to `--color-calendar-cell-bg-hover`), while the day-number button stays the accessible control; clicks landing on a button inside the cell defer to that button. Cell and pill fills come from the calendar-surfaces roles: the cell keeps its hover while the pointer is on a pill inside it, and the pill separates through its own hover (`--color-calendar-event-bg-hover`, the resting cell tint — one step off the hovered cell, darker than it in dark and lighter in light) plus a hover-only `--color-calendar-event-border` ring; resting pills carry a transparent border, so the ring costs no layout shift. The "+N more" overflow row hovers onto the same `--color-calendar-event-bg-hover` step. Months are `YYYY-MM` strings on the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`month`/`defaultMonth`/`onMonthChange`); unlike DatePicker's day grid, it is deliberately not `role="grid"`: it has no 2D keyboard model, so a labelled group of buttons is the honest structure. The weekday row comes from `weekdayLabels` and the visible "+N more" from `moreLabel`.

### Dropdown

**`ds-dropdown`** — Select-style form field. Closed state matches Input anatomy (label, `--radius-300`, input border/bg tokens, helper/error text); open state reveals a `--radius-300` listbox on `--color-bg-page-primary` elevated with `--shadow-floating`, max-height 240px with scroll, placed by the shared anchored-position behaviour (flipped above when there is no room below, capped to the room it has). The list sizes to its longest option, never narrower than the trigger and capped inside a phone's width, and an option never wraps. Open state follows the pair convention (`open`/`defaultOpen`/`onOpenChange`). Options support disabled entries, grouped sections (`groups`) with headings and separators, an optional per-option `icon` — a Material Symbol at `--icon-size-500` in `--color-icon-primary`, drawn before the label in the option row and on the closed trigger while selected (inline in the value so its single-line ellipsis holds) — and an optional per-option `font` — a CSS stack the option's label renders in (routed through the `--ds-dropdown-font` custom property, runtime data rather than theme), so a typeface picker previews each face in the row and in the closed trigger. The selected option is marked with `--color-action-primary-text-tertiary` and the shared ChosenMark (see the Components intro); hover uses `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`; rows are `--radius-200`. Listbox keyboard pattern (arrows, Home/End, Escape, Enter) with `aria-expanded`/`aria-activedescendant` wiring. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Width is deliberately `100%` — a form field fills its field column; when a Dropdown is toolbar furniture instead (a date-range select in a panel header, a table filter), the consumer caps it with a fixed width via `className`, which is the sanctioned sizing hook. For action menus (not form values) use DropdownMenu. The selection follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`). A consumer's `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby` reach the combobox trigger, and a passed description is joined to the helper's.

### Combobox

**`ds-combobox`** — Filterable select: a text field that narrows a listbox as the user types. Control matches Input anatomy (label, `--radius-300`, input border/bg tokens, helper/error text) with a leading 24px `search` icon and a trailing chevron that rotates when open; the menu is a `--radius-300` surface on `--color-bg-page-primary` elevated with `--shadow-floating`, max-height 280px with scroll. Options carry an optional `description` second line (`--font-paragraph-sm-*` tertiary) and support disabled entries plus grouped sections. `multiple` renders selections as `--radius-pill` chips in `--color-bg-container-primary` inside the control, each with a remove button; Backspace on an empty query pops the last chip. `clearable` adds a clear button, `loading` swaps the list for a status row, and `emptyMessage` covers the no-match case. Async callers pair `onSearchChange` with `manualFiltering` to filter upstream. Full combobox keyboard pattern (arrows, Enter, Escape, Tab) with `aria-expanded`/`aria-activedescendant` wiring. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Use Dropdown when the option list is short and static; Combobox is for long, searchable, or server-backed lists. The selection follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`), a string in single mode and an array when `multiple`; the chip remove buttons and the clear button are named by `removeLabel` ("Remove <label>") and `clearLabel` ("Clear selection"). The loading status text is `loadingMessage`. `name` submits the selection, never the search text, through hidden inputs (one per selected value in `multiple` mode, none while disabled). A consumer's `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby` reach the text input, and a passed description is joined to the helper's rather than replacing it.

### RichDropdown

**`ds-rich-dropdown`** — Dropdown's rich sibling: a select where every option is a self-portrait. The shared cell pairs a 24px swatch dot of the option's key colour (Swatch's geometry — hairline `--color-bg-container-border` inset so light colours stay visible; `--radius-pill` by default, with a per-option `swatchRadius` override so a sharp-cornered look squares its own dot) with the option name set in its own heading face and a detail line set in its own body face. The per-option fonts, colour and swatch radius are runtime data, not theme, so they reach the stylesheet through custom properties (`--ds-rich-dropdown-heading-font`, `--ds-rich-dropdown-body-font`, `--ds-rich-dropdown-color`, `--ds-rich-dropdown-swatch-radius`), falling back to `--font-family-heading` / `--font-family-body` when an option carries no faces. The name takes `--font-title-body-*`, the detail `--font-paragraph-sm-*` in `--color-text-tertiary`. The closed trigger renders the selected option with the same cell, so the current choice previews itself too. Everything else is Dropdown's anatomy: Field composition, `--radius-300` trigger on the input tokens, a `--shadow-floating` listbox on `--color-bg-page-primary` (max-height 320px — the cells run taller than Dropdown's plain rows), selected name in `--color-action-primary-text-tertiary` with the shared ChosenMark, hover/focus rows on `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`, and the full listbox keyboard pattern with `aria-expanded`/`aria-activedescendant` wiring. Controlled (`value` + `onValueChange`) or uncontrolled (`defaultValue`). The dividing line with Dropdown: when the label alone is enough, use Dropdown — its per-option `font` already covers a typeface previewing itself; RichDropdown is for choices whose whole look is the point — theme presets, brand kits, style packages. A consumer's `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby` reach the combobox trigger, and a passed description is joined to the helper's.

### FileInput

**`ds-file-input`** — Click-or-drop upload zone plus a list of chosen files. The dropzone is a dashed `--border-025` `--color-input-border-primary` rectangle at `--radius-300` with a centred 24px `upload_file` icon and instruction copy; hovering moves the border to `--color-input-border-hover`, and an active drag fills `--color-bg-container-primary` with the selected border. The real `<input type="file">` stays in the DOM (visually hidden) for form semantics, with the zone exposed as `role="button"` and Enter/Space activation. The file list is fully controlled — each row is a `--radius-300` hairline container with a `description` icon, name, human-readable size, an optional 4px progress track filled in `--color-core-accent-mint` (ProgressBar's fill), and a 32px ghost remove button. Per-file `error` swaps the row to the error tokens with an `error` icon and message in `--color-status-error-text`. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Each file's progress and remove controls are named by `progressLabel` and `removeLabel`. The accept hint is `acceptHint(accept)`.

### NumberInput

**`ds-numberinput`** — Numeric field composed in `Field`, with the native spinners hidden and replaced by decrement/increment stepper buttons (Material Symbols `remove` / `add` at `--icon-size-500`) flanking a centred `<input type="number">`. The shell carries the input anatomy: `--radius-300` (12px), `--color-input-bg-primary` fill, `--color-input-border-primary` hairline, hover `--color-input-border-hover`, `:focus-within` `--color-input-border-selected`. Steppers clamp to `min`/`max` and disable at the bounds; a typed out-of-range value clamps into range on blur; stepping from an empty field starts at 0 and clamps into range. Works controlled (`value`, `number | ''`) or uncontrolled (`defaultValue`); `onValueChange` receives `number | null` (null when empty) and fires on typing alongside the native `onChange`, and alone on stepper clicks, where no native change event exists. Stepper hover uses `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`. Sizes: `default`, `compact`. Error: `--color-status-error-border` on the shell. Disabled: the input disabled token set on the shell, `cursor: not-allowed`, steppers at `opacity: 0.4`. The steppers are named by `decreaseLabel`/`increaseLabel`.

### TagInput

**`ds-taginput`** — Multi-value text input holding committed entries as removable tags. The shell (`ds-taginput__shell`) is a focus-within box matching Input anatomy: `--radius-300`, `--color-input-bg-primary` fill, `--color-input-border-primary` hairline, hover `--color-input-border-hover`, focus-within `--color-input-border-selected`, error `--color-status-error-border` on border and helper text. Tags are `--radius-pill` pills in `--color-bg-container-secondary` with `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` labels and a trailing remove button (Material Symbols `close` at `--icon-size-500`, accessible name "Remove {tag}"). Enter or comma commits the trimmed draft as a tag (no duplicates, no empty entries); Backspace on an empty draft removes the last tag; `maxTags` stops new entries at the limit. Controlled via `values` plus `onValuesChange`, uncontrolled via `defaultValues`; the native `onChange` fires for draft edits. Composes Field for the label, helper and error wiring. Sizes: `default`, `compact` (draft text drops to `--font-paragraph-sm-*`). Disabled: `opacity: 0.4`, `cursor: not-allowed`, tags not removable. Use Combobox when the values come from a known option list; TagInput is for free-text entries. Each remove button is named by `removeLabel(tag)`.

### PinInput

**`ds-pininput`** — Segmented one-time-code input composed in `Field`: a row of one-character cells (`--gap-200` between them) below the standard label, with helper/error text underneath. Each cell is a 44×52px `<input maxLength={1}>` at `--radius-300` on `--color-input-bg-primary` with a `--color-input-border-primary` hairline, centred `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` text, and the input hover/focus borders (`--color-input-border-hover` / `--color-input-border-selected`); keyboard focus adds the 2px `--color-action-primary-bg` ring. Typing fills a cell and advances focus, Backspace clears and retreats, arrows move, and paste distributes a full code from cell 0. `format` is `numeric` (default, rejects non-digits, `inputMode="numeric"`) or `alphanumeric`; `mask` renders password cells; `length` defaults to 6. `onValueChange` emits the joined code, `onComplete` fires once when every cell fills; the first cell carries `autoComplete="one-time-code"` for OTP autofill. The cells wrap in a `role="group"` labelled by Field's label, and every cell has an accessible name ("Digit 1 of 6"). Error: `--color-status-error-border` on cell borders. Disabled: cells at `opacity: 0.4`, `cursor: not-allowed`, input disabled tokens. Each cell is named by `cellLabel(position, count)`.

### FilterBar
