**`ds-filter-bar`** — A wrapping row of filter chips at `--gap-200` for narrowing a collection. Each chip borrows Chip's pill language (`--radius-pill`, `--color-bg-page-primary` on a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` in `--color-text-secondary`); a filter with anything active flips to the action selected fill (`--color-action-primary-bg` / `--color-action-primary-text`, matching Chip's selected convention) and grows a trailing clear button. The chip summarises its state: bare label, "Label: Option" for one choice, "Label: n" for more. The popover is a real listbox — `--color-bg-container-primary` at `--radius-300` under `--shadow-floating`, options at `--font-paragraph-sm-*`, a multi-select row led by the checkbox indicator (`CheckboxIndicator`, compact) and a single-select row marked by the shared ChosenMark (see the Components intro) — driven by `aria-activedescendant` so focus never leaves the chip: arrows move, Enter/Space toggle, Escape closes, single-select filters (`multiple: false`) close on choice. State is one `Record<filterId, string[]>`, controlled (`values` + `onValuesChange`) or uncontrolled (`defaultValues`); a "Clear all" text button appears once anything is active. `compact` tightens to Chip's compact paddings. Each filter's clear control is named by `clearFilterLabel`.

### Rating

**`ds-rating`** — Star-scale rating control. A radio group of Material Symbol steps (`icon` defaults to `star`, `max` to 5): filled steps take `--color-core-accent-gold` with the icon font's `FILL` axis at 1, empty steps sit in `--color-icon-secondary`. Hover previews the would-be selection (steps scale to 1.1 at `--motion-duration-fast`); selection is controlled (`value`) or uncontrolled (`defaultValue`) with `onValueChange`, and `allowClear` lets re-selecting the current step clear back to 0. Keyboard follows the radio-group pattern: the checked step holds the roving tab stop, arrows move and select, Home/End jump to the ends. `readOnly` renders the same row as `role="img"` with a spoken "N out of M" label; `disabled` dims to the standard 0.4. Sizes: `default` (`--icon-size-600` steps), `compact` (`--icon-size-500`). The spoken value is `valueLabel(value, max)`, "N out of M" by default, read after `label` and a colon.

### Toast

**`ds-toast`** — Ephemeral notification via `ToastProvider` + `useToast()` hook. Rendered via React portal into `document.body`. Default duration: 5000ms. Pauses on hover/focus. Progress bar animates across bottom. Position: configurable (`top-right`, `bottom-right`, etc.), defaults to `bottom-right`. Max stack: 5 toasts. Five variants share the same `--color-status-*` token mapping as Badge/Alert. The container sits on `--layer-toast`, above every modal (Elevation & Depth → Stacking owns the ladder), flush to its corner; the layer and the corner are both consumer hooks. A host that keeps its own fixed chrome in that corner sets `--ds-toast-inset-top/bottom/left/right` so the queue stacks clear of it, and raises `--ds-toast-z` above its own overlays: toasts are transient feedback and must be seen, so they outrank persistent chrome. The website does exactly this for its chat launcher (`globals.css`), and moves the queue left of the panel while it is docked on the right; on the left dock only the bottom inset changes, since nothing stands in the queue's way. The standalone Toast renders its dismiss button only when given `onDismiss`; the region and dismiss names are `regionLabel` and `dismissLabel` on ToastProvider.

Default icons (Material Symbols Rounded): `info`, `check_circle`, `warning`, `error`, `info` for each variant. Override via `icon` prop. Dismissal plays the exit animation (`--motion-duration-base`), the unmount following on `MOTION_EXIT_SYNC_MS`.

### NotificationCenter

**`ds-notification-center`** — The persistent inbox for everything that happened while the user was away: Toast interrupts, this accumulates. A `--color-bg-container-primary` panel at `--radius-300` with a hairline border, so it works dropped from a bell icon or inline on a page. Header: `--font-title-body-*` title with a tertiary "N unread" line beside it (a polite live region), and a mark-all-read text control on the right in `--color-action-primary-text-tertiary` (an action, so it may take the action colour) that only renders when `onMarkAllRead` is provided. Optional filter tabs sit under the header — `--radius-pill` pills with `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` labels and `tabular-nums` counts, active pill on `--color-bg-container-tertiary`, `role="tablist"` with arrow-key movement, each tab wired to the list below via `aria-controls` — and the list, a `role="tabpanel"` labelled by the active tab, scrolls beneath a `--color-divider` rule. Filtering is the consumer's job: swap the children when the tab changes. No children renders a built-in compact EmptyState (overridable via `emptyState`). `tabsLabel` names the filter tablist and `unreadCountLabel` writes the announced unread line; NotificationItem's visually hidden "Unread" is `unreadLabel`. The active filter tab follows the value pair, `value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`, like Tabs and SegmentedControl; `activeTab`, `defaultTab` and `onTabChange` remain as deprecated aliases, and the new names win when both are set. The built-in empty state's copy is `emptyStateLabels`, merged over "Nothing new" and "Notifications will collect here as they arrive."

**`ds-notification-item`** — One row of the inbox, exported alongside: a leading `media` slot (Material Symbol name or an element such as an Avatar, `--icon-size-600`) rendered as a fixed column the width of its largest medium — an sm Avatar, the `--icon-size-800` rung — with smaller media centred in it, so every row's text shares one left edge whether its medium is an avatar or a bare icon; a title row with right-aligned time (`--font-paragraph-sm-*` tertiary), supporting copy in `--color-text-secondary`, and an `actions` row for compact Buttons. `unread` emphasises the title to `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` and adds an 8px dot filled with `--color-status-info-border` at full border weight, Meter's small-mark reasoning — the weight change alone is too quiet to scan for, so the dot is the signal and has to register at a glance — informational, so it takes the info status colour rather than the action colour — with visually hidden "Unread" text for screen readers. Rows separate with `--color-divider` hairlines; renders a semantic `<article>`.

### ProgressBar

**`ds-progress-bar`** — Determinate progress as a horizontal `--radius-pill` track in `--color-bg-container-tertiary` with a `--color-core-accent-mint` fill scaled to the clamped 0–100 value. Optional percentage label (`showLabel`) in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*`. `role="progressbar"` with `aria-valuenow`/`-valuemin`/`-valuemax` and an `ariaLabel` describing what is loading. Sizes: `default`, `compact` (bar height).

### Meter

**`ds-meter`** — Level indicator for a known quantity: storage used, tokens spent, password strength, battery left. The counterpart to ProgressBar — same `--radius-pill` track in `--color-bg-container-tertiary`, but the number means "how full", not "how far along", and the fill is coloured by the five status roles so the level carries its own judgement. The fill uses the full-strength `--color-status-*-border` weight, not the tinted `-bg` — a tint would vanish in an 8px ribbon. Optional header row: `label` in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` on the left, a `showValue` readout in tabular figures on the right (`valueText` replaces the default percentage and is spoken via `aria-valuetext` — "128k of 200k tokens"). Custom `min`/`max` ranges; the fill animates between levels at `--motion-duration-base` and grows in from empty on mount (`--motion-duration-slower` `--motion-ease-emphasized`, collapsed by the reduced-motion token guard). `role="meter"` with the value attributes, named by `label` or `aria-label`. Sizes: `default` (8px track), `compact` (4px). Presentational — no `'use client'`, renders from a Server Component.

### Skeleton

**`ds-skeleton`** — Loading placeholder with a pulsing fill stepping between `--color-bg-container-primary` and `--color-bg-container-secondary`. Three shapes: `text` (`--radius-200` line, multi-line via `lines` — the last line renders at 75% width), `circular` (`--radius-pill`), and `rectangular`. `width`/`height` accept any CSS length. Announced as `role="status"` with `aria-label="Loading"` and `aria-busy`.

### Spinner

**`ds-spinner`** — Indeterminate loading indicator: an SVG circle with a `--color-bg-container-tertiary` track and a rotating arc in `--color-text-primary` (`tone="default"`) or `--color-text-secondary` (`tone="neutral"`); `tone="inherit"` draws both circles in `currentColor` (track at 0.25 opacity) so the spinner matches the surrounding control — it's what Button and CircularButton use for their `loading` state. Sizes `sm`/`md`/`lg` (16/24/36px). The single-axis `variant` (`primary`/`neutral`/`inherit`) is the deprecated spelling of `tone`. `role="status"` with a configurable `label`. Use Spinner for indeterminate waits and ProgressBar when the completion fraction is known.

### StatusDot

**`ds-status-dot`** — The bare status mark: a `--radius-pill` dot in one of the five status roles beside an optional `--font-paragraph-sm-*` label in `--color-text-secondary`. The dot colours through the plain-surface `--color-status-*-icon` steps (the deep inks in light, the mid-ramp hues in dark), never the text tints — it sits on bare containers, where the tinted-text steps wash out. Sizes ride the icon scale at half-diameter (`sm`/`md`/`lg` = half of `--icon-size-500/600/800` — 10/12/16px), so the dot can never drift from the glyphs it sits beside; `xs` is the one step off it, the 8px list-row mark on `--gap-200` (the gap scale because no icon token is that small), which ThreadPanel and ThreadTabs compose. `outline` draws the dot hollow — the role's colour as an inset ring (`--border-025` at `xs`/`sm`, `--border-050` at `md`/`lg`) around an empty centre — for a settled or empty state that still holds its seat beside filled dots. `pulse` radiates a ring of the dot's own colour (a `::after` in the role's colour, swelling to 2.2× and fading on `--motion-duration-loop-shimmer`) for live states — recording, online now, deploy in flight; under reduced motion the ring parks invisible rather than strobing. Where Badge is a text label on a tinted fill, StatusDot is the mark alone — table rows, avatars, nav items. Root is `role="status"`; the dot itself is `aria-hidden`, so a bare dot needs its meaning carried by a row label or a passed-through `aria-label`; `decorative` drops the role and hides the whole mark, for a dot repeated down a list whose host announces the state in the row's own text. Together the three give a list one reading — **fill says whether a row wants the reader, colour says what it wants, motion says it is live** — which is the thread status convention ThreadPanel's spec owns. Purely presentational — no `'use client'`.

### EmptyState

**`ds-empty-state`** — Placeholder for a list, table, search, or dashboard with nothing to show. Centred stack: a 48px `--radius-pill` `--color-bg-container-primary` disc holding a 24px Material Symbol, a `--font-title-body-*` headline in `--color-text-primary`, supporting copy in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` tertiary capped at 420px, and an action slot that wraps consumer-provided Buttons. `variant="bordered"` adds a dashed `--color-bg-container-border` container at `--radius-300` — use it inside a card, table, or panel; `plain` sits directly on the page. `size="compact"` drops to a 36px icon disc, `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` headline, and `--padding-500`, for dense sidebars. The `icon` prop takes a Material Symbol name or an arbitrary element for illustrations. Write the description as the next action, not just a statement of absence.

### Card

**`ds-card`** — Table-of-contents navigation tile. Two parts: `.ds-card__preview` (content/component preview area) + `.ds-card__title` (h3 label below). Used in component/foundations index grids. When `interactive`, gains `role="button"` and keyboard support. Background: `--color-bg-container-primary-semi`; radius: `--radius-600` (24px). Interactive and case-study variants lift on hover with `0 8px 24px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)` — the system's one sanctioned container shadow (see Do's and Don'ts). The case-study variant's cover slot takes either an image (`coverSrc`) or drawn content (`cover` — an SVG, a chart, a live preview); both fill the same fixed-ratio box, so a grid keeps one cover shape whichever kind each card was given. The cover is inset, not full-bleed: `--padding-300` (12px) of card surface surrounds a `--radius-300` (12px) cover, keeping the inner corners concentric with the card's 24px shell — the same geometry rule the Composer follows.

### EntityCard

**`ds-entity-card`** — Compact icon-or-image + label card. Used specifically on Icons and Logos pages in the Foundations section. Takes a Material Symbol name (`icon`) or image path (`imageSrc`). Centered layout with label beneath. Radius: `--radius-600` (24px), matching Card. Not interactive — display-only.

### SelectionCard

**`ds-selection-card`** — Card-based form control for selecting from a list. Three modes: `radio` (single select), `checkbox` (multi-select), `toggle` (per-card on/off). Each card renders the option label, optional description, and the real control's own indicator at its end (`RadioIndicator`, `CheckboxIndicator` or `SwitchIndicator`, so the mark is exactly the one the standalone control draws; the card carries `ds-control-host`, so hovering it lights the indicator). The card is a form control and wears Input's surface: `--color-input-bg-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-input-border-primary` hairline at `--radius-300`, `--color-input-border-hover` on hover. Full keyboard navigation and ARIA roles. Selected state: the `--color-input-border-selected` border, the way an active field is marked; the fill stays, so a picked card does not change weight. Use when options need more space or explanation than a standard radio/checkbox group. The selection follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`), a string in `radio` mode and an array otherwise.

**When to use which card:**
- `Card` → navigational tile in a documentation index grid
- `EntityCard` → icon or logo display in a foundations catalog
- `SelectionCard` → form input when options need card-style layout

### CardStack

**`ds-card-stack`** — A deck of cards showing one at a time, the rest peeking out beneath it; the stack is the pattern, not the cards — any children work, Card and EntityCard are the intended fillings, and every card in a deck should share one size. Rendering is a CSS grid with every item in the same cell; each item's place in the deck is a single transform driven by two custom properties — `--ds-card-stack-peek` (the per-card offset, default `--gap-400`) times the item's `--ds-card-stack-pos`, with `scale(1 - 0.04 × pos)` from a bottom-centre origin so buried cards narrow while their bottom edges still peek below. The container pads its own bottom by `peek × --ds-card-stack-peek` so the deck occupies its true height. Flipping forward plays a lift-and-settle: the top card animates up and away (`translateY(-18%)`, a 3° tilt, fading — percentages of the card itself, so the choreography scales with whatever the deck holds) over `--motion-duration-deliberate` `--motion-ease-emphasized`, and the index commits on `animationend`, never a timer, so reduced motion (which collapses the duration via the token guard) commits immediately. Flipping back commits first and drops the incoming card in from above on `--motion-ease-entrance`. Position moves between slots transition on the same duration. Flip by clicking the top card (clicks landing on a link or control inside the card keep their meaning — `advanceOnClick` turns the rest off), or with the arrow keys when the stack has focus. `index`/`defaultIndex`/`onIndexChange` follow the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention; `loop` (default on) wraps the deck. The root is a focusable `role="group"` with `aria-roledescription="card stack"`; non-top cards are `inert` and `aria-hidden` so a buried card's controls can never take focus, and a visually hidden `role="status"` announces "Card X of N" as the deck turns. The position announcement is `positionLabel(position, count)`.

### Widget

**`ds-widget`** — A titled tile for a dashboard or a home screen, with the three pieces that fill and arrange it. The tile is a `<section>` named by its `title` (overridable with `aria-label`): the heading in `--font-title-body-*` primary (an `h3` by default, `titleAs` moves it in the outline) with an optional `count` trailing it in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` tertiary, an optional `action` slot opposite, then the body. The surface is the translucent container (`--color-bg-container-primary-semi`) over a 24px backdrop blur behind a `--border-025` hairline in `--color-bg-container-border`, at `--radius-600` with `--padding-500` — the glass recipe's quiet form, so a board of tiles sits over an ambient background without going flat, and reads as a bordered card on a plain page. No shadow: depth stays token-owned. **`ds-widget-row`** (WidgetRow) is one line of a tile: a `leading` mark (a StatusDot, PixelAvatar, Avatar, Spinner or icon, sized on `--icon-size-600`), a `title` in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` over an optional `description` in `--font-caption-*` tertiary (both ellipsised), and a trailing `meta` in the caption face with tabular numerals. It renders as an `<a>` with `href`, a `<button>` with `onClick`, and a plain `<div>` otherwise, so a list offers a target only where there is somewhere to go; an interactive row's hover fill (`--color-action-passive-bg-hover`, `--radius-300`) bleeds into the tile's padding so the whole line reads as one target. **`ds-widget-group`** (WidgetGroup) splits rows into labelled runs (`role="group"`, the label in the caption face). **`ds-widget-grid`** (WidgetGrid) is the board: CSS columns, so tiles of different heights pack with no holes beside the tall ones, capped at `columns` (1 to 3, default 2) and folding to fewer as the board narrows — the column floor is stated on the icon scale rather than as a breakpoint. All four are presentational and server-renderable. Where Panel is the bare surface a dashboard composes on, Widget is the finished tile: reach for Panel when the content brings its own header.

### Panel

**`ds-panel`** — The plain dashboard surface: `--color-bg-container-primary` fill at `--radius-600`, `--padding-500` inside, a `--gap-400` column, no border and no shadow. Card cannot play this role — it requires a title and carries its own navigational look — so Panel is what a dashboard's regions build on, with charts dropped in `bare` and LegendTile rows beneath them. `padding="compact"` steps to `--padding-400`, `"none"` removes it for full-bleed content. Presentational and server-renderable.

### BentoTile

**`ds-bento-tile`** — A feature tile for a bento grid: a heading over a content area, on one of three backgrounds, that can open into a full overlay. The tile is an `<article>` at `--radius-400` behind a `--border-025` hairline in `--color-bg-container-border`; the heading (`headingLevel`, an `h3` by default, never an `h1`, since a tile is never the page's top heading) sets in the `--font-heading-2-*` face at the sub-display weight, balanced and capped at 22ch (a deliberate exception to one weight per tier: a feature tile's title is display copy at a heading's size, so it takes the display tier's weight), with an optional `description` in `--font-paragraph-*` secondary under it.

**The content area is free and the guidelines hold it.** `children` can be anything; the tile owns only where it sits. Content lays out in flow under the heading, inside a safe inset (`--ds-bento-tile-inset`, `--padding-500` by default), so it can never land under the heading or the expand control. `align` (`start`, `center` the default, `end`) places it in the space, using auto margins so a box taller than the space starts at the top and overflows the foot rather than the head. Whatever does not fit is clipped at the content area and fades out over `--ds-bento-tile-fade` (`--gap-1000`) at the edge it overflows, measured live, so a hard cut never shows. The content area is a size container named `ds-bento-tile`: content answers the tile's width with `@container ds-bento-tile`, not the viewport's, because a tile in a three-column grid is as narrow as a phone. The rule for content is that every component inside keeps its natural width: a scene may fade at the tile's edge, but nothing inside it truncates, so content drops a part (a side stat, a column) at narrow widths rather than squeezing it. A surface placed inside a tile steps along the container ramp from the tile's own: on a solid tile a Panel is the tile's surface again and disappears, so a nested card wants `--color-bg-container-secondary` or no fill at all. A tile fills its grid cell (`height: 100%`); a grid whose rows are `minmax(row, auto)` lets a row grow to fit its tallest tile, and a fixed row shows the fade.

**Backgrounds.** `shader` (the default) is the container surface with a shader pair over it; `solid` is the surface alone; `transparent` is no surface, the border and the content. A shader pair is two halves that always come together: a **wash**, a ShaderField of three soft light sources pooled in the bottom-left corner under a radial mask, and a **texture**, a BentoTileArt pattern laid over it in the same corner, so light and pattern read as one surface. Six pairs exist and **pair N is ambient colour N with texture N**: the wash is led by `--color-ambient-N` with its two neighbours under it, and the texture is drawn in the same colour. Tiles rotate through the six by position among their siblings, so a grid of shader tiles comes out told apart with no configuration, and `highlight` (1 to 6) pins a pair; a pin outranks the rotation. The pair samples the ambient tokens rather than the core accents because a theme can tune the ambient slots toward its key colour without moving the chart palette, so the pair reads as the theme. Both halves share one strength, `--ds-bento-tile-shader-opacity` (half by default), because at full strength the pair competes with the content over it. Before the field resolves, and wherever WebGL is missing, a gradient in the same three colours stands in under it.

**Expanding.** Passing `expandedContent` makes the tile a control: the whole tile opens a modal overlay, and a compact CircularButton in the top-right corner (`open_in_full`) is its accessible handle, with `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded`. The content area goes `inert` while the tile is expandable, since a control inside a control cannot be reached. Hovered or with its handle focused, the tile's border takes `--color-action-primary-bg-hover`, doubled to two hairlines by an inside outline so nothing moves, and the handle steps up to the primary variant: the tile is an action, so it signals with the action colour and nothing decorative. The overlay is a `role="dialog"` panel named by the tile's title, in the container surface at `--radius-400` with `--shadow-modal`, over a frosted backdrop of `--color-bg-page-primary` at 55% with a 14px blur; it sits on `--layer-modal`, is capped at `--ds-bento-tile-panel-width`, and below 600px fills the viewport. It joins the shared behavior layer: the dismissal stack (Escape), the focus scope (focus to the close control, page inert, focus restored to the handle), and the counted scroll lock in its `cancel-events` strategy, which keeps the page scrollbar rendered so the page cannot shift sideways under the morph. Opening is a shared-element morph through the View Transitions API where the browser has it and the visitor allows motion: tile and panel take one transition name in turn, the root never crossfades (its rules are scoped by a class on the root for the length of the tile's own morph), and the backdrop stays outside the transition because a snapshot cannot sample a blur. The overlay's body rises in after the box has travelled. Controlled with `open` and `onOpenChange`, or uncontrolled with `defaultOpen`. The handle is named by `expandLabel` ("Open" and the title by default, or "Open" alone when the title is not a string), and the overlay's close control by `closeLabel` ("Close").

### BentoTileArt

**`ds-bento-tile-art`** — The texture half of a BentoTile's shader pair, exported for a surface that wants it outside a tile. Six patterns in one vocabulary: `dots`, `lines`, `rings`, `grid`, `waves` and `rays`, in the order tiles rotate through them. Every pattern is drawn in 1px marks of its tile's highlight colour at 45% before the pair's shared opacity (on its own, outside a tile, there is no pair and no wash under it, so the marks take `--color-text-tertiary`, which reads on either theme's surface), eases in and out around the bottom-left corner (nothing at the corner itself, where rings and rays converge, swelling in and fading toward the middle, with the bottom and left edges eased so no mark meets the border), and drifts slowly along its own repeat; rings and rays hold still. Only the geometry differs. Rays are stroked lines with a non-scaling stroke rather than a conic gradient, whose marks widen with distance from the corner. Pattern sizes are drawing geometry in px; the drift is decorative and stands still under reduced motion. Inside a tile the pattern follows the tile's pair; `pattern` pins one directly. Pure CSS, presentational and server-renderable, hidden from assistive technology.

### SplitPane

**`ds-split-pane`** — Two resizable regions with a draggable divider: sidebar and canvas, list and detail, editor and preview. The split is a percentage of the first pane riding a `--ds-split-pane-split` custom property, so it survives container resizes; panes clip their content (`overflow: hidden`, `min-width/height: 0`) rather than growing the page, and a region that should scroll brings its own focusable scroll container (a bare scrollable pane would fail axe's scrollable-region-focusable rule, and a pane that is always a tab stop would be worse). The divider is a `--gap-200` hit area (transparent, tinting to `--color-bg-container-secondary` on hover and drag) holding a `--radius-pill` grip bar in `--color-bg-container-border` that darkens to `--color-icon-secondary` when engaged; both transition at `--motion-duration-fast`. It is a real `role="separator"`: focusable, `aria-valuenow` reporting the percentage, arrow keys nudging by 2 (Shift for 10), Home/End jumping to `minSplit`/`maxSplit` (default 10–90), and the focus ring is the standard `--color-action-primary-bg` outline. Pointer drags use capture so a fast drag cannot escape the handle, and `user-select` is suppressed while dragging. Controlled (`split` + `onSplitChange`) or uncontrolled (`defaultSplit`); `direction="vertical"` stacks the panes.

### AppLayout / AppSidebar

**`ds-app-layout`** — Full-page shell: an `AppSidebar` rail beside a `<main>` area that centres the page content, with no header of its own (a page that needs one renders it inside the content). The layout keeps the rail's expanded state, seeded from `defaultExpanded`, and offsets the content to the rail's width. Pins `data-theme="dark"` by default (the historical behaviour); `theme="inherit"` drops the pin so the layout follows the surrounding theme. **`ds-app-sidebar`** — The side navigation component. Sidebar background: `--color-bg-page-primary`. Nav pills hold one inset in both states (12px margin + 8px padding), which makes the collapsed hover pill a circle clear of the 64px rail's edges and keeps every icon stationary through the expand/collapse. The transition choreography is deliberate: inner layout never changes between states — labels keep natural width with `min-width: 0` so they truncate under the sweeping rail, gaps are constant, and only the rail's clipping width, explicit widths (toggle, profile-more), and opacity fades animate; `width: 0 ↔ auto` and gap snaps read as a bounce and are never reintroduced. `floating` renders the rail as a glass card inset from the viewport edges (`--color-bg-glass` + 24px backdrop blur, `--radius-600`, `--shadow-floating`, inset via `--ds-sidebar-float-inset`, default 20px). Items take an optional `badge` count pill (hidden while collapsed); `topSlot` (under the logo row) and `footerSlot` (above the profile) host consumer content that fades out while collapsed without changing the rail's layout. The profile row composes `Avatar` at `md`, so a profile with no `avatarUrl` falls back to the person's initials and then to the generic glyph rather than showing the blank disc the hand-rolled box drew before it. An item or sub-item with an `href` renders as a real link through the rail's `linkComponent` or a `LinkProvider`, which is how it routes client-side (its `onClick` still fires too); accordion rows stay buttons. The active row carries `aria-current="page"`. In the tablet band (769-959px) entering the band folds the rail to the icon rail once, through the same path a click takes so controlled hosts hear it — entry-only, so the visitor can re-expand by hand. Below the mobile breakpoint (768px — the Breakpoints section owns the number) the rail leaves the layout and a fixed hamburger trigger opens it as a **modal overlay drawer** on the shared behavior layer (dismissal stack, focus trap, counted scroll lock): the full expanded layout slides in from the left over `--color-scrim`, always expanded — an icon rail inside a modal would be labels withheld for no gain. While the drawer is open the logo row's collapse control becomes its close button — a `close` glyph labelled "Close navigation" — because a modal drawer has no icon rail to collapse to; it is driven by the drawer's own open flag, which can only be true below the breakpoint. The trigger and scrim exist only inside the mobile media query, so the overlay can never activate on wider viewports, and crossing back above the breakpoint while open closes it. `showMobileTrigger={false}` hides the built-in trigger for hosts that render their own in the page chrome.

**Three levels.** Navigation goes three levels deep and never further, in one of two shapes chosen once per sidebar with `subNav`. `accordion` (the default) keeps level 2 as the accordion under a top-level row; a sub-item that has children of its own shows a trailing `chevron_right` at `--icon-size-500` and opens them as level 3 in a `SidebarPanel` fixed beside the rail. `panel` skips the accordion: a top-level row opens its sub-items straight into the panel, whose own accordion carries level 3, and the collapsed rail widens to 80px to name every destination, each icon sitting in a 56 x 32 `--radius-pill` indicator (the hover and selection fill, `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`) with a caption-scale label beneath it. The accordion holding the current page starts open, and so does the panel holding it (`defaultPanelOpen`, or controlled through `panelOpen` + `onPanelOpenChange`); `activeTertiaryKey` names the third-level page. While the panel is showing, the row feeding it carries the selection fill, so the rail shows which section is open, while `aria-current` stays on the real current page. Pressing that row again, or the panel's own collapse control, closes the panel. The panel tracks the rail's width (64, 80 or 280px) on the rail's own slow emphasized glide, sits one z-index step under it with hairlines either side, and slides a `--padding-300` step out from under it at `--motion-duration-slow`; hidden, it stays mounted and `inert` so the slide runs both ways. `floating` makes it a second glass card one `--gap-300` along from the first. On the collapsed rail in accordion mode, a row with children expands the rail and opens its accordion, since the accordion has nowhere else to go. Below the mobile breakpoint the panel never shows; the drawer drills in instead: a row with children replaces the nav with the panel's level as a second screen (a `SidebarPanel` with a back row naming the level above, sliding in from the right at `--motion-duration-slow`), focus moves to the back row, and going back returns focus to the row that drilled in. Opening the drawer lands on the screen that holds the current page. `AppLayout` passes `subNav` and `activeTertiaryKey` through and widens its content offset by the panel's 240px while the panel shows.

**Generated marks.** An AppSidebar item takes `pixelAvatar` to draw a generated PixelAvatar in its icon seat instead of `icon`, for rows that name a thing rather than a place (projects, workspaces, accounts): `true` seeds the character from the label, a string from that value, so a renamed item can keep its character. The sidebar spreads the inks across all of its avatar rows with `distinctPixelInks`, so neighbours differ in colour as well as shape.

### SidebarPanel

**`ds-sidebar-panel`** — The secondary navigation column: one section's pages under a heading, optionally split into labelled runs (`group` on each item) and nested one level with `children`. It stands alone, and it is what `AppSidebar` opens beside the rail for a third level. Its row geometry repeats the sidebar's sub-items so the two columns read as one system: 40px `--radius-pill` rows with a `--padding-300` edge inset and `--padding-200` padding, `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` on hover and on the current page, `--font-paragraph-*` labels (the current page and a collapsed accordion hiding it step to the emphasis weight, so the reader finds their place without opening anything), and the sidebar's count pill for `badge`. The heading is `--font-overline-*` in `--color-text-secondary`, uppercase, and wraps rather than truncates, because a clipped section name is the one thing a reader cannot recover; `onCollapse` adds a 32px `left_panel_close` control centred on its first line. Group labels are `--font-paragraph-sm-*` in `--color-text-tertiary`. Accordion rows toggle with an `expand_more` chevron at `--icon-size-500` rotating 180 degrees, carry `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`, and open through `grid-template-rows: 0fr to 1fr` at `--motion-duration-slow` / `--motion-ease-emphasized`; their children hang from a `--border-025` tree line in `--color-bg-container-border` under the parent label's first letter, and a closed accordion is `inert`. The accordion holding `activeKey` starts open. `onBack` adds a back row above the heading (`chevron_left`, `backLabel`), which is the drill-in screen a drawer shows on small screens. The root is a `<nav>` named by the heading. Width is 240px, overridable through `--ds-sidebar-panel-width`. The collapse button takes `collapseLabel`.

### ShaderField

**`ds-shader-field`** — A WebGL2 canvas summing soft Gaussian light sources into an ambient field of colour. Each source names a semantic colour token, read from the canvas's computed style at runtime, so the field re-themes with `data-theme` and with any scoped override — a consumer's palette reaches it for free. Colours are decoded to linear RGB and blended there, so midpoints keep their saturation; theme crossfade is `--motion-duration-slow`, and the reveal is `--motion-duration-slower` / `--motion-ease-entrance`, matching the page's own entrance.

The component owns the canvas and nothing else: `position: absolute; inset: 0` in whatever positioned ancestor the caller provides, `pointer-events: none` so a full-bleed layer never swallows a click. Placement, clipping and any mask belong to that ancestor — baking one in would make it unusable anywhere but a page background. **The default composition never samples `--color-action-primary-bg`**: a full-viewport decorative field is exactly the use that would dilute the action colour, so the last source takes `--color-core-ui-secondary`.

Eight parameters — `intensity`, `warp`, `scale`, `speed`, `grain`, `streak`, `react`, `crop` — pass as a partial merged over the defaults. `streak` stretches sources along a fixed diagonal, discs at 0 to light streams at 1. `grain` doubles as the dither that kills 8-bit banding, so it is never truly off. `react` is the cursor wake, shipping at 0 — built and dormant, not absent; raising it steps the loop from 30fps to 60fps only while a wake is alive. `crop` decides what a narrow container does to the composition: at 0 — what a CSS blob layer does — the whole field is fitted into it, so a phone shows every source shrunk to phone scale; at 1 the field holds the 1440px scale it was composed at and the viewport crops into it instead. Values between blend the two, keeping enough shrink that colours still bleed together on a small screen. The default sits between the two, which suits a full-viewport background (the site runs the default); set it to 0 wherever seeing the whole composition is the point. A negative source `weight` absorbs light, cutting a shadow that occludes whatever shines behind it. `BLOB_COUNT` (8) sizes the shader's uniform arrays as a compile-time constant; passing fewer sources parks the unused slots off-field.

Two behaviours are the component's rather than the caller's, and both are why it can be dropped in. **It honours reduced motion in JavaScript** — the `tokens-motion.css` guard cannot see a `requestAnimationFrame` loop, so this draws one static frame and never starts the loop (see **Do's and Don'ts**). **And it always reports how it resolved**, via `onStatusChange` and a `data-status` attribute: `pending`, `active`, `unavailable`. No WebGL2, a blocked or lost context, a compile failure, a stalled first frame, and `enabled={false}` all land on `unavailable`, so one fallback covers every failure. `pending` is the state worth handling: paint neither, since swapping a fallback out a frame later reads as two backgrounds in sequence — a 1.5s watchdog bounds the wait so it can never be terminal. It renders no fallback of its own; what to paint instead is a design decision, not a rendering one. The canvas is `aria-hidden`.

### Toolbar

**`ds-toolbar`** — A grouped strip of controls sharing one pill shell: Buttons, CircularButtons, SegmentedControls, split into clusters by `ToolbarSeparator` hairlines (`--border-025` on `--color-divider`, stretched to the control height with `--gap-050` margins). The shell is `--radius-pill` on `--color-bg-container-primary` with a `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, `--gap-100` between controls, and `--padding-150` all round — the padding clears the focus ring's full reach (2px offset + 2px outline), so a focused control's ring seats inside the shell instead of slicing its border. `variant="floating"` is the glass pill for controls hovering over content — `--color-bg-glass`, a 24px frost (a component-local effect length), `--shadow-floating` — deliberately unpositioned: the consumer's layout decides where it floats (the site's StageControlBar is that consumer's shape). `orientation="vertical"` turns the pill into a capsule and rotates the separators. Announces as `role="toolbar"` with a required accessible `label`; arrow keys walk focus between the controls (Home/End jump to the ends) while every control keeps its natural tab stop, and a control that owns its arrow keys (a SegmentedControl) keeps them by preventing default before the event bubbles.

### Nav

**`ds-nav`** — Top navigation bar: a 78px-tall, max-width 1440px flex row with a brand slot (icon + `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` text, `--gap-500`) on the left and a horizontal ButtonGroup plus optional `trailing` content (e.g. the theme ToggleSwitch) on the right at `--gap-1000`. Purely compositional — active states come from the Button configs passed in. The landmark is labelled "Main" by default so it stays distinguishable from any other `<nav>` on the page; a passed `aria-label` overrides it, and other native attributes spread onto the `<nav>`.

### NavList

**`ds-nav-list`** — Vertical navigation link list for drawers and side menus, up to three levels deep. Every row is a real link rendered as a full-width, left-aligned **tertiary Button** (level 1 at the default size, deeper levels compact), so shape, padding, typography, and hover/pressed states have one home in the Button spec and stay identical to every other button. The current page gets `aria-current="page"` plus the tertiary active background (`--color-action-passive-bg-active`) — the same treatment the showcase sidebar uses. A collapsible item adds a separate 40px `--radius-pill` chevron toggle (`expand_more` at `--icon-size-500`, rotating 180° when open) wired with `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls` — tapping the label navigates, tapping the chevron expands. Every row reserves that same 40px height, so leaf rows and toggle rows sit on one vertical rhythm at the list's `--gap-050` spacing. Sublists indent one `--padding-1000` step (then `--gap-500`), take the same `--gap-050` below their parent row, animate open via `grid-template-rows: 0fr → 1fr` at `--motion-duration-slow` / `--motion-ease-emphasized`, and are `inert` while collapsed so their links leave the tab order. Expansion is uncontrolled (`defaultExpandedIds`) or controlled (`expandedIds` + `onExpandedChange`), single-open by default; `collapsible: false` renders a permanent, always-visible group. The branch toggle is named by `expandLabel`/`collapseLabel`, each given the row label.

### BottomNav

**`ds-bottom-nav`** — The tab bar of a mobile app: three to five destinations along the bottom of the screen, icon over label, the selected icon drawn filled (Material Symbols' `FILL` axis, the convention on every platform). `activeKey` marks the current destination with `aria-current="page"`; `onValueChange` reports a press; items with an `href` render as links. The landmark is named "Tabs" by default, distinct from Nav's "Main" so a screen carrying both announces two different navs; `aria-label` overrides it. `search` is a destination set apart from the rest: its glyph defaults to search, and its `icon` repurposes it (an assistant launcher, say). A `badge` is a count or, as `true`, a dot on the icon's top-end corner, with visually hidden text announcing it after the label: `badgeLabel(badge)`, ", 3 new" for a count and ", new" for a dot by default. `fixed` pins the bar to the viewport bottom; the home-indicator safe area is padded in every placement. `platform` picks one of three renderings of the same markup:

- **`default`**, the Rift bar and fully on-token: a 64px bar on `--color-bg-page-primary` under a `--border-025` hairline, caption labels in `--color-text-secondary`, and the selection in `--color-action-primary-bg`, since the current tab is the selected item of a mutually exclusive set.
- **`ios`**, the iOS 26 Liquid Glass capsule: a 62pt glass pill floating 21pt in from the screen edges, a lighter glass lens behind the selection that slides between slots on `--motion-ease-spring`, 10pt medium labels in the system face, and the tint on the action token. `search` becomes its own glass circle beside the bar (elsewhere it joins the bar as the last tab), and `minimized` folds the capsule to the selected slot alone, the way the system bar shrinks while a page scrolls down.
- **`android`**, the Material 3 Expressive navigation bar: 64dp on `--color-bg-container-secondary` (the surface-container role), a 56 x 32dp pill indicator in a tint of the action colour growing out from the icon's centre on selection, and 12/16 medium labels tracked 0.5.

The platform variants are the system's one sanctioned departure from its own type and material rules, because their job is to read as the real system chrome in a mock or prototype; a shipped native app uses the native component. Their metrics, the platform faces (the device's `system-ui` face on iOS, Google Sans Flex then Roboto on Android, neither bundled), the glass fill, blur, saturation, white rim and shadow, and the platforms' own badge reds are restated from each platform under the `platform` directive category, scoped to those variant blocks. Colour roles still come from the tokens wherever the platform has a role for them, so a theme preset still reaches the accent and the surfaces. On the web the glass is frosted, not refracted: Apple's lensing needs shader access to the content behind it, which no browser gives a stylesheet.

### TopAppBar

**`ds-top-app-bar`** — The header of a mobile screen: a leading control (`navigation` of `menu`, `back` or `close`, reporting through `onNavigate`, labelled "Open menu", "Back" or "Close" unless `navigationLabel` says otherwise), the title with an optional `subtitle`, and trailing icon `actions`: the first three show as buttons, and any more join a `DropdownMenu` behind a "More" button, ahead of the `overflowActions` listed there (the button's label set by `overflowLabel`). Overflow rows are menu buttons, so the `overflowActions` type leaves out `href` and `badge` rather than dropping them silently, and an action that spills into the menu renders as a plain row that routes from `onClick`. An action's `badge` is a dot, announced as ", new". `size="medium"` and `"large"` add an expanded title under the row that folds away as content scrolls under the bar (`grid-template-rows` at `--motion-duration-slow` / `--motion-ease-emphasized`) while the bar's own title fades in; exactly one of the two copies is the heading (`headingLevel`, default 1) at any moment, and the other is `aria-hidden`. The bar watches its `scrollTarget` (the window by default), or takes `scrolled` from the host. It is `sticky` to the top of its scroll container by default, and pads the status-bar safe area. `align` centres the title (the iOS default) or starts it at the leading edge. A badged action's accessible name is `badgeLabel(label)`, "<label>, new" by default.

- **`default`**: a 56px row on `--color-bg-page-primary`, 40px `--radius-pill` icon buttons with the passive hover, the title in `--font-title-body-*`, the expanded title in `--font-heading-2-*` (medium) or `--font-heading-1-*` (large), and a `--border-025` hairline that is transparent until content scrolls under, so the scrolled state moves no geometry.
- **`ios`**: the iOS 26 navigation bar. No bar fill and no hairline: the leading control is a 44pt glass circle and the trailing actions share one glass capsule, the inline title is 17pt semibold and the large title 34pt bold, and once content scrolls under, the scroll edge blurs and fades it toward the top of the screen.
- **`android`**: the Material 3 Expressive top app bar. A 64dp row with 48dp targets around 40dp state layers, a 22/28 title and 12/16 subtitle, medium and large flexible titles at 28/36 and 36/44, and the surface lifting from the page role to `--color-bg-container-secondary` on scroll.

The platform rules are BottomNav's: the same sanctioned `platform` exception, the same faces, and the tokens wherever the platform has a role for them.

### AnchorNav

**`ds-anchor-nav`** — "On this page" list of anchor links for long pages. Optional header row (Material Symbol at `--icon-size-500` + `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` title) above a list carried on a `--border-025` `--color-divider` left rail. Links are `--font-paragraph-*` in `--color-text-tertiary` with `--padding-150`/`--padding-400` padding; the active link turns `--color-text-primary` at em weight, paints a `--border-050` indicator in `--color-text-primary` over the rail, and carries `aria-current="location"`. Uncontrolled, a scroll listener marks the last section whose top passed the `offset` prop; pass `activeId` to control the highlight instead. Focus-visible ring in `--color-action-primary-bg`.

The `floating` variant trades the inline rail for a minimap that costs no column width: a right-aligned stack of one `--border-050`-tall, `--radius-pill` line per section (16px in `--color-divider`; the active line stretches to 24px in `--color-text-primary`) at `--gap-200` spacing inside a `--padding-200` hit area. Hover, keyboard focus, or a tap expands the full header + list as a panel over the lines — Popover's floating-surface recipe (`--radius-200`, `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, `--color-bg-page-primary` fill so nothing shows through, `--shadow-floating`), right-anchored and vertically centred, capped at 70vh with internal scroll, sliding in one `--gap-050` at `--motion-duration-fast` / `--motion-ease-standard`. The links stay focusable while collapsed (opacity, never `visibility: hidden`), so tabbing in opens the panel via `:focus-within`; Escape or a click elsewhere closes a tapped-open one. The lines are `aria-hidden` decoration — the list is the semantics in both variants. The caller positions the nav itself, typically fixed on the right page edge.

### Breadcrumb

**`ds-breadcrumb`** — Location trail as a `<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">` ordered list. Items are links in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-secondary` separated by `chevron_right` Material Symbols at `--icon-size-500` in `--color-text-tertiary` (the 20px step matches the 20px line-height of the labels either side, so the chevron centres on the trail); the current (last) item is plain text in `--color-text-primary` at the `-em` weight. `maxItems` collapses the middle of long trails to an ellipsis, always keeping the first and the trailing items. Link focus rings use `--color-action-primary-bg`.

### Tabs

**`ds-tabs`** — A `role="tablist"` row built from a `tabs` array; the selection follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`, with `activeTab` and `onTabChange` kept as deprecated aliases), and the tablist is named "Tabs" by default through the native `aria-label`; default and compact sizes, and `fullWidth` to share the row equally. Arrow keys, Home and End move between the enabled tabs. It renders the tab list only: the consumer renders the panel. Use for grouping related content on a single page (e.g., component page showing Light/Dark/Mobile views).

### Accordion

**`ds-accordion`** — Collapsible content sections in a single card surface: `--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline at `--radius-300`, row dividers inside. Headers are `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` buttons with a rotating `expand_more` chevron and `--color-bg-container-secondary` on hover, the collapsible-section convention the agent cards set; panels hold arbitrary content at `--font-paragraph-*`. Single-open by default, `multiple` allows several; the open set follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`expanded`/`defaultExpanded`/`onExpandedChange`). Headers carry `aria-expanded` and are wired to their panels through ids scoped to the instance, so two accordions with the same item ids never collide; a closed panel is `inert`, so its links leave the tab order.

### TreeView

**`ds-treeview`** — Collapsible hierarchy for files, folders, and nested structures, rendered as a WAI-ARIA tree (`role="tree"` on the root `<ul>`, `role="treeitem"` on each `<li>`, children in `role="group"`). Rows carry a `chevron_right` chevron that rotates a quarter turn on expand (`--motion-duration-fast` / `--motion-ease-standard`; leaves render a spacer in the chevron column so labels align), a Material Symbol at `--icon-size-500` (`folder` default for branches, `draft` for leaves, per-node `icon` overrides), and a `--font-paragraph-sm-*` label that ellipsises rather than wraps. Indentation is a per-depth custom property: each row's `--ds-treeview-depth` steps `padding-left` by one `--gap-400`. Hover takes `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`; the selected row (`ds-treeview__row--selected`) takes `--color-action-passive-bg-active` with the label bumped to `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-weight`; rows round at `--radius-200`. The tree items themselves are focusable via a roving tabindex (one tab stop, never nested buttons): ArrowDown/ArrowUp walk the visible rows, ArrowRight expands or steps into children, ArrowLeft collapses or moves to the parent, Home/End jump to the ends, and Enter or Space selects (click on a branch row also toggles it). Expansion (`expandedIds`/`defaultExpandedIds`/`onExpandedChange`) and selection (`selectedId`/`defaultSelectedId`/`onSelect`) each follow the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention. Each treeitem is named by `aria-labelledby` pointing at its own label, so a branch never announces its whole subtree as its name; expanded state styling keys off `aria-expanded`, so visuals and semantics cannot desync.

### Carousel

**`ds-carousel`** — Sliding content viewport. Navigation arrows are 36px `--radius-pill` bordered buttons on `--color-bg-page-primary` (no shadow — the arrows are not floating surfaces); dot indicators mark the active slide with `--color-action-primary-bg`. Supports `autoPlay` with configurable interval, `loop`, and keyboard navigation (arrow keys); the current slide follows the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`slide`/`defaultSlide`/`onSlideChange`), and every accessible name it writes (arrows, slides, dots) is a `*Label` prop with an English default. Autoplay pauses on hover or keyboard focus and never starts under `prefers-reduced-motion` (a JS-side check; see Do's and Don'ts). The dots are a roving-tabindex `role="tablist"`: one tab stop, arrows move and select. Slides are the component's children.

### ImageCompare

**`ds-image-compare`** — Before-and-after comparison of two images sharing one frame: a `--radius-400` container on `--color-bg-container-secondary` under a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, sized by `aspectRatio` (default 16 / 10) with both images covering it. The after image is the base layer; the before layer sits on top clipped by `clip-path` to the divider position, so the two stay pixel-aligned as the split sweeps. Dragging anywhere in the frame moves the split (pointer capture, `touch-action: pan-y`, so vertical page scroll survives over the frame on touch); the handle — a 32px `--color-bg-page-primary` disc under `--shadow-floating` riding a `--border-050`-wide divider line — is a `role="slider"` with arrow keys (±1), PageUp/PageDown (±10), and Home/End. Corner labels (`beforeLabel`/`afterLabel`, defaults Before/After, hidden via `showLabels`) render as inverse pills in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*`. Position is controlled (`position`) or uncontrolled (`defaultPosition`, 50) with `onPositionChange`. The handle is named by `handleLabel` and announces `handleValueText`.

### Table

**`ds-table`** — Data table with header row and body rows; presentational only (no sorting, selection, or row expansion). Header: `--font-title-body-*` (weight 600). Body: `--font-paragraph-*`. Every colour is the table family (Table surfaces, above): row dividers on `--color-table-divider`, hover on `--color-table-row-hover-bg`. The plain table takes the background of whatever it sits on. `bordered` wraps it in a `--border-025` `--color-table-border` outline with `--radius-300` over `--color-table-body-bg`, so it stands off the surface it sits on (brighter in light, a little darker in dark), with the header band on `--color-table-header-bg`: the look every markdown table shares, which `Prose` draws for markdown in chat and docs. Three additive hooks carry semantics a consumer can set: a column's `sort` (`ascending` or `descending`) becomes its header's `aria-sort`, a `rowHeader` column renders its cells as `<th scope="row">` drawn as plain cells, and a row's `selected` holds the `--color-table-row-selected-bg` tint over hover and stripe. The bordered wrapper is the table's scroll container: while the table overflows it, the wrapper becomes a `role="region"` named by the caption with `tabIndex={0}`, so a keyboard user can scroll it, and while it fits it is a plain div (the internal `ScrollRegion` behaviour, a client boundary, so Table stays server-renderable). `striped` fills every even body row with `--color-bg-container-primary-semi`, keeping the same hover tint. `size="compact"` tightens the cell padding and sets header and body cells in `--font-paragraph-sm-*`. `columnDividers` adds a `--color-table-divider` line between columns, beside the lines between rows. A bordered table's visible caption is drawn above the outline, where the plain table's sits, with the `<caption>` itself kept in the table and visually hidden, so the table and its scroll region keep their name: a caption inside the outline would sit under the border and scroll with the body.

### DataTable

**`ds-data-table`** — The wired version of Table: sorting, search, row selection, and client-side pagination assembled around the presentational pieces (Table's `bordered` markup and classes, Pagination, Checkbox, Input, EmptyState), so a working data view is one component rather than an afternoon of plumbing. TanStack Table (v9, headless) runs the row model underneath: the search, the sort and the selection state, with rift's own comparator and search test so the order and the matches are what they always were; every type the component exposes is rift's own, so the engine's version never reaches the API. It is generic over the row type (`DataTable<R extends DataTableRow>`, defaulting to `DataTableRow`), so a row may carry more than its values and a column's `render` reads it typed, and it takes its ref as a plain prop. Rows carry raw values keyed by column; a column's `render` turns them into cells (Badges, Buttons) while sorting and search keep reading the raw value underneath. Chrome only in this component's own CSS — rows and cells are entirely Table's. The sorted header carries `aria-sort`, and its button is named by the header text alone (`sortLabel`), so the state is not read twice. A `rowHeader` column names each row, and its value names the row's checkbox ("Select Ada Lovelace", `selectRowLabel`; the first column stands in when none is marked, the id only when that is empty). Selected rows hold the table's selected tint. What changes without moving focus is announced through one polite region, mounted empty: a sort (`sortAnnouncement`), the result count once a search settles (`resultsLabel`, debounced by `MOTION_ANNOUNCE_DEBOUNCE_MS`), a page change (`pageAnnouncement`) and select-all (`selectedLabel`); a single row's checkbox speaks for itself. The visible footer count is `resultsLabel` and `selectedLabel`, and is not itself live. The built-in pager and empty state are localised through `paginationLabels` (the arrows, the readout and the landmark's name) and `emptyStateLabels` (the title and description), each merged over the English defaults; a consumer `emptyState` replaces the whole node.

Sortable headers render the label inside a button with a trailing sort glyph (`swap_vert` idle, `arrow_upward`/`arrow_downward` active, `--icon-size-500`) that fades in on hover/focus and stays at full opacity while active; clicks cycle ascending → descending → unsorted, and the button's `aria-label` narrates the current state. **Multi-sort**: with `multiSort`, a Shift-click adds the column after the current sorts (or cycles it in place among them) while a plain click still makes it the only sort, and a header menu offers Add to sort and, with several sorts, Remove from sort. While more than one column sorts, each sorted header shows its rank beside its glyph in caption type (in its floor, so ranks never squeeze the label); `aria-sort` marks only the first sort's header, and the others say their rank in the sort button's name (`sortRankLabel`, "Owner, sort 2, ascending"). Sorting compares numbers numerically and text with `localeCompare` (numeric-aware). The toolbar puts a consumer `toolbar` slot (filter Dropdowns) on the left and the built-in search Input (`search` icon, capped at 240px) on the right; searching matches every column's raw value and resets to page 1. Selection adds a 40px checkbox column with an indeterminate select-all scoped to the visible page. The footer pairs a `tabular-nums` readout (`--font-paragraph-sm-*` tertiary; result count, or "N selected" once a selection exists, in an `aria-live` region) with Pagination. No matches renders the bordered EmptyState. **Density is one setting**: `size` sets the rows and the table's own controls together, so a `default` table has the default-height search field and the numbered pager and a `compact` one the compact field and the "Page X of Y" readout; the controls inside rows (checkboxes, grips, menu and action buttons) stay compact at either density, so a row is as tall as its text. **Paging**: `pageSize` (or `defaultPageSize`) turns the pager on, and both the page (`page`/`defaultPage`/`onPageChange`, counting from 1) and the size (`pageSize`/`defaultPageSize`/`onPageSizeChange`) are pairs; `pageSizeOptions` puts a compact Rows per page Dropdown before the pager, with a caption label. A search, a filter, a sort or a new page size starts over at page 1 (reported once, not per keystroke), and a page past the last shows the last in the same render. **On a phone** (the table's own width under `BREAKPOINT_PHONE_PX`, measured, so a table in a narrow panel counts) `phoneLayout` picks the reading: `scroll`, the default, keeps the rows and pins the row header to the start while the rest scroll sideways, its width capped at 40% of the table so the columns passing it keep room, without touching the pinning the reader set; `stack` draws each row as a card on the body fill, divided like rows: the checkbox, the row header as the title in emphasis type and the actions on one line, then every other column as a caption label beside its value, the label taking a quarter of the card so the value sits close. The header row is visually hidden and the cells state their roles, since a card's display strips table semantics in some browsers; widths, pinning and row grips step aside. **Grouping and totals**: a `groupable` column can group the rows (`grouping`/`defaultGrouping`/`onGroupingChange`, column keys, the first drawn in this milestone), from its header menu (Group by this column, Ungroup) or a TableToolbar's Group control. The matching rows gather by the column's value, groups in the column's own order (its sort direction when it sorts, a status by its options' order, the rows with no value last under "No value") and rows keeping the sort within each group; paging runs over the gathered rows. Each group is its own `tbody`, led by a group row on the header's tint that takes no hover: a `th scope="rowgroup"` holding an `aria-expanded` chevron toggle (rotating a quarter turn), the value drawn through its column's type and the count in caption type, its label running past the first column over the empty cells beside it, then each column's `aggregate` (`sum`, `avg`, `min`, `max`, `count`, `countDistinct`, `range` or a function) in that column's cell, formatted through its type. Groups start open and fold for the session; the group row's checkbox selects the group, indeterminate while partly selected; a grouping owns the row order, so row grips step aside. Native grouped bodies keep table semantics for a read-only table, where D4's `treegrid` would switch them off. `totalsRow` adds a footer sticky at the table's foot on the header's tint, divided from the rows like the header: each cell a menu picking its calculation over every matching row (`totals`/`defaultTotals`/`onTotalsChange`, Notion's Calculate set, the numeric ones only on a numeric column), showing the calculation's name in caption type beside its result, an unpicked cell offering Calculate only while the footer is hovered or focused (in the layout at rest, so nothing moves). Stacked, a group row is the cards' heading. **Row details**: `renderDetail` adds a chevron column (after the checkbox, its header hidden as "Details") and an opened row shows what it returns beneath it, in one cell across every column, the panel as wide as the visible table and held to the start edge so it stays in view while the columns scroll; it renders only once opened, takes no hover tint, and opens with a height ease (`grid-template-rows` from `0fr`), which the reduced-motion guard makes instant. The open rows are a pair (`expandedIds`/`defaultExpandedIds`/`onExpandedIdsChange`), `expandMode="single"` closing the others as one opens; the chevron is an `aria-expanded` toggle with `aria-controls` naming the details (`toggleDetails`), and in a grid the right arrow on a row header opens a closed row and the left closes an open one, as a tree row does. Stacked, the details close their card. **Saved views**: a `DataTableView` is one serialisable object (`version: 1`, then any of the sorts, filters, search, hidden columns, column order, pinning, widths, grouping, density and page size), how the table is set up and never what the reader is in the middle of: the selection, the page and the open details stay out. The table applies a view by filling in what it leaves out from the setup it opened with, so switching views never carries one view's filters into the next, and runs `reconcileDataTableView` (exported) on it first: columns that no longer exist drop out of every part, a filter whose condition no longer fits its column's type is dropped, new columns join the end of the order and widths clamp to each column's limits. `onViewChange` hears the setup once per change, for a host keeping the reader's last view. **Scale**: an unpaged, ungrouped table of more than 200 rows (`virtualize`, `auto` by default, or `true` and `false`) draws only the rows in view and a few either side (TanStack Virtual, in a small `VirtualRows` part, so the React Compiler's opt-out stays with the rows), between two spacer rows that hold the scroll height, each row measured as it lands, so rows of any height scroll true. It scrolls inside its frame (`maxHeight`, 640px when unset) with its header held, sends its first screen of rows in the server's HTML, states `aria-rowcount` on the table and `aria-rowindex` on each drawn row, so a reader hears "row 340 of 10,000", and scrolls a row into view before the keyboard focuses it. Row grips and the stacked and grouped layouts draw every row. `hasMore` ends the rows with a loading row (a neutral Spinner and `loadingMoreLabel`), which calls `onLoadMore` as it comes into view, once per batch. The engine's sort, filter and selection state is rebuilt only when what it says changes, and text compares through one shared collator, so a re-render never re-sorts thousands of rows. **Column types**: a column's `type` sets what the column leaves unsaid, its cell, alignment, sort, filter conditions and the editor `editable` opens, and every type composes a part the library already draws: `text`; `number` and `currency` (`Intl`, tabular figures, end-aligned); `date` (a `YYYY-MM-DD` value, medium by default, the long form on hover, or `relative`, which shows the absolute date until the browser knows today, so the server's HTML always matches); `status` (a Badge in its option's `status` role, sorted in the options' order, so a workflow reads in its own order); `person` (an Avatar and the name, or an AvatarGroup stack for a list); `tags` (neutral Badges, the first two shown and a caption `+N` for the rest); `progress` (a compact ProgressBar and its percent, end-aligned); `checkbox` (the shared check, read as Yes or No, centred, the checked first ascending, and a live Checkbox when editable); `link` (the URL through the app's link component, `linkComponent`, shown without its scheme, in body text with its underline, since the action colour's link shade misses AA on a dark card). The table's own controls whose glyph is all a reader sees of them (the row grip, the filter mark, the group and details chevrons, the toolbar's grips) take `--color-text-secondary`, which holds the 3:1 a control needs on every table surface in both modes. `format` carries the locale, the currency, the digits and the date style; the locale defaults to `en-US` rather than the browser's, for the same hydration reason. `render` still wins over the type, and an empty value draws nothing, so a sparse column stays quiet. An end-aligned column's header sits over its figures with its options button at the start, and a centred one centres its label. A `checkbox` column filters by is checked and is not checked, its chip always naming the condition. Sort and selection follow the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention (`sort`/`defaultSort`/`onSortChange`, its several-column form `sorts`/`defaultSorts`/`onSortsChange`, which wins when both are given and fires alongside `onSortChange` with the first sort, `selectedIds`/`defaultSelectedIds`/`onSelectionChange`) so server-driven tables stay possible.

**Columns and rows.** DataTable draws its own `ds-table` markup inside a `ScrollRegion` frame, so it can pin, size and stick cells Table's markup does not know about. `resizableColumns` puts a grip on each header's end edge (drag it, double-click to fit the content, or Alt+Left and Alt+Right on a focused header, `RESIZE_STEP` pixels a press); `reorderableColumns` lets a header be dragged to a new slot or moved with Ctrl+Shift+Left and Ctrl+Shift+Right; `columnMenu` adds a menu to every header (sort, pin to either edge, move, fit, reset, hide), opened from the header's options button, Alt+Down, Shift+F10 or a right-click; a column's `pin` holds it at the start or end edge over an opaque body, with a hairline on its inner edge and `--shadow-table-pinned` once content has scrolled under it; `locked` keeps a column where it is and visible. `reorderableRows` adds a grip at each row's start (drag it, or Alt+Up and Alt+Down on it), off while a sort owns the order. `stickyHeader` with `maxHeight` scrolls the body inside the frame under a header that holds. `cellText` sets how text fits (`wrap`, `nowrap`, `truncate` with the full text as the cell's tooltip, `lines-2`), table-wide or per column. `rowActions` adds an actions column stuck to the end edge, inline actions as compact tertiary CircularButtons whose tooltips open to the side, the rest in a More menu. The More button always shows, so the column says what it holds at rest; the inline actions show on hover and focus by default (`rowActionsVisibility`) and always on a touch screen. Widths, order, pins, hidden columns and row order each follow the controlled/uncontrolled pair. Once any of these is on, `navigation` defaults to `grid`: the table is one Tab stop with arrow-key focus across cells. Every move, pin, hide and resize is announced through the polite region, and every word is a `columnLabels` entry. Every drag follows the gesture rule: the dragged column, row or guide is written to the DOM per frame through the folder's `dragGesture.ts`, and state is committed once on release. Widths ride custom properties on the table (`--ds-dt-w-*`, `--ds-dt-off-*`, `--ds-dt-total`) under a fixed layout, with a filler column taking the space a narrower table leaves, so the header band always reaches the frame's edge.

**Filters.** `filters` (with `defaultFilters` and `onFiltersChange`) holds the table's filter conditions, run by the engine's column filters with rift's own test, AND across them; `search` (with `defaultSearch` and `onSearchChange`) and `size` (with `defaultSize` and `onSizeChange`) are pairs too, so a TableToolbar can own them. With `columnMenu`, every filterable column's menu ends in Filter…, which opens the column's filter in a Popover beside a filter mark after the header label (adding a filter when the column has none); a filtered header keeps the mark, which reopens it. The header filters and a TableToolbar's chips are the same list. A column's kind is read from its values and `options` until typed columns land. A change of filters starts from the first page and announces the new count.

**The table holds still.** Nothing in the table moves under the pointer. Hover, focus and selection change only tint and opacity, never a cell's size or position: the sort glyph, the header menu button, the resize line and the row actions are all in the layout at rest and fade in. A column that declares a pixel `width` starts at it, so a table whose columns all declare one lays out fixed from its first frame; the rest are measured once from the natural layout, a truncating column capped at `TRUNCATE_START_WIDTH` so its longest text cannot set its width. Layers are one fixed order in DataTable.css (body cells, pinned body cells, a hovered or focused pinned cell, the sticky header, pinned header cells, a dragged header, the guide and drop lines), so the header always paints over a row scrolled half under it, hovered or not, and a cell holding an open menu rises over all of them. **Columns never overlap**: a column's floor (`minWidth`, by default what its header needs: its padding, two letters of label, and its sort glyph and menu button) holds against every resize, a header narrower than its label truncates the label and never its controls, so a control's hover fill keeps the cell's padding from the divider, and under fixed widths a body cell clips what does not fit (menus and tooltips are fixed panels and escape the clip). The actions column's width is CSS, from the live compact control height, gap and cell padding, so a theme's density moves it too.

**Editing.** A column's `editor` makes its cells editable, with one of the library's fields (`text` Input, `textarea` Textarea, `number` NumberInput, `select` Dropdown, `multiselect` and `combobox` Combobox, `tags` TagInput, `date` DateInput, `time` TimePicker) or a function that renders the consumer's own; the choice kinds read the column's `options`, and a cell shows the chosen option's label. A cell reads as text until it is edited, the same reason status is a Badge: a page of mounted fields is heavy and noisy. `checkbox` and `switch` are the exception, live controls in the cell that save as they toggle. The editor is always the compact field, at either density, and covers its cell's own box, so a row never grows while it edits; a textarea grows over the rows below as a floating layer. An editable column's floor also fits its editor (`EDITOR_MIN_WIDTH` in the folder's `editorKinds.ts`, each compact field's narrowest width with a short value whole beside its chrome, plus the editor's inset), so a field always fits its cell, in row mode too, where the editors sit side by side. An editing cell stops clipping, so the field's focus ring shows whole, but keeps its layer: a pinned column, the actions column's Save included, still covers a scrolling cell's editor, and only a cell whose list is open rises over the table. In cell mode an editable cell shows a pencil (a compact tertiary CircularButton on a solid fill, the body under the hovered row's tint, so text never shows through it) on the side the value leaves free, the end edge of a cell that reads from the start and the start edge of an end-aligned one (figures), centred on the row, while the pointer is over it or the cell holds focus, and the pencil opens the editor. In grid mode Enter or F2 opens a cell, a printable key opens a text or number cell with that character as the new value, Enter saves and moves down, Tab saves and moves to the next editable cell, Escape cancels, a double click opens a cell, and a press outside saves it. A column's `validate` returns a message to keep the editor open, the field in its error state and the message floating under it as an alert. Rows stay the record: the table holds only the open draft and reports the save through `onCellEdit`; a save that returns a promise shows its new value quieter (`--color-text-tertiary`, `aria-busy`) until it settles, and a rejection rolls the value back, outlines the cell in `--color-status-error-border` with the message as its tooltip, and announces it on an assertive channel, the table's one assertive message. `editMode="row"` adds an Edit button to each row that opens every editable cell of the row at once, with Save and Cancel in its actions column (their glyphs in `--color-status-positive-icon` and `--color-status-error-icon`, so the two read apart by colour as well as by shape), saved together through `onRowEdit`; the open row takes `--color-table-row-selected-bg`, so it reads as the one row that is open. With no column pinned to the end, the actions column is the end edge and carries its hairline and scrolling-edge shadow. Every word is a `columnLabels` entry. The editors live in the folder's `CellEditor.tsx`, internal to DataTable until a host needs one alone.

### TableToolbar

**`ds-table-toolbar`** — The control bar over a table: search, filters as chips, a More panel holding the density switch and the columns, a bulk bar for a table with row checkboxes, the page's primary and secondary actions, a result count and an `actions` slot, in one `role="group"` row that is **always one line**. The start cluster (search, then the chips that fit) takes the room, and the chips that do not fit fold into a `+N` chip that opens the filter panel (every filter edited in place, then Add filter and Clear filters in one row); the end cluster holds the end, with Filter beside what it makes, straight after search and before its chips, and **the icon controls grouped together** at the end: Sort, Group, then More, then the secondary actions, and last the primary action. **At most two controls in the bar carry a label**; in the bar's own row that is the primary action, and everything else is an icon, named and tooltipped. The search field flexes between 160px and 280px around a 200px basis and yields first, so the chips take the room it would otherwise hold. The fit is measured: chips past it stay in the layout out of sight and inert, so every chip can be measured, and the bar re-fits on every resize and every filter change. In a DataTable's `toolbar` slot the bar reads and sets the table's own state through context and needs no props; over any other table its props carry the same state as controlled and uncontrolled pairs (`filters`, `search`, `hiddenColumns`, `columnOrder`, `size`) with the `columns` it knows about and a `count`. A prop always wins over the table's state. **Sizing**: every control in the bar, and everything the bar opens (the Filter menu, a chip's editor, the More panel), takes the bar's density (the table's `size`), all at `--control-height-default` or `--control-height-compact`, the chips held to that height since Chip's own sizes sit between the two; controls inside the table (the header menu and its filter editor, row actions, cell editors) stay compact at either density. Toggles and panel triggers are icon-only to save room, each still named and tooltipped: Filter and More are tertiary CircularButtons, and so is each secondary action (`secondaryActions`, an action without a string icon falling back to a neutral secondary Button); the primary action (`primaryAction`) is the bar's one primary Button, folding to a primary CircularButton when the bar is narrow. A trigger whose panel is open holds `--color-action-passive-bg-active` until it closes. **The bulk bar**: in a `selectable` table, selecting a row turns the bar into the bulk bar, laid over its own row at the same height with no fill of its own, since it is the same bar in another mode and the selected rows' tint already says what changed (the row beneath stays laid out and inert, so nothing below moves and the chips keep their fit); the count reads at the button labels' size for the bar's density.
