One row at the width of a card (full width up to 360px): a 40px mark on `--color-bg-container-secondary`, its radius concentric with the card's (`--radius-600` less the `--padding-300` between them, computed from the live tokens so the corner holds under any theme's radius scale) holding an `--icon-size-600` glyph (`icon`, defaulting to `draft`, or any element), the title in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` over an optional `meta` line in `--font-caption-*` on `--color-text-tertiary`, both ending in an ellipsis rather than growing the row, and a trailing `--icon-size-500` cue on `--color-icon-secondary`. The row is a `--radius-600` card, the chat's card-furniture radius, with a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` edge on `--color-bg-container-primary`, washing to `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` on hover over the fast duration, with the house focus ring.

It is a toggle button and nothing more: a real `<button>` carrying `aria-pressed`, with `open` the whole state and `onClick` the whole behaviour, so the host decides what opening means. Open, it holds `--color-bg-container-tertiary`, the "on stage" fill ThreadPanel's active row and ThreadTabs' active tab wear, and the cue turns from `open_in_new_down` to `close`; the cue is decorative, since the pressed state already says it. Hook-free and stateless, with no `'use client'`. Disabled follows the house rule.

### ArtifactPanel

**`ds-artifact-panel`** — The surface a chat opens beside the conversation to show something it made: a dashboard, a document, a page, a site. Deliberately **surface-less** like AgentRail and ThreadPanel, so the host paints the card it sits in and decides where that card goes; the panel fills the box it is given (`width` and `height` at 100%) and scrolls exactly one region, the body. A `<section>` named by its own title through `aria-labelledby`. `expanded` is fully controlled by design, with no `defaultExpanded`: only the host can change the size it names, and ChatArtifactPane passes it straight through for the same reason. `codeBlockLabels` passes the Code view's copy and region names through.

The header is one row of 40px controls standing `--padding-300` in from the top and trailing edges, the inset AgentRail gives its own corner control, so the two panels that share a chat's trailing seat put their close in the same place and a rounded host card has room to turn its corner around it, over a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` rule. Leading: an optional `--icon-size-500` mark on `--color-icon-secondary`, the title as an `<h2>` in ChatHeader's `--font-title-body-*` face, and an optional `meta` line in `--font-caption-*` on `--color-text-tertiary`. The name outranks its meta when the row runs short: the meta carries a shrink factor large enough to give way entirely before the title loses a letter, and the title then ends in an ellipsis. Trailing: a `compact` `neutral` SegmentedControl switching Preview and Code (labels only, and only when the artifact has both), the host's own `actions`, then two tertiary CircularButtons with downward tooltips, the expand toggle (`expand_content` / `collapse_content`, `aria-pressed`, rendered only when `onExpandedChange` is given) and the close (`close`, rendered only when `onClose` is given). The panel never sizes or dismisses itself: `expanded` only picks the toggle's icon and name, and both callbacks report intent to the host that owns the seat. Every label the component writes is a prop, so none of its English is fixed.

The body is one of three previews or the source. `children` is live React content, scrolling inside the body and bringing its own padding (a dashboard wants an inset, a full-bleed page does not); `html` is a complete document drawn in a frame through `srcDoc`; `src` is a URL drawn in a frame. `children` wins over `html`, which wins over `src`. Both frames carry `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-forms"` by default and `referrerPolicy="no-referrer"`: the document runs in an origin of its own with no reach into the host page, which is the right default for output a model wrote, and `sandbox` widens it only for a source the host trusts. A framed document is somebody else's page, so it fills the body edge to edge and owns its own scrolling and its own colours; what it can do is follow the reader's scheme, since a frame reads `prefers-color-scheme` from the `color-scheme` of the element that embeds it, so a host whose theme sets `color-scheme` gets a preview that turns light or dark with it (the demo's page does). `code` (with `language`) adds the Code view, a CodeBlock inset by `--padding-400` with its own copy button; with no preview the source is the whole artifact and the switch does not render. The view is controlled through `view` or kept by the panel from `defaultView`, and a view the artifact cannot show falls back to the one it can, so a host swapping artifacts under a standing choice never lands on an empty body. The scrolling regions wear ThreadPanel's tucked-away thin scrollbar.

### AgentStatus

**`ds-agent-status`** — What an agent is doing right now: a dot-matrix indicator beside a line of status text. The matrix is a 4×3 grid of 12 `--radius-pill` dots sized off the icon scale (`--icon-size-600` wide at the default size, `--icon-size-500` at `compact`) with `--gap-050` between them, so the dots derive their size from the grid rather than carrying pixel values of their own. The label is set in `--font-title-body-*` — a status is a heading for the work underneath it, not prose. `compact` keeps the `--font-paragraph-sm-*` metrics (the scale has no 14px title face) but carries the same `--font-title-body-weight`, so one size of the component can't read as a heading and the other as prose; the size still pairs with ChatMessage's default and compact message text (`sm`/`md` remain as legacy aliases). The default size costs nothing in layout: the title-body and paragraph sets share 16px/24px metrics, so only weight and tracking change.

The named patterns — `AgentStatusPatterns.tsx` is their authoritative list (`braille`, `orbit`, `breathe`, `snake`, and the rest) — are choreography over that one grid. Every pattern runs on the same twelve-slot cycle (`--motion-duration-loop-matrix`, 1400ms): each dot is told which slot it lights in via `--ds-agent-step`, and dots a pattern never lights opt out of the animation. One grid and one cycle length means changing pattern never changes the indicator's footprint or its rhythm.

Six states: `idle`, `thinking`, `working`, `waiting`, `done`, `error`. The three working states are deliberately monochrome (`--color-text-secondary`, `--color-text-tertiary` when idle) — colour is reserved for the states where it carries meaning: the dots take `--color-status-warning-icon`, `--color-status-positive-icon` and `--color-status-error-icon` (they are a mark, and a theme may set its status text near the page's ink), and the label the matching `--color-status-*-text`. The terminal states fill the matrix solid so the shape itself reads as finished, and twinkle: on a fixed scatter at a sixth of the cycle's pace, a dot at a time dips to 0.6 and back, well under once a second across the matrix, a flicker short and shallow enough that a finished status stays alive without drawing the eye. `twinkle` is also a pattern of its own, the same flicker over a solid matrix, for a live state that should read as calm. While active, a sweep walks the label left to right (`--motion-duration-loop-shimmer` over a `background-clip: text` gradient from `--ds-agent-shimmer-base`, default `--color-text-tertiary`, through `--ds-agent-shimmer-sweep`, default `--color-text-primary`; `waiting` keeps its warning base and sweeps `--color-text-primary`); `shimmer` overrides the default. The base is the dimmer colour and the sweep the brighter one, so what travels is a highlight rather than a shadow — the reading `waiting` always had, now the rule. The band is narrow, 12% of a 250%-wide image and so under a third of the label at a time: spread wide it washes the whole label at once and stops registering as movement at all. When the `label` string changes, the outgoing text fades up and out while the incoming fades up and in over `--motion-duration-slow` — a status that narrates progress glides between steps rather than snapping. The shimmer's text clip stands down for the swap (an animated descendant composites on its own layer, which escapes `background-clip: text`) and resumes on the settled text; `children` are consumer-owned markup and render without the transition. Every colour on the component routes through custom properties — `--ds-agent-color` for the dots, `--ds-agent-label-color` for the text, plus the shimmer pair — so a consumer can tint a row (e.g. one `--color-core-accent-*` per agent when several share a surface; the showcase page demos one variation per core accent).

`variant="bar"` wraps the row in a full-width card surface (`--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--color-bg-container-border` hairline) at `--radius-300` for the top of a panel. The root is a `role="status"` live region, and the matrix is `aria-hidden`, so the state change is announced once as text rather than as twelve dots. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the matrix parks at a legible static opacity and the shimmer resolves to flat text — the global guard would otherwise collapse every dot to its dark final frame.

`AgentStatusMark` (`ds-agent-status-mark`), exported beside the component, is the dots alone for an icon slot, drawn once so every icon-sized "the agent is working" looks the same. It is a square 4×4 grid sized by the inherited `--icon-size` (`--icon-size-600` when none is set), and inset by a twelfth of that on every side, the margin a Material Symbol keeps inside its em box, so it swaps with a glyph without moving or reading larger than it. Every AgentStatus pattern is redrawn for the square on the matrix's own twelve-slot cycle (sixteen dots land on fractional slots, so the rhythm is unchanged); `orbit` is the default, twelve edge dots clockwise round four dim core dots, and `waiting` defaults to `breathe`, a still pulse of every dot that reads as the person's turn rather than work in progress; the states, colours, solid terminal fill and reduced-motion parking are AgentStatus's. It is `aria-hidden`: its host announces the state.

`variant="pill"` is a glass status for a run that goes on while the person keeps working: `--color-bg-glass` behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline at `--radius-pill`, on `--shadow-floating`, with a light component-local frost. The label truncates rather than widening the pill past its host. Once the run ends the dots fill solid in the status colour, as in every variant, so a finished pill reads green or red before its label is read. `action` seats a trailing control after the text (a link that opens the run), for the pill and bar variants. The pill does not position itself: the host seats it, and a shell can give it a home.

**The agent ring** (`ds-agent-ring`, set with `agentRingProps(state)` from `AgentRing.tsx` beside the component, which also carries the stylesheet) marks whatever an agent is working on: a section, a whole table, a row, a card, a field, a panel. It is a class and a `data-agent-state` attribute rather than a component, because a wrapper cannot go around a table row and a class works on any element. It is AiButton's ring recipe, the AI gradient masked to a `--border-050` band, drawn with the element's `::after` so it never moves layout, and it takes the element's radius. Two hooks let any surface wear it: `--ds-agent-ring-radius` gives a square surface corners, and `--ds-agent-ring-offset` stands the ring clear of a borderless one. `thinking` and `working` turn it once per `--motion-duration-loop-spin`; `waiting` takes `--color-status-warning-border` and breathes on `--motion-duration-loop-matrix`, the dots' own cycle, between 0.45 and full strength (the dots dim further, but a ring that faded that far would read as switching off); `done` and `error` take `--color-status-*-border` for the same 2s beat AiButton holds, then fade off, leaving the element as it was. The ring is colour only, never the action colour, and the fill stays the host's. An element that already uses `::after`, and a replaced element (an input, a textarea, a select, an image), takes the ring on a wrapper. `prefers-reduced-motion` stops the turn and the breath; the ring and the done and error fade stay.

**One vocabulary across every agent surface.** AgentStatus, the square mark, the pill, the agent ring and AiButton read a state the same way, so the person learns it once. `idle`: dim, still (`--color-text-tertiary`). `thinking` and `working`: monochrome and travelling (the chosen pattern, `orbit` by default; the ring turns). `waiting`: the warning colour, breathing in place on the twelve-slot cycle between 0.45 and full strength, dots and ring alike (a lower floor lets the mark all but vanish at the bottom of each breath) (`breathe` is every dot surface's default while waiting; the ring breathes on the same cycle). `done` and `error`: the positive or error colour, filled solid (the dots) or for a beat (the ring, and AiButton's field), with nothing moving. Dots and rings take the `--color-status-*-icon` and `--color-status-*-border` steps, never the text steps, which a theme may set near the page's ink; labels take the text steps. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` every surface stops moving and keeps its colour.

### AiButton

**`ds-ai-button`** — The AI entry point: icon and label on a solid `--color-ai-button-bg` field — the mode's extreme surface, lighter than the light floor and deeper than the dark one, so the pill stands off any page floor — ringed by the AI gradient and haloed by a soft glow of the same gradient escaping behind the opaque fill. The ring is the system's signal for "a model answers here": ordinary actions keep the flat `--color-action-primary-bg`, and this treatment is reserved for AI surfaces so neither affordance dilutes the other. It is a pill (`--radius-pill`, the button invariant) on Button's control scale — same padding tokens, same `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` face, `default` and `compact` sizes, the icon on `--icon-size-600` at `default` and `--icon-size-500` at `compact`, where the label also steps down to `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*`. The summary panel's region, dismiss button and prompt row are named by `summaryRegionLabel`, `dismissLabel` and `suggestionsLabel`.

The three stops are the AI gradient tokens (see **AI** in the Colors chapter for the role and its per-theme values). The ring paints a conic gradient of the three stops (start → mid → end → back to start, so the seam at 360° is invisible) across a `::before`, masked to a band with an exclude-composited two-fill mask. At rest the band is a `--border-025` hairline at 0.6 opacity, so the resting button stays quiet; hover and a busy status widen it to `--border-050` at full strength over `--motion-duration-base`, so the ring wakes when the button is in play. Keyboard focus leaves it at rest: the focus outline is focus's signal, as on Button, and a widened ring inside it would read as two concentric strokes. Rotation animates a registered `@property` angle on a deliberately off-scale 21s loop of three uneven turns — keyframes at uneven angles, eased per segment with `--motion-ease-emphasized`, so the ring drifts, surges and settles rather than ticking round at one rate, ending on a whole turn so the wrap is seamless and the average pace stays one turn per 7s (sanctioned in place with `ds-allow(motion)`: ambient signature motion, far slower than the interaction tokens); browsers without `@property` get a static ring. The glow is a `::after` of the same gradient blurred (a component-local `--ds-ai-button-glow` length, chosen by eye) at 0.3 opacity, rising to 0.55 with the ring — a colour effect, not an elevation shadow, so depth stays token-owned. On mount the pair plays a one-shot entry: the glow blooms out of the bottom-right corner (scale from a corner `transform-origin`, opacity from 0, `--motion-duration-slower` on `--motion-ease-entrance`) and the ring fades up into place a `--motion-duration-fast` beat behind it, so the button lights rather than arriving at full strength — from-only keyframes settling onto the base styles, composed alongside the turn as a second animation; a disabled button pauses only the turn, letting the entry complete. Focus is the standard `--color-action-primary-bg` ring; disabled drops to 0.4 opacity and pauses the rotation; `prefers-reduced-motion` stops the turning and keeps the ring and glow. Renders `<button>` or, with `href`, `<a>`.

`status` reports the agent behind the button in AgentStatus's six states, and the label never changes; the icon carries the state. The icon slot is a fixed `--icon-size` square, so no state's icon moves the label or the edge. `thinking` and `working` show `AgentStatusMark`, swap the ring's drifting cadence for a steady linear spin of one turn per `--motion-duration-loop-spin`, set `aria-busy`, and hold the summary panel shut. `waiting` shows the mark in `--color-status-warning-icon`, breathing rather than orbiting, with the ring at rest: the next move is the person's, so nothing travels and the button only pulses. `done` and `error` turn the whole button to their status family for `MOTION_FEEDBACK_RESET_MS`: the field takes `--color-status-*-bg`, the ring and glow `--color-status-*-border` at full strength (the ring's stops are component-local properties, so one rule recolours both), and the icon is `AgentStatusMark` filled solid in `--color-status-*-icon`, the same finished mark AgentStatus shows; then the AI look and the resting icon return while the host's status stays. Each status icon fades and settles in over `--motion-duration-base` on `--motion-ease-entrance`. The button stays clickable throughout, unlike Button's `loading`, so a click can open the conversation to watch. A visually hidden polite region beside the control (never inside it, where it would join the button's name) announces each status in `statusLabel`'s words. `prefers-reduced-motion` stops the spin, the mark's cycle and the icon's entrance; the swap still shows the state.

With `summary`, the button summons a TLDR panel (`ds-ai-button__summary`): a frosted glass card (`--color-bg-glass` thinned in light via a sanctioned `color-mix`, heavy blur sanctioned in place) at `--radius-600` with the ordinary `--border-025` container border, opening above or below per `summaryPlacement` and right-aligned to the button. Button and panel live in one hover host (`ds-ai-button-host`), so pointer travel between them never closes the panel: it opens after `MOTION_HOVER_SHOW_DELAY_MS`, hides `MOTION_HOVER_EXIT_GRACE_MS` after the pointer leaves the whole host, and `summaryPinned` holds it open for callers with their own reveal signal. On open the AI gradient sweeps once around the panel's border (the button's masked conic ring run as a single 0→360° pass, then fading out — a registered `@property` angle, one-shot), the button gives one `--motion-ease-spring` acknowledge pulse, and the content rows stagger up on `--motion-ease-entrance`. The panel fakes its generation once per mount: Skeleton lines for `MOTION_SUMMARY_THINK_MS`, then the pre-written text through StreamingText within `MOTION_SUMMARY_REVEAL_MS`; later openings show the text instantly, as if cached. The overline sits in the `--font-overline-*` face, the title in `--font-title-body-*`, the text in `--font-paragraph-*`, and the chips are PromptSuggestions in its stacked compact form. The chips wait for the reveal to finish — the same "here is something you could ask" beat the chat's follow-ups take; showing them mid-think would give the staging away. During the type-out an invisible ghost of the finished text sizes the container, so the stream never jumps the panel and a short summary reserves no space it won't use. The panel stays mounted and hides on `visibility` — HoverCard's approach — so closing eases back down the path the entrance sprang up while the hidden panel stays out of the accessibility tree and the tab order. A corner dismiss button closes it for the visit and fires `onSummaryDismiss`; leaving the host re-arms it, so the next hover summons it fresh. The card caps at a component-local 340px measure (minus a phone margin) on `--shadow-floating`. `prefers-reduced-motion` drops every panel animation; the think beat still stages, being a schedule timing, while StreamingText's own check makes the reveal instant.

With `request`, the button carries what the agent needs from the person while `status` is `waiting`, in a request panel (`ds-ai-button__request`) at the button, so the question appears where the person is already looking. The panel is the summary's glass surface, opening sweep and pop, with its own `requestPlacement`, and it is the only surface: it adds no padding, and the content brings its own spacing. An InterruptCard approval with `bare` is the usual filling, which drops the card's own fill, border and radius so the approval reads as the panel itself with one border; a question, a form or a preview work the same way, padded by the host. It opens by itself each time the status moves into `waiting` (it never waits for hover), and it is a non-modal layer on the dismissal stack: Escape folds it back into the button, as does a click on the button the host's `onClick` does not prevent, and the next click opens it again, with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` on the button saying so. There is no light dismiss, since a stray click should not hide a pending decision, and no corner close, since the content is the host's. Open state is the `requestOpen`/`defaultRequestOpen`/`onRequestOpenChange` pair, so a host can keep it shut while its chat shows the same request. While it is open the summary panel stays shut.

### ChatHeader

**`ds-chat-header`** — The top row of a chat surface: the conversation's name on the left, its controls on the right. A space-between flex row padded `--padding-150` `--padding-400` at `--gap-400`, deliberately drawing no bottom border — like Composer at the other end, it floats free of the transcript, and the thread's own top padding provides the separation. A string `title` renders in the `--font-title-body-*` set in `--color-text-primary`, truncating with an ellipsis so a long conversation name never wraps the row; a node `title` renders as-is for consumers who need their own heading element. The `title` prop deliberately shadows the native tooltip attribute — a header's title is content.

`actions` is a trailing flex row at `--gap-100` that only lays controls out: the intended fillings are tertiary CircularButtons (new chat, close) and a view-switch toggle, each owning its own behaviour and accessible name. The component is purely presentational — no `'use client'` — so it renders from a Server Component.

### ChatMarker

**`ds-chat-marker`** — The inline separator a conversation uses for anything that is not a turn: date breaks, joins, mode changes, system notes. A `--font-paragraph-sm-*` label in `--color-text-tertiary` sits between two flanking `--border-025` lines in `--color-divider` (`--gap-300` off the text), so the row reads as furniture rather than as a message. An optional leading Material Symbol renders at `--icon-size-500` and is `aria-hidden` — the label carries the meaning. `line={false}` keeps the flanking segments as transparent spacers, so a bare note stays centred on the same geometry.

The root is `role="separator"` with the label as its content: screen readers treat it as a boundary, not a message, matching how the eye skips it while scanning turns. System *events* belong here; system *messages* with content are ChatMessage's job.

### ChatMessage

**`ds-chat-message`** — A single chat turn: avatar, author, timestamp, and the content itself, aligned by role. User turns are right-aligned bubbles; assistant turns are surface-less full-width text in `--color-text-primary`, so a transcript keeps its question-and-answer rhythm without every row wearing a surface. The `bubble` prop overrides the role default in either direction: an assistant turn can take a received bubble for classic messenger layouts, a user turn can go plain. Bubble surfaces come *only* from the four chat bubble tokens — sent bubbles pair `--color-chat-bubble-sent-bg` with `--color-chat-bubble-sent-text`, received bubbles the matching received pair — never the container ramp directly, so re-theming a chat means repointing those four tokens and nothing else. The action colour never appears as a bubble fill: an action-coloured bubble would spend the "click here" signal on something that is not clickable.

Bubbles sit at `--radius-600` with `--padding-300` `--padding-500` padding and cap at `--ds-chat-message-max-width` (default 75%), a consumer-override hook; `tail` squares the speaker-side bottom corner to `--radius-100` — bottom-right on sent, bottom-left on received — a corner treatment only, with no pseudo-element pointer. Content sits on the `--font-paragraph-*` set; `size="compact"` drops it to `--font-paragraph-sm-*` and tightens bubble padding. Content is width-locked to its container — `min-width: 0`, `max-width: 100%` and `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the content block, so an unbroken run (a URL, a token) wraps inside the bubble or column instead of pushing the surface wider. The meta row is the author in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` `--color-text-secondary` beside a free-text tabular-nums timestamp in `--color-text-tertiary`. The avatar gutter is a fixed `--ds-chat-message-gutter` (default `--icon-size-800`, matching Avatar's small size): `showAvatar={false}` and `grouped` hide the avatar but keep the gutter, so consecutive rows in a run stay aligned; `grouped` also drops the meta row and pulls the row up towards the one above. Card furniture inside a turn (MessageCard, InterruptCard, ToolCall as direct content children) takes `--gap-300` of clearance from the content before and after it — margins, not gap, so a card standing alone in a turn adds nothing.

`pending` replaces the content with three `--color-text-tertiary` dots pulsing in a staggered wave on `--motion-duration-loop-matrix`, wrapped in `role="status"` with a visually hidden `pendingLabel` and the dots `aria-hidden`; under `prefers-reduced-motion` the dots park at a legible static opacity rather than freezing at the loop's dim frame. `actions` renders under the content, revealed on hover and `:focus-within` over `--motion-duration-fast` — and always visible where hover does not exist (`hover: none`), so touch users are never locked out; `showActions` pins the row on permanently, for surfaces where the actions are part of the response (the site chat's copy and thumbs row) rather than an affordance to discover. `attachments` holds the files sent with the turn, an AttachmentGroup drawn outside the bubble, above the text, on the speaker's side; a turn that carries files and no text draws no bubble at all, rather than an empty padded one, and a compact thread seats smaller tiles. `footer` is an always-visible slot for sources or an edited note. The package ships no markdown renderer: render markdown yourself, ideally wrapped in Prose, and pass the result as children.

### ChatThread

**`ds-chat-thread`** — The scrollable conversation column, and the home of the chat surface's most important interaction: the send choreography. The root is the scroll container; turns stack inside an inner `__content` wrapper (flex column at `--gap-1000`, padded `--padding-500` with the inline gutters routed through `--ds-chat-thread-padding-inline` as a consumer-override hook for mobile densities), and every entry is `flex-shrink: 0` so children that clip their own overflow never collapse. A second hook, `--ds-chat-thread-content-max-width`, caps the conversation column and centres it — for wide surfaces where the container grows but the column should not — while the scroll container, its fades, and the scrollbar keep spanning the full component width. Both ends fade through a pure-CSS `mask-image` gradient with `--gap-500` stops — the PromptSuggestions treatment turned vertical — so content scrolling out passes through the fade with no scroll listeners involved (the mask alpha keywords carry a `ds-allow(color)` sanction).

On append, a newly added turn *floats to the top*: the component sizes a trailing spacer so the first new child can sit exactly at the top of the viewport — even when the newest exchange is short — then scrolls to it, pushing the prior conversation upward. The glide comes from `scroll-behavior: smooth`, which the reduced-motion guard in the motion tokens forces back to instant; because content streaming in *below* the viewport never moves the scroll position, the anchored turn stays put while a response grows beneath it. When a user turn and the agent's pending turn are appended in one update, the user turn is the anchor. The first send into an empty thread has no scroll distance yet, so it gets the same feel from a transform instead: the new turn starts at composer level and floats up to the anchor position over `--motion-duration-slower` `--motion-ease-entrance` (a thread that mounts with a restored transcript instead opens instantly at the latest message, re-establishing the anchor). The spacer holds exactly the shortfall between the anchor and what the content can already reach, tracked in both directions for the whole exchange, so the scroll range ends precisely at the anchor and never past it. That is what keeps the dead space honest: reserved room a growing answer has since filled is given back as it goes, so the space is gone by the time the answer lands rather than collapsing after it — no jump, because the range it defines never moves. Growing it back is the direction that needs the clamp guard: content shrinking mid-exchange pulls the range under the anchor a frame before a ResizeObserver can react, so the position is restored alongside the range. The send has a clamp guard of its own: a render that folds part of the conversation as it appends the reply (a QuestionCard answered) can be laid out partway through its commit (removing a focused element forces it), pulling a reader at the end of the range upward before the thread reacts, so the glide starts from where the reader last scrolled to rather than the clamped spot; the `FoldOnSend` stories hold it. A shrinking child count reads as a conversation reset: the spacer collapses and the thread returns to the top. `anchor={false}` opts out entirely.

That choreography is the default `flow`, `top-down`, and the one every chat surface here uses unless a host chooses otherwise. `flow="bottom-up"` is the classic message thread for hosts that want it: no anchor and no spacer, the root becomes a flex column whose `__content` grows to fill it and pushes its turns to the end (`justify-content: flex-end`), so a short conversation sits just above the composer. A send glides to the end through the same `scroll-behavior`; content that grows while the reader is at the end (a streaming answer, a card, the follow-ups) keeps the newest line in view with an instant scroll, and scrolling up lets go until the reader returns to the end or sends again. Only an upward scroll lets go, so the send's own glide never counts as leaving.

The scrollbar is deliberately subtle: `scrollbar-width: thin` with `scrollbar-color` held transparent until a scroll event lands, then `--color-divider` over a transparent track while scrolling continues, fading back out after a settle delay (`MOTION_SCROLL_SETTLE_MS`, from the shared JS motion constants in `src/tokens/motion.ts`); `scrollbar-gutter: stable` reserves the gutter so a scrollbar popping in never reflows content sideways, and the component publishes the measured gutter width as `--ds-chat-thread-gutter`, which the content's right padding subtracts (clamped at zero) — so on classic-scrollbar platforms the column stays perfectly symmetric and flush with its surroundings, with the scrollbar tucked outside it at the far edge. Because the inline gutters live on the inner wrapper, the scrollbar rides the component's far edge rather than sitting inside the content column. The root is a `role="region"` named by `ariaLabel` (default "Conversation") with `tabIndex={0}`, so the scrollable area is keyboard-reachable and takes the house focus ring inset.

A scroll-to-bottom control appears whenever there is anything left to scroll to: a `--radius-pill` circle matching Composer's send button — the same 40px footprint (`--icon-size-600` glyph plus the `--padding-150` ring), pointing the other way — on the action-passive ramp painted over a `--color-bg-container-primary` surface with a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` edge and `--shadow-floating`, centred in a sticky zero-height slot pinned just above the bottom fade, floating over the transcript right on top of the composer. Clicking it glides to the bottom through the same CSS `scroll-behavior` the anchoring uses, and once nothing is left to scroll to it fades out and leaves the accessibility tree (`aria-hidden` plus `tabIndex={-1}`); `jumpLabel` names it for screen readers. The content column's bottom padding is `--padding-1000`, so the end of a long response rests 40px clear of the composer.

### Composer

**`ds-composer`** — The chat input shell: an optional context note, a tray of queued files, an auto-growing textarea, a leading actions slot, and a trailing send button. The shell is the control — a `--color-input-bg-primary` surface behind a `--border-025` `--color-input-border-primary` border, floating on `--shadow-floating`. It stacks two zones with different densities: a text zone padded `--padding-400` `--padding-500` `--padding-200` (roomy above and beside the words, tighter toward the bar), and an action bar padded a uniform `--padding-200` holding the leading `actions` slot, the `trailingActions` slot, and the send button. Bar icon actions take *tertiary* CircularButtons at the default size — borderless ghosts that only show a fill on hover — so every control in the bar shares the send button's exact 40px footprint and `--icon-size-600` glyph, and the send button stays the only drawn circle. The radius is the one deliberate departure from the inputs-are-`--radius-300` rule: the corner is the component-local `--ds-composer-radius`, derived live as the send button's effective radius (its `--radius-pill` capped at half its size) plus the bar's `--padding-200` ring plus the `--border-025` width — 29px in the base theme, and automatically concentric under any re-themed radius (a crisp preset that squares the pill squares this corner with it); it is one component's geometry, deliberately not a step on the global radius scale. The textarea inside is borderless and outline-free so the shell carries the whole interaction ramp: `--color-input-border-hover` on hover, `--color-input-border-selected` on `:focus-within`, transitioning over `--motion-duration-fast` `--motion-ease-standard`. The shell is also the click target: clicking anywhere on it that is not a control focuses the textarea, with a `cursor: text` affordance everywhere except the action bar, which keeps the default cursor. Text sits on the full `--font-paragraph-*` set in `--color-input-text-primary` with `--color-input-text-placeholder` placeholders. The send button is a composed primary CircularButton (`arrow_upward`) — the *one* sanctioned use of the action colour in the chat set, because sending a message is a genuine primary CTA, exactly what that token is reserved for. It disables while there is nothing to send, which means no text and no ready file, and while any queued file is still uploading (a file on its own is a whole message, and `onSubmit` then fires with an empty string); while `streaming` it swaps to a stop button (`stop`, firing `onStop`) and both Enter and `onSubmit` go inert, so a person can never fire a submit into a running response. Enter without Shift submits; Shift+Enter breaks the line. The trimmed-empty guard means whitespace never sends. Native `disabled` takes the house 0.4 opacity with `--color-input-bg-disabled` and not-allowed cursors, and disables the send button with it. `attachmentTileLabels` carries the queued tiles' status lines, progress name and remove name (`removeLabel(name)`).

The textarea starts at one row and grows with its content up to `maxRows` (default 8), then scrolls internally: the cap is `calc(var(--ds-composer-max-rows, 8) * var(--font-paragraph-line-height))` — the shell owns the block padding, so rows × line-height is the whole sum — with `--ds-composer-max-rows` set inline from the prop. Where the browser supports `field-sizing: content` the sizing is fully native; elsewhere a measurement effect sets the height from `scrollHeight` on every value change. The step between one height and the next is eased rather than snapped: a ResizeObserver publishes the textarea's measured height to the text zone as `--ds-composer-text-height`, and the zone states its height as that plus its own block padding, so growing a line and collapsing on send both run between two pixel values (`auto` has nothing to interpolate) over `--motion-duration-instant` `--motion-ease-emphasized`. At 75ms it lands under the threshold where a change reads as motion — the edge comes off the jump and nothing more. The zone clips (`overflow: hidden`) while the textarea sits at its new height already, so a new line is uncovered rather than pushed, and the action bar's buttons hold their geometry throughout — the bar translates with the shell, never resizing. `files` is the queue on this message, drawn as a tray of square AttachmentTiles above the textarea: one row that scrolls sideways instead of growing the shell. Composer owns intake and the tray, and the caller owns the list, mirroring FileInput's philosophy. Passing `onFilesSelected` arms three ways in at once: a built-in attach button at the head of the action bar (a tertiary CircularButton like every other bar action, opening a hidden picker that honours `accept` and `multiple`), files pasted into the textarea, and files dropped on the shell, which lights the selected border under a `dropLabel` hint while they hover. Composer hands the raw `File`s over and never stores one; `onFileRemove` reports a pressed remove button, after which focus moves to the tile that took the removed one's place, or to the textarea when the tray empties. A file drag the shell takes stops there, so a drop target around the composer never handles the same files twice. `pasteThreshold` is opt-in: with it and `onPasteAsAttachment` set, a pasted block at least that long becomes an attachment instead of flooding the field. A visually hidden `role="status"` line says what joined the queue, left it or failed, since a paste or a drop moves no focus (`formatFileAnnouncement` localises it). The tray sits `--padding-300` in from the shell's edge and sets the tiles' radius to the shell radius less the border less that inset, so the tile corners stay concentric with the shell under any theme (16px in the base theme). The older free-form `attachments` slot still renders, after the tray, and is deprecated. `actions` is the leading footer slot for a model picker, after the attach button. `context` renders above both, at the very top of the shell: a full-width, non-interactive chip — filled with `--color-chat-context-bg`, the quietest tint per theme (the nearest neutral to each shell that still separates from it: `--primitive-neutral-01` light, `--primitive-neutral-09` dark) at `--radius-pill`, on ModelPicker's trigger geometry (the send button's 40px height, `0 var(--padding-400)` inset) so the chip and the bar's pills read as one family, inset by the action bar's `--padding-200` ring, `--font-paragraph-sm-*` in `--color-text-tertiary` (the tone of AttachmentTile's `meta` line — the note is ambient context, quieter than anything a person typed) — the "what the model is looking at" note a chat host pins over the message ("Looking at “Page name”"). `contextIcon` names an optional Material Symbol rendered at its left (`--icon-size-500`, `aria-hidden`, inheriting the chip's text colour — one tone for the whole note; none renders by default, the ai set's icon-free-unless-asked convention). The chip is one line: the text sits in its own span carrying `text-overflow: ellipsis`, so a note that outgrows the shell clips with an ellipsis and one that fits carries no trailing dots — an ellipsis is a truncation mark, not punctuation — while the icon never shrinks. Composer owns the chrome, the caller passes the text, and a click on it falls through the shell handler to focus the textarea like any other non-control surface. `contextPlacement` decides where the chip sits: `inside`, the default, is everything above; `above` lifts it out of the shell into its own full-width bar a `--gap-200` gap above it (Composer then wraps the bar and the shell in a plain `ds-composer-group` column, so a host's own flex or grid gap can never land between them; `className` stays on the shell, and the bar carries the same click-to-focus handler), so the note reads as what the model can see rather than part of the message. The above placement also aligns the layout on one optical rail, `calc(var(--padding-200) + var(--padding-400))` (24px, the bar's ring plus the pill inset): the text zone and attachments row take it as their inline padding through the shell's `--context-above` modifier, a ModelPicker trigger's label already lands on it, and the bar's own inline start adds the `--border-025` it no longer sits inside. The bar's icon is pulled back by its glyph's side bearing so the ink rather than the box lands on the rail: 4/24 of the icon size by default, the bearing of the portrait document glyphs a context note usually carries, overridable through the `--ds-composer-context-icon-bearing` fallback hook. The inside placement keeps its own geometry unchanged. The site chat uses `above`. `onSubmit` reports the value but never clears it: the consumer owns the value and empties it after a successful submit. The textarea defaults to `aria-label="Message"` when the caller provides no accessible name; ref and unrecognised props land on the `<textarea>`, `className` on the shell.

The opt-in `aiGlow` prop dresses the focused shell in AiButton's exact signature — the system's "a model answers here" signal, for composers whose messages are answered by a model. On `:focus-within` the plain selected border goes transparent and two pseudo-elements play AiButton's entry: the blurred halo blooms out of the send-button corner (scale from a corner `transform-origin`, opacity from 0, `--motion-duration-slower` on `--motion-ease-entrance`) and the ring fades up a `--motion-duration-fast` beat behind it — transition-driven rather than AiButton's mount animation, because the composer wakes on a state change and must replay on every focus; the resting rules carry the way back, so losing focus collapses the halo quickly on `--motion-duration-base`. The pair: a conic-gradient ring in the `--color-ai-gradient-*` triple, masked to the same `--border-050` band as AiButton's ring, its outer edge on the shell's own border box — one step up from the shell's `--border-025` border, with the extra pixel growing inward so the geometry never shifts — and the same gradient blurred (14px, matched to AiButton) behind the opaque shell at 0.3 opacity so it reads as a halo around the edge. Both rotate in phase on AiButton's ambient cadence (the 21s three-uneven-turns loop), driven by a Composer-scoped registered `@property` angle so the component never depends on AiButton's stylesheet; browsers without `@property` render the ring static, and reduced motion keeps the ring but stops the turning. Off by default — the plain composer keeps `--color-input-border-selected` on focus, and a disabled composer keeps the pseudos dark even if a control inside it holds focus. `aiGlow` and `streaming` together define one more state, the working glow: while a response streams, the ring stays lit and keeps turning whether or not the field holds focus — the visible half of the streaming contract for glowing composers, carried by the same pseudo-elements with no new API. Either prop alone behaves as before, and the same reduced-motion rule stills the turn.

`mentions` arms the Mention pattern on the composer: one `MentionSource` per trigger character, `@` for entities and `/` for skills. A trigger typed at the start of a word opens that source's MentionMenu from the trigger that called it: `--gap-100` clear of the line the trigger is on, above that line by default and below it with `mentionPlacement="bottom"` (so on a message of several lines the menu lies over the text before or after the trigger, never a whole shell away from it), with the rows' first column of content lined up on the trigger character and the menu held inside the shell's width (the character's position is read from the overlay described below, since a textarea cannot report one); the list narrows as the word grows and closes when nothing matches. A trigger inside a word (the slash in a path, the at-sign in an address) opens nothing. Arrows move the highlight and wrap, skipping disabled items; Enter or Tab writes the choice over the typed word with a trailing space and never sends; Escape closes the menu for that word, takes the keypress so a dialog around the composer stays open, and the menu returns for the next trigger. The choice is written as a real edit (a range replacement followed by a genuine input event), so `onChange` fires with an ordinary event and form libraries see what a keystroke would have shown them; `onMentionSelect` reports the item and its trigger. A textarea cannot colour part of its own text, so while armed the words are drawn twice: the textarea keeps the caret, the selection and every keystroke with its text transparent and its caret in `--color-input-text-primary`, and an `aria-hidden` overlay beneath it draws the same string through MentionText, in the same type on the same rail, which is where a mention gets its tag. The overlay takes the textarea's measured height and client width (published as `--ds-composer-text-height` and `--ds-composer-text-width`, the second read again on every change because a scrollbar at the row cap narrows the measure without resizing the box) and follows its scroll position, so the two wrap and scroll as one. The textarea keeps its implicit textbox role and gains `aria-autocomplete="list"`, with `aria-controls` and `aria-activedescendant` while the menu is open, and a visually hidden `role="status"` line says how many suggestions arrived, since the menu opens without moving focus (`formatMentionAnnouncement` localises it). The text rail itself is one shell-level property, `--ds-composer-text-inset`, which the text zone, the attachments row under `contextPlacement="above"` and the overlay all read.

### AttachmentTile / AttachmentGroup / AttachmentViewer / AttachmentDropZone

The attachment family: how a file looks and behaves everywhere a conversation touches it. Four components share one stylesheet, `Attachment.css`, and one data shape, `AttachmentItem` (id, name, kind, size, status, and an optional preview). Two headless modules ship beside them: `fileTypes` reads a file's kind from its extension and mime type and formats sizes, with no React in it, and `useAttachments` owns a list of files, validates what joins it (accepted types, size, count, duplicates), runs an optional upload per file, and creates and revokes the object URLs behind picture previews. The library reads files in the browser and uploads nothing; sending a file anywhere is the host's `upload` function.

**`ds-attachment-tile`** — One file as a square, the same object in a composer's queue and on a sent message. The tile is `--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` line at `--radius-400`, `--padding-300` inside, with its content a centred stack: the type mark, the name, and one secondary line in `--font-caption-*` `--color-text-tertiary`. The name truncates in the middle, so the extension always shows: it is split into a head that ellipsizes and a tail (the end of the stem plus the extension) that never shrinks, which is pure layout with no measuring, so the tile renders from a Server Component. One visually hidden copy carries the full name to assistive technology. The upload spinner is named by `progressLabel`.

The type mark says what kind of file it is, and the family draws it one way. The four well-known formats (pdf, spreadsheet, document, presentation) get a file silhouette in their `--color-file-*` role with a short label lettered beneath it (PDF, XLS, CSV, DOC, PPT) in `--color-text-secondary`; every other file wears its extension as a neutral chip on `--color-bg-container-secondary`. The silhouette, the lettering and the fold are one inline SVG in a 32-unit square, so the lettering is drawing geometry that scales with the mark rather than a step on the type scale, and its fold is `--color-scrim` laid over the format colour. Every mark, and every glyph a state swaps it for, fills the same square (`--icon-size-800`, `--icon-size-600` at `size="compact"`) at the same seat, so a file changing state never moves or resizes anything. An item's `typeLabel` replaces the derived label wherever the family draws the mark (the tile, a lone picture's badge, the viewer, a composer's queue), to localise it or to name a format the derivation does not know.

`status` is the file's life. `ready` shows the stack with `meta` (a size, typically). `uploading` swaps the mark for a spinner, drawn to a state glyph's own circle and stroke and exposed as a `progressbar` with `progress` when known, over an "Uploading…" line. `error` swaps it for an alert in `--color-status-error-icon`, turns the border to `--color-status-error-border` and prints the `error` message or `errorLabel`. `unavailable` is a neutral `block` glyph over "Unavailable", for a file that has expired. Two things fill the whole square instead of the stack, and only while ready: `previewSrc` (a picture, or a first page the host rendered for a document, which keeps its format as a small badge over the corner; the library carries no document renderer), and `excerpt`, the first lines of a pasted block fading out under a "PASTED" badge.

`onClick` makes the body a `<button>` and `onRemove` adds a 24px circle straddling the top-right corner, siblings that never nest. The remove button shows on hover and focus, always on a failed tile, and always where hover does not exist; it hides by opacity, never visibility, so it stays in the tab order. A pressable tile answers hover by lifting its border to `--color-text-tertiary` and its surface to `--color-bg-container-secondary`, and a picture brightens and eases up a step. The tile's geometry routes through consumer hooks with fallbacks: `--ds-attachment-tile-size` (136px; 96px compact, raw edge lengths because a tile is a fixed object like an avatar, not a step on the spacing scale), `--ds-attachment-tile-radius`, and `--ds-attachment-mark-size`.

**`ds-attachment-group`** — The files on a sent message. One ready picture keeps its own aspect ratio, capped by `--ds-attachment-single-max-width` and `--ds-attachment-single-max-height` (320px each) at the bubble's `--radius-600`, with its extension badged over the corner; an item that states its `width` and `height` holds that capped box from the first frame, before the picture loads, so the thread never shifts when the pixels arrive. Anything else is a wrapping list of square tiles, `--gap-200` apart, hugging the `align` edge (`end` for the sender's turns); four cells that cannot share a row sit two by two rather than wrapping three and one. Past `max` (default 4) the last cell becomes the next file dimmed under `--color-scrim` with a "+N" count, a button that expands the grid in place; a "Show fewer" button under the expanded grid folds it again. That is the system's disclosure trio (`open` / `defaultOpen` / `onOpenChange`), and focus follows whichever control replaced the one pressed. Pressing a file opens the built-in AttachmentViewer on it (`viewer`, on by default); an unavailable file stays on the message as a record and opens nothing. `attachmentTileLabels` and `attachmentViewerLabels` pass the tiles' and the viewer's words through.

**`ds-attachment-viewer`** — The preview dialog, a modal overlay on the shared behaviour layer (dismissal stack, focus scope, counted scroll lock). Its open state is controlled by design (see the Components intro): the host owns `open` and hears dismissals through `onOpenChange`, while the file on show follows the pair convention (`activeId`/`defaultActiveId`/`onActiveChange`). A header names the file with its type mark, its size and its position ("2 of 5"), with a Download button when `onDownload` is passed and a close button; a stage shows the file on a dotted canvas ground (`--color-bg-container-border` dots on `--color-bg-page-primary`, the texture of the docs site's dotted stages); a footer steps through the rest, and the arrow keys do the same. The viewer draws pictures itself and leaves everything else to the host through `renderPreview`, which is how a PDF's pages get in. A file with nothing to draw says "No preview for this file type" beside its mark, name and size; one that failed or expired says "Couldn’t load this file" and offers "Try again" when `onRetry` is passed. On a wide screen it is a centred dialog at `--radius-300` under `--shadow-modal`; below 30rem it becomes a full-screen sheet with no margin or radius.

**`ds-attachment-drop-zone`** — A wrapper that makes a whole surface a drop target, so a file dragged anywhere over a conversation lands without aiming for the composer. While files hover, an overlay covers the surface, flush with it and sharing its corner radius: a solid `--border-050` `--color-input-border-selected` edge (the active-input border, because the surface is the input about to take the files) with a soft glow of the same colour around it, over a frosted wash of `--color-bg-container-primary` that blurs the content behind, carrying an `--icon-size-800` icon in the edge's colour (`upload_file` by default), the `label` ("Drop files to attach") and a `hint` line for accepted types and limits. The glow is the edge's own colour blurred, a colour effect like the Composer's AI ring and not an elevation shadow. The zone hears drags in the capture phase and keeps the ones carrying files to itself, so a target nested inside it, or a page-level one outside it, never handles the same drop twice; a drag of anything else passes through untouched. The overlay is decorative and `aria-hidden`: dropping is a pointer path, and the keyboard path is the composer's attach button.

### DocumentChip

**`ds-document-chip`** — Deprecated: the earlier one-row file reference, superseded by AttachmentTile and kept working for existing consumers. A compact file reference: a two-line tile with a type icon, name, metadata line, upload progress, and an optional remove button, for documents attached to chat messages or queued above a composer. The tile is `--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--border-025` border at `--radius-300`, with the border colour routed through a local `--ds-document-chip-border` custom property so the error state repoints one variable to `--color-status-error-border` instead of restyling parts. A module-level map picks the Material Symbol for each of the eight `fileType` values (pdf, doc, sheet, slide, image, code, archive, generic) at `--icon-size-600`, overridable via `icon`; the name truncates with an ellipsis at a consumer-overridable `--ds-document-chip-max-width`, in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*`. `meta` is free text in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-tertiary` ("1.2 MB", "12 pages"), so callers keep their own formatting. `progress` swaps the metadata line for a composed compact ProgressBar with its percentage label; `error` replaces it with a `--color-status-error-text` message and colours the border and icon with the error pair. `size="compact"` tightens the padding and drops to one line — name only — for dense composer rows.

Passing `onClick` turns the body (icon + text) into a `<button>` inside the root `<div>`, so click and remove coexist without nesting interactive controls; the remove button carries `removeLabel` as its accessible name and every icon is `aria-hidden`. Chip stays the one-line `--radius-pill` pill for attributes and filters; FileInput stays the form control that owns selection — DocumentChip only references a file the host already holds.

### InterruptCard

**`ds-interrupt-card`** — A human-in-the-loop checkpoint: a question from the agent with option buttons for the person to decide ("Allow file edit?" — allow once, always allow, deny). It shares ToolCall's skeleton and footer: the same neutral `--color-bg-container-primary` container at the chat furniture's shared `--radius-600` shell behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border`, and an options footer with ToolCall's exact actions-footer values (the card's own background, flex-start at `--gap-200`, `--padding-200`/`--padding-500`/`--padding-400`, separated from the body by whitespace alone — no rule), so a decision on a card and an approval on a call read as the same row. The card is deliberately single-tone in both states — no status colour on icon or border; the question and its buttons are the signal, and a visually hidden status announcement carries the waiting state. The icon sits in `--color-icon-secondary` via the local `--ds-interrupt-card-color`. Once `value` is set the decision is made: the options are replaced by a quiet echo of the chosen label behind a check icon, on the same footer padding (the `--answered` modifier stays on the root as a consumer styling hook). The header stacks a `--font-heading-3-*` title (the question is the card's one heading, so it takes the same heading tier as MessageCard's title — the chat cards share one title scale) over a `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-secondary` description, beside an optional Material Symbol at `--icon-size-500` — none renders by default, keeping the ai cards icon-free unless a caller opts in. The waiting announcement is `waitingLabel`. `bare` strips the shell (fill, border, radius) when another panel supplies the surface, the charts' `bare` convention; AiButton's request panel is the host it exists for.

InterruptCard is for a run that stops on a tool call until a person decides; a choice that simply continues the conversation (permission asked in a reply, a retry, applying a proposed change) is a QuestionCard instead, whose answer is sent as the next message (see QuestionCard). Options are structured data (`{ value, label, variant, tone }`), not a free slot, because the options *are* the component — each renders as a compact Button with the option's emphasis and tone (`primary` for the recommended choice, `tone: 'destructive'` for a dangerous approval; the legacy `variant: 'danger'` maps to secondary × destructive) firing `onValueChange` with its value. The card is fully controlled and holds no state. Freeform "tell it what to do instead" input is deliberately out of scope in v1; compose richer detail via `children`, whose intended pairing is `<InterruptCard><ToolCall status="pending" /></InterruptCard>` — the question above, the exact call it covers below. The root is `role="group"` named by the title, with a visually hidden `role="status"` "Waiting for your decision" while unanswered.

### QuestionCard

**`ds-question-card`** — An agent's question with answers it can list, drawn under the reply that asked it: one question or a short set (the chat widget caps a set at `MAX_QUESTIONS`), each `single` (one option) or `multi` (several, with an optional `min` and `max`), and an optional last "Something else" row that opens a text field in its place. The open field is a row like the others: solid with its mark on while it holds an answer, the mark off again when the text is cleared; a lone one-answer question sends it from a compact CircularButton in the mark's place. QuestionCard is the default for any choice that keeps a conversation going, including permission, a retry or applying a proposed change: the answer is sent as the next message, so the exchange reads as one continuous thread. InterruptCard is the hard stop on a tool call: the run is paused on that card, with the call it covers inspectable inside it, and the decision is made on the card rather than in the thread. The two stay separate because their shapes differ: buttons in a footer against option rows, a step count and multi-select. The hidden chosen marker is `labels.chosen`.

The card has no shell. It sits inside the assistant's message under the prose, so the rows carry the structure: each a full-width control on Input's surface (`--color-input-bg-primary` at `--radius-300`) with `--padding-300`/`--padding-400`, drawn with a `--border-025` dashed `--color-text-tertiary` line while it is an answer on offer. Hover turns the line solid in `--color-input-border-hover`; a pick turns it solid in `--color-text-primary`, doubled by an inset ring of the same width so the row does not grow. Every row carries the real control's indicator at its end, which says what kind of question it is before anything is touched: `RadioIndicator` for one answer, `CheckboxIndicator` for several, each lit on hover through the row's `ds-control-host` class and showing the control's checked state when picked. A `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-tertiary` line above the options says it in words too (`labels.chooseOne`, "Choose one"; `labels.chooseMany`, "Choose one or more", or the count `min` and `max` set), and names the group through `aria-describedby`; in a set it tucks up under the heading. The label is `--font-paragraph-*`, an optional description `--font-paragraph-sm-*` in `--color-text-tertiary`. The row's border, in the neutral ink, is what outlives the moment as the record of a choice; the indicator shows the control's checked state the way the form control would. Rows rise in with the suggestion row's staggered pop.

One answer to one question is a tap: the option is the answer. A `multi` question collects its picks until Send, which waits for `min`, while a reached `max` disables the rows still off. A set asks one question at a time under a `--font-paragraph-emphasis-*` heading with a `--color-text-tertiary` "2 of 3" count; a one-answer question moves on when answered, the last question waits for Send so a slip there can be taken back, Back steps back, and focus moves to the new question's first option at every step. The digits 1 to 9 pick by position. The footer (tertiary Back and Skip, then Next as secondary or Send as primary, all compact Buttons) appears only when there is something to press. Skip shows only when the host passes `onSkip`.

Answered (`values` set) the card stops taking input and keeps what was offered: one question keeps every row, "Something else" included, the picks marked and the rest at the dimmed convention's 0.4; a set folds to a `QuestionSummary`. An empty `values` records a skip and `disabled` records that the moment passed: one question dims every row under a `--color-text-tertiary` line saying which, and a set lists every question it asked with that word in `--color-text-tertiary` where the answer would be, never just the first, which would read as if one question had been asked. **`ds-question-summary`** — `QuestionSummary`, the second export of the QuestionCard module, is the record of a set: each prompt in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` over its answers in `--font-paragraph-*` (several joined with commas), `--gap-050` within a row and `--gap-300` between rows, and `missingLabel` in `--color-text-tertiary` where a question has no answer. The `card` variant (QuestionCard's) frames it as a card at `--radius-300` on `--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline with `--padding-300`/`--padding-400` and sets the prompt in `--color-text-tertiary`; `plain` drops the frame and takes its ink from the surface it sits on, with the prompt in `--color-text-secondary` so it stays legible on a bubble's fill. One export for both sides of the conversation, so the question's record and the message that answered it are the same pattern.

Fully controlled: `onValuesChange` reports every question's answer once the last is given, as `{ questionId, values, other? }` with values in the options' order.

### Mention

**`ds-mention`** — A reference written into a sentence, and the menu that reaches for it: `@` for an entity (a person, a file), `/` for a skill. One registry entry, three exports from one module, because they are one pattern: `Mention` is the inline tag, `MentionMenu` is the list a trigger opens, and `MentionText` draws a plain string with every mention in it as a tag. The pure helpers behind them (`getMentionQuery`, `filterMentionItems`, `splitMentions`, `insertMention`) live in a React-free sibling module with a package subpath of its own (`./components/Mention/mentions`), so a Server Component or a route handler can run them without crossing the components' client boundary; the component module re-exports them too.

The pattern stores nothing beside the text. A mention is recognised wherever a trigger character is followed by the exact label of one of that source's items, standing as its own word, and it stops being one the moment the name is edited. That is what lets the value stay a plain string through typing, sending, storing and rendering: the composer and the sent message both derive their tags from the same text and the same `MentionSource` list.

The tag is drawn so that it can be laid exactly over the text of a textarea. It takes the surrounding type whole and adds the theme's action colour in its text form, `--color-action-primary-text-tertiary`, the tone a tertiary button writes its label in: the name is set in it with two parts in five of `--color-text-primary` mixed in, over a faint wash of the unmixed tone (10% of it over whatever surface is beneath) at `--radius-100`, each routed through a consumer hook (`--ds-mention-color`, `--ds-mention-bg`). A mention is the one word in a sentence that was chosen from a menu and points at something, which is why it may wear the colour reserved for what can be acted on; it uses the text token and never the action fill. The mix is there because a name in running text is smaller and lighter than the button label the token was tuned for: unmixed, it fell under 3:1 against the page in the themes whose action colour sits nearest their surface, and at this ratio it holds 4.5:1 in every shipped theme and both modes. A fixed hue was tried first (the info family's saturated tone) and blended into the themes built near it, where the action colour moves with the theme. The fill backs it up, because in a theme whose action colour is a neutral the colour alone says little. Nothing on the tag may move a glyph off its advance, so it carries no weight, no letter spacing and no net width: the fill reaches `--padding-050` past the glyphs on each side through padding that an equal negative margin pays back (`--ds-mention-bleed`), and `box-decoration-break: clone` keeps the fill and corners on both lines of a name that wraps. The trigger character sits a step quieter than the name, at reduced opacity, since it is punctuation and the name is the word.

**`ds-mention-menu`** — The floating-surface recipe the chat set's menus share: `--color-bg-page-primary` (opaque, because the container fill is translucent in dark mode) behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` line at `--radius-300` under `--shadow-floating`, fading in over `--motion-duration-fast` `--motion-ease-entrance`. Rows are one line on the `--font-paragraph-sm-*` set: an optional leading mark (a Material Symbol at `--icon-size-500` in `--color-icon-secondary`, or any node), the label, and a description in `--color-text-tertiary` that takes the remaining width and truncates. Rows sit `--gap-050` apart inside a `--padding-100` inset at `--radius-200`, concentric with the panel. The list shows `--ds-mention-menu-max-rows` rows (default 8) and half of the next, so a longer list is cut mid-row and reads as scrollable; `--ds-mention-menu-width` sets the width (320px by default, never wider than its container).

The menu never takes focus. The caret stays in the field being typed in, which owns the arrow keys and points `aria-activedescendant` at the highlighted row (`mentionOptionId` builds the id, `mentionListboxId` the id for `aria-controls`); a press on the menu is kept from blurring that field. There is one highlight, shared by the pointer and the keyboard: the pointer moves it (on move, not on enter, so a list scrolling under a resting pointer never steals it) and it is drawn in `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`, DropdownMenu's focused wash, a translucent neutral that differs from the panel in every theme where an opaque surface step may not. The highlighted row is kept in view by moving the list alone, never by scrolling ancestors. While a `query` narrows the list, each label steps back to `--color-text-secondary` and the typed part holds `--color-text-primary` in the emphasis weight, so the cue survives a theme that sets both paragraph weights the same. A disabled item stays listed at the house 0.4 opacity and cannot be chosen. The list is a `role="listbox"` named by `label` and is itself a tab stop with the house focus ring inset, because a region that can scroll has to be reachable from the keyboard on its own terms; in normal use the field takes Tab as "choose", so the stop is only met by someone who went looking for it. With no items the menu renders nothing unless `emptyText` is passed.

### MessageActions

**`ds-message-actions`** — The icon-button row for message-level actions: copy, retry, feedback. Built to slot into ChatMessage's `actions` prop, which reveals it on hover and keyboard focus, so the row's own job is just the buttons. Each item is a ghost button — no border, transparent at rest, `--radius-200` with `var(--padding-100)` padding around a Material Symbol at `--icon-size-500` in `--color-icon-primary` — hovering onto `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` and `--color-text-primary` over `--motion-duration-fast` `--motion-ease-standard`. The row itself is a flex line at `--gap-050`. An `active` item (the chosen feedback thumb) holds `--color-action-passive-bg-active`; focus takes the house ring inset (`outline-offset: -2px`), disabled the house 0.4 opacity. A `destructive` item rests like its neighbours, keeping the row quiet, and on hover takes `--color-action-destructive-text-secondary` over the `--color-status-error-bg` tint, as a destructive menu item does. These stay ghost buttons rather than CircularButtons because a resting surface per icon would give a quiet utility row four competing pills.

Every button carries its item's `label` as `aria-label`, and by default a composed Tooltip shows the same label on hover and focus (`showTooltips={false}` drops the tooltip, never the name). `aria-pressed` renders only when an item sets `active` — a toggle-like action announces its state, a plain command like copy stays a plain button. The row is stateless beyond hover: copy feedback is the consumer swapping the item's `icon` to `check` (and its `label` to match) for a moment, so the component never owns a timer or a clipboard call — `onActionClick` fires with the item's stable `id` and the consumer decides everything else.

### MessageCard

**`ds-message-card`** — A structured rich-content card embedded in a chat message: media, title, body, and an actions row, for link previews, search results, and booking-style rich responses inside an assistant turn or bubble. Card is the navigation tile you click to go somewhere; MessageCard is content furniture inside a conversation — but the two share one card geometry, and MessageCard's `--radius-600` shell matches the chat bubbles around it. The container is `--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` border. The optional media slot is inset, not full-bleed: `--padding-300` (12px) of card around a `--radius-300` (12px) slot keeps its corners concentric with the card's 24px shell — the exact geometry of Card's case-study cover, and the same rule the Composer follows (images inside render block at full width). The slot carries its own `--color-bg-page-primary` surface (lightest in light mode, darkest in dark), so drawn media — a chart, an SVG — reads as the same image-shaped panel a photo would, on a ground that recedes rather than tints. A Chart placed in the slot sheds its own panel chrome (background, border, radius, most of its padding) — the card is already the panel, and stripping the chart's container tone keeps the drawing at maximum contrast on the slot's page tone. The body stacks at `--gap-200` inside `--padding-400` top / `--padding-500` side / `--padding-200` bottom padding (`--padding-400` bottom when no actions follow), so the media-to-body distance equals the body-to-actions distance and the copy sits centred between image and CTA row. It opens with a header unit — an optional Material Symbol at `--icon-size-500` `--color-icon-primary` beside a `--font-heading-3-*` title, with the free-text `meta` line ("transit.example", a date) in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-tertiary` tucked `--gap-100` beneath it as the title's subline — then a description in `--color-text-secondary`, then any children — a Prose block, a DocumentChip row. The card claims no width of its own: the bubble or turn constrains it, with `--ds-message-card-max-width` as the consumer-overridable cap.

The `actions` footer mirrors ToolCall's actions footer exactly — the card's own background, flex-start at `--gap-200`, `--padding-200`/`--padding-500`/`--padding-400`, separated from the body by whitespace alone rather than a rule — so approvals on a tool call and link actions on a card read as the same row across the ai components.

### ModelPicker

**`ds-model-picker`** — The model selector for a chat surface, built for Composer's `actions` slot but freestanding anywhere. The trigger is a quiet `--radius-pill` pill on `--color-action-passive-bg` (`--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` in `--color-text-secondary`, rotating `expand_more` chevron) — deliberately chrome, not a call to action, so the send button keeps the only action colour in the composer. It is the row height (`--control-height-*`, CircularButton's too) at `size` `default` or `compact`, with only horizontal padding, so the pill and the composer's round icon buttons read as one row of equals. The panel is the floating-surface recipe (`--color-bg-page-primary` — the opaque page fill the floating menus and panels share, because the container fill is semi-transparent in dark mode and would show text through — hairline border, `--radius-300`, `--shadow-floating`, min-width 260px) placed by the shared anchored-position behaviour, `placement` naming the side it prefers (`top` for a composer pinned to the bottom of the screen); it flips on its own when that side has no room. The model list and the effort group are named by `listLabel` and `effortLabel`. The trigger carries `role="combobox"` (a select-only combobox, Dropdown's pattern), which is what makes its `aria-activedescendant` valid while the list is open. The empty trigger text, and the default name, is `placeholder`.

Models list as a `role="listbox"` of rows — `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` name with an optional `--color-status-info-bg` badge pill, a tertiary description line, and the shared ChosenMark on the selected row — with Dropdown's keyboard model (arrows move, Home/End jump to the ends, Enter selects, Escape closes, outside click dismisses) and `aria-activedescendant` tracking the highlighted row. A disabled entry stays visible in the list rather than vanishing: it takes the house disabled treatment (opacity 0.4, `cursor: not-allowed`) with `aria-disabled` on the row, clicks are suppressed, and arrows and Home/End skip over it — which is what lets a host grey a model out while keeping the lineup readable (the site chat's budget lock leans on exactly this). When an effort value is provided (`effort`/`defaultEffort`/`onEffortChange`), an effort row appears below a `--color-divider` rule: an "Effort" label beside a compact neutral SegmentedControl, the library's own mutually exclusive set (one tab stop, arrows move focus and selection together), named by the label through `aria-labelledby`. Effort levels default to Low/Medium/High and are overridable via `effortOptions`. Model and effort selection both follow the controlled/uncontrolled pair convention.

### PromptSuggestions

**`ds-prompt-suggestions`** — Tappable prompt suggestions to start or steer a conversation. In the chip layouts each suggestion renders as a clickable Chip, so one component owns the pill look — the row only arranges them and never restyles the pills. `size` picks which Chip size to ask for, one step above Chip's own scale because a conversation starter is something to tap rather than metadata about something else: `default` renders `large` chips at the body-paragraph scale, so a suggestion reads at the same weight as the message it will become, with a `--gap-300` gutter; `compact` renders Chip's default 32px pill at `--gap-200`, for rows sitting alongside a live conversation. `layout` picks the arrangement — three chip rows, described here, and two block layouts, described below — named on the element rather than inferred so each owns its own rules. `scroll` (the default) is a single line (`overflow-x: auto`) whose edges fade out through a pure-CSS `mask-image` gradient with `--gap-500` stops: the fade is the overflow hint, with no scroll listeners and no JS measurement. `wrap` trades that for `flex-wrap` across multiple lines, the empty-state hero placement where everything is visible and there is no overflow to hint at. `stack` gives each suggestion its own line for narrow columns, where a wrapped row breaks wherever the labels run out of room and the ragged edge reads as an accident; the pills still hug their labels (`align-items: flex-start`), since stretching them to a shared width would make a set of prompts look like a set of buttons. `padding-block: var(--padding-050)` keeps chip focus rings clear of the scroll clip. The boolean `wrap` prop is a deprecated alias for `layout="wrap"`, ignored when `layout` is set. Two further layouts leave the chip behind for an empty-state home screen. `tiles` is an even row of quiet blocks, each an icon over the label and a `description`; `cards` gives each suggestion a cover holding its icon, with the label and description beneath. Both draw their own button on the Widget surface (`--color-bg-container-primary-semi` over a 24px blur, a `--border-025` hairline, `--radius-600`) and keep the row's shared behaviour: list semantics, the pending shimmer in the block's own shell, the staggered entrance, and the press-and-spring acknowledgment. The two answer the pointer differently on purpose: a tile stays put and firms up (the fill to `--color-bg-container-primary`, the hairline to `--color-text-tertiary`), while a card lifts with Card's own hover (the same 4px rise and the same sanctioned hover-lift shadow, restated under `ds-allow(color)`), so a prompt card and a navigation tile behave alike.
