A card's cover is a top-to-bottom wash of one `--color-pixel-ink-*` token with the icon standing in it in the same ink, the inks cycled by position so a row is told apart by colour as well as title. The grid folds on its own width: the column floor is three cover icons for a tile and four for a card, so four tiles or three cards share a row on a conversation column and drop to two columns and one on a phone. `value` is fully controlled by design, with no `defaultValue`: it records a choice the host acted on, and the row never latches one itself. The hidden chosen marker is `chosenLabel`.

The root is `role="list"` (named by `ariaLabel`, default "Suggested prompts") with each chip in a `role="listitem"` wrapper, so a screen reader announces how many suggestions there are before reading any. The chips themselves are real buttons — Chip's clickable mode — which also keeps the scrollable region keyboard-reachable. Tapping one fires `onValueChange` with the suggestion's stable `id`, never its display text.

`pending` swaps the suggestions for placeholder pills (`ds-prompt-suggestions__skeleton`, `pendingCount` of them, default 3): the chip's real shell — its `--color-bg-page-primary` surface, `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` border, and the current size's padding and computed height at `--radius-pill` — holding a text-height shimmer bar (`ds-prompt-suggestions__skeleton-bar`, ThreadPanel's 0.75em `--color-bg-container-secondary` bar with Skeleton's sweep on `--motion-duration-loop-shimmer`) where the label will be, so what reads as loading is the label generating inside a finished pill, and the row is exactly as tall pending as loaded. Shell widths cycle through three em values so the set reads as questions of different lengths rather than identical pills. The placeholders are `aria-hidden` and the root reports `aria-busy`, so assistive technology hears a busy list rather than three empty items — the `aria-busy` is load-bearing, exempting the momentarily item-less list from the required-children rule. The site chat uses the state both ways: honestly under a finished answer while its follow-up questions are generated, and staged for `MOTION_SUGGESTIONS_THINK_MS` when the welcome screen's pre-written starters swap on navigation — the summary panel's think beat applied to chips.

Items pop in one after another on mount — a `--motion-duration-slow` rise-and-settle (`translateY` + slight scale) on `--motion-ease-spring`, staggered one `--motion-duration-instant` per pill and capped at the fifth so a long scrolling row does not keep its far end waiting, `backwards`-filled so a delayed pill waits unseen for its turn. The pending placeholders and the chips that replace them are both items, so each set lands one-two-three; every timing is a token, so the reduced-motion collapse stills the entrance with everything else. `entrance={false}` suppresses the pop-in for a set that has already landed — a host re-showing suggestions the visitor watched arrive (the site chat reopening over the same page's starters, or over follow-ups from earlier answers) renders them standing rather than arriving twice.

Tapping a chip plays an acknowledgment beat before the host hears about it: the chosen chip presses in and springs back (`--motion-duration-slow` on `--motion-ease-spring` — the overshoot is the bounce) while its siblings dim to the system's 0.4 convention on a `--motion-duration-base` opacity transition, and `onValueChange` fires `MOTION_EXIT_SYNC_MS` after the tap — so a host that unmounts the row on selection (the chat sending the suggestion) still lets the choice be seen. One choice at a time: taps are ignored while a beat is in flight, which also guards a double-tap firing twice; the chosen state clears when the pulse ends, so a row that persists without `value` returns to normal and stays tappable. A row that should keep the choice once used, such as the chat's follow-ups under an earlier answer, sets `value` (below).

`value` turns a row that stays on screen after use into the record of the choice (`--kept`): the taken suggestion outlined solid in `--color-text-primary`, doubled by an inset ring of the same width so it does not grow, and every other one faded to 0.4 behind a dashed `--color-text-tertiary` line; every one drops its fill, so the record reads as an outline; no chip or block answers the pointer any more, and a visually hidden "(chosen)" names the taken one. It is QuestionCard's answered treatment less the indicator and the input surface, so a chip keeps its shape.

### Prose

**`ds-prose`** — Token-styled typography for rendered markdown and rich agent output. The package ships no markdown renderer: consumers render markdown with whatever library they already use and wrap the output in Prose, which styles the descendant elements through scoped selectors. Body text sits on the `--font-paragraph-*` scale in `--color-text-primary` with `--gap-400` between blocks, edges trimmed (`first-child`/`last-child` margins zeroed) so it slots into bubbles and cards cleanly. Headings step down the token tiers — `--font-heading-1-*` through `--font-heading-3-*`, then `--font-title-body-*` for `h4` — each applying its full five-property set, so headings take the theme's tier weights without Prose restating them. Links use `--color-action-primary-text-tertiary` with an underline; `strong` takes `--font-paragraph-emphasis-weight`; lists indent by `--padding-400` with `--gap-100` between items.

Inline code and `pre` both use `--font-family-code`: inline code sits on `--color-bg-container-secondary` at `--radius-050` with `--padding-050` horizontal padding, one size step down; `pre` is a `--color-bg-container-primary` panel with a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` border at `--radius-300`, `--padding-400`, scrolling horizontally rather than wrapping, and `pre code` drops the inline chrome. Blockquotes take a `--border-050` `--color-divider` left rail with `--padding-400` inset in `--color-text-secondary`, mirroring Reasoning's trace rail. Tables are the markdown tier of the table family (Table surfaces, above), with the blueprints' tables' metrics exactly: a `--color-table-border` outline at `--radius-300` over `--color-table-body-bg` (separate borders, since a collapsed table cannot round its corners), `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` headers on `--color-table-header-bg`, `--font-paragraph-sm-*` secondary body text top-aligned, `--padding-300` by `--padding-400` cells, and `--color-table-divider` row rules. `tableSize="compact"` tightens the cells to the library Table's compact step (`--padding-150` by `--padding-300`) for long or dense tables; the site's MarkdownTable takes the same `size`. They are their own scroll container: `display: block` with `width: max-content`, `max-width: 100%` and `overflow-x: auto`, so a table wider than its container scrolls sideways instead of being clipped. Prose styles markup it does not render and so cannot add a wrapping element, and the price of making the table element the scroller is that it sizes to its content rather than filling the container, since the anonymous table box inside a block table cannot be stretched. Cells also set `overflow-wrap: normal`, which holds each column's minimum width at its longest word no matter what the surrounding context does; without it an ancestor's `overflow-wrap: anywhere` (ChatMessage sets it so a long URL cannot widen a bubble) inherits in, collapses every column to a single character, and auto table layout squeezes headings into stacks of letters instead of letting the table reach its natural width. Consumers who want the scroll reachable by keyboard alone should put `tabindex="0"` on the table, which is markup only the consumer can supply; `role="region"` belongs on a wrapping element if the scroller also needs an accessible name, never on the table itself, where it would replace the table semantics. `hr` is the same hairline; images cap at `max-width: 100%` under `--radius-300`.

`size="sm"` moves the body scale to `--font-paragraph-sm-*` for dense chat contexts while headings keep their tiers. Prose adds no roles or behaviour of its own — the semantics are the consumer's markup — and its `pre` is the plain fallback: CodeBlock remains the richer choice for standalone code. A renderer may also map fenced code to CodeBlock inside Prose (the site chat does, for the copy button): a `.ds-code-block` takes the same `--gap-400` block rhythm, and the plain-`pre` panel and inline-code chip step aside so CodeBlock's own border, header and padding are the only chrome.

### Reasoning

**`ds-reasoning`** — A model's thinking, disclosed behind a one-line summary. The trigger is a borderless `--font-paragraph-*` button in `--color-text-secondary` (stepping to `--color-text-primary` on hover) with an `expand_more` chevron *after* the summary — trailing, like ToolCall's, so the summary line starts at the same left edge as everything around it — rotated −90° to point at the collapsed trace, turning back to pointing down as the panel opens, the same closed/open convention CodeBlock's chevron uses. `summaryOnly` drops the chevron, the trigger, and the panel, leaving the summary line alone — for a model that reports what it is doing but produces no trace to read, where a disclosure would open onto nothing. The line keeps the trigger's own geometry, so a trace arriving mid-response promotes it to a real disclosure without moving anything. The panel collapses with the `grid-template-rows: 0fr → 1fr` technique over `--motion-duration-slow`, and toggles `visibility` alongside it so collapsed content leaves the accessibility tree. The trace sits on a `--border-050` `--color-divider` rail in `--color-text-secondary` — the rail dissolves to the transparent container colour at its foot, the same end-of-line treatment as Timeline's last stem — its geometry derived from an AgentStatus summary: the rail runs down the centre of the matrix (half an `--icon-size-600`, corrected by half the rail width) and the trace text starts exactly where the status label's first letter starts (`--icon-size-600` + `--gap-300`; the compact size derives the same sums from `--icon-size-500` + `--gap-200`). A plain text summary shares the same geometry, so swapping a live status for the thought-for line never shifts the trace — quiet enough never to compete with the answer beside it. The text scale pairs with ChatMessage: the default size matches default message text, and `size="compact"` drops trigger and trace to `--font-paragraph-sm-*` to match compact message text. The summary's three states are `thinkingLabel` ("Thinking"), `durationLabel(seconds)` ("Thought for Ns") and `doneLabel` ("Thought process"); `label` overrides all three.

`streaming` opens the panel and shimmers the summary with the same treatment AgentStatus uses, so "the model is working" looks the same wherever it appears; when the stream ends the panel collapses to `Thought for {duration}s`. That auto-collapse yields to the reader — once someone has toggled the panel themselves, the stream ending no longer moves it. Only the summary is a `role="status"` live region: a trace announced token by token floods a screen reader, so the body stays ordinary expandable content and the announcement covers the boundaries.

The `summary` slot replaces the line with custom content — designed for an AgentStatus, so the live indicator and the trace disclosure work as a pair: AgentStatus-with-trace while the agent runs, the quiet thought-for line above the finished response. A node summary owns its own appearance and announcement, so Reasoning's text shimmer and live region stand down (AgentStatus shimmers and announces itself). Dropping the `summary` prop animates the handoff itself: the departing node fades out in place while the text summary slides left from the label position into the flush edge, the distance derived from `--ds-reasoning-align` (matrix + gap, the same sum the rail geometry uses) — consumers flip `streaming` off and drop the prop; the transition is the component's job.

Inside the panel, a list is the trace's node list: one thinking step per item, no bullets (the rail is the marker), `--gap-500` between nodes — the same beat the panel opens with — and new nodes rise in as they mount, so a trace that builds while an agent works animates without any consumer wiring.

### SourceChip

**`ds-source-chip`** — A numbered citation pill linking a claim to its source: a borderless `--radius-pill` pill on `--color-bg-container-secondary` holding a leading slot and, `--gap-200` after it, a source title in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-secondary`, the title truncating with an ellipsis at `24ch`. The leading slot is never empty and is always one of three things, each in an `--icon-size-500` box: the citation number (`index`) — a `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*` `tabular-nums` numeral in `--color-text-on-inverse`, centred in a solid `--color-bg-container-inverse` circle (the digit itself, not its line box: the numeral's box is trimmed to cap height and baseline with `text-box`, since a line box's unused descender room would set a lining figure high), so it reads dark on light in dark mode and light on dark in light mode and carries the weight a logo's full-colour disc does; else the source's `logo` — an image URL or a custom element, cropped to the same circle over a `--color-bg-container-tertiary` ground, so a transparent favicon still reads as a badge; else a glyph in `--color-icon-primary`, the `language` globe unless `icon` names another Material Symbol. The slot is tucked into the pill's start: the chip's padding is `--padding-100` above, below and before the slot and `--padding-300` after the title, and the circle's radius is the pill's less that inset, so the two are concentric and a squared theme squares the badge with the pill. With an `index` set, the `logo` still leads the preview's top line.

The root is an `<a>` when `href` is set, a `<button>` when the chip has a preview but nowhere to link, and a plain `<span>` otherwise. The anchor and the button are the interactive forms, and they take Chip's passive wash: `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` on hover and `--color-action-passive-bg-active` while pressed, the text stepping to `--color-text-primary`, over `--motion-duration-fast` `--motion-ease-standard`. The numeral's inverse disc holds through every state; the wash moves behind it. `text-decoration: none` throughout — the pill shape is the affordance — and focus draws the house `--color-action-primary-bg` ring. The plain span keeps `cursor: default` and no hover. It renders inline after a sentence or in a wrapping sources row under an assistant answer, pairing with ChatMessage's footer slot.

An `excerpt` gives the chip a source preview: a panel on the floating surface (`--color-bg-page-primary`, `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` hairline, `--shadow-floating`, `--radius-300`, capped at 320px) holding a top line in `--color-text-tertiary` (the `logo` when there is one, in the leading slot's circle and decorative because `source` carries the name; then `source`, then `meta` after a middle dot, and an `arrow_outward` at `--icon-size-500` when the chip is a link), the full untruncated title in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*`, and the excerpt in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-secondary`, clamped to four lines. It opens like HoverCard: hover or focus with the shared `MOTION_HOVER_SHOW_DELAY_MS`/`MOTION_HOVER_HIDE_DELAY_MS` timings, staying open while the pointer is inside the panel; a press opens it at once, which is how touch reaches it. Escape or a press outside dismisses it, and only one preview is ever open: a chip held open by keyboard focus gives way when the pointer opens its neighbour. While it shows, the chip holds its hover step. The panel is `role="tooltip"` and the chip points at it with `aria-describedby`, so the excerpt is read with the title whether or not the panel is showing. Placement is measured, since a chat's message list clips its overflow: above the chip and flush with its leading edge, flipped below when the space above is short, and shifted sideways until it sits inside the viewport and every clipping ancestor. Hidden state is `display: none`, transitioned discretely, as HoverCard's is.

### SourceTrail

**`ds-source-trail`** — The research trail under an agent's answer: the sources it opened, in order, each row a SourceChip with a status indicator and an optional contribution note. The shell is ToolCall's exactly — `--color-bg-container-primary` behind a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border` at the chat furniture's shared `--radius-600`, header and list inset `--padding-500` from the sides, the panel on the same `0fr → 1fr` grid collapse with `visibility` handling — so a collapsed trail reads as one more row in a run of tool calls rather than a different species. The header is a disclosure button carrying a computed count ("Reading 3 sources" while anything is pending or active or while `streaming`, "Read 3 sources" once settled; `title` overrides it) in `--font-paragraph-sm-emphasis-*`, a leading indicator (a `tone="inherit"` Spinner while unsettled, `travel_explore` at `--icon-size-500` once done), and ToolCall's rotating `expand_more` chevron. Each row's visually hidden status reads from `statusLabels`. The computed header comes from `readingLabel(count)` ("Reading N sources") while any visit is unsettled or the trail is streaming, and `readLabel(count)` ("Read N sources") once every visit has settled; a `title` replaces both.

Rows are an `<ol>`: per-item status follows AgentPlan's vocabulary — `circle` for `pending` (the whole row at 0.6 opacity: not opened yet), a Spinner for `active` in `--color-status-info-icon`, `check_circle` for `done` in `--color-status-positive-icon` — each with visually hidden status text announced beside the title. The chip is a real SourceChip: `href` makes it a link (opened in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`), `icon` fills its leading slot (the globe when unset), and the `detail` note truncates in `--font-paragraph-sm-*` `--color-text-tertiary`. While `streaming`, an appended row eases in over `--motion-duration-slow` `--motion-ease-standard` — pure CSS, so the reduced-motion token collapse stills it; there is deliberately no JS-timed stagger. Disclosure is the established trio (`open`/`defaultOpen`/`onOpenChange`, default open), with `useId`-derived trigger and panel ids.

### StreamingText

**`ds-streaming-text`** — The reveal for text that arrives in chunks: an LLM response typing itself out. It owns no text style — `white-space: pre-wrap` and `overflow-wrap: break-word` on an inline span that inherits the surrounding typography, so it drops into a ChatMessage or Prose paragraph without changing its face. Feed it the accumulated `text` on every render and it animates through what was appended from a `requestAnimationFrame` loop that spends real elapsed time, so the pace is frame-rate independent and a fast stream renders at most once per frame. The rate scales with the backlog rather than being fixed: a thin trickle types at `MOTION_STREAM_FLOOR_CPS` (70 characters per second, from `tokens/motion.ts`) and however much is waiting is fully on screen within `MOTION_STREAM_DRAIN_MS` (250ms), so a dumped paragraph catches up in a beat instead of typing for seconds; `floorCps` and `drainMs` tune both per instance (`charIntervalMs`, the retired interval prop, maps to the floor when `floorCps` is not set). A value that does not extend the previous one is a new message and reveals from zero, while text present on mount shows whole. The engine is published headless from the same folder — `useStreamReveal` (declarative) and `createStreamReveal` (imperative), via `components/StreamingText/useStreamReveal` — for a consumer whose streamed text needs its own renderer: the site chat paces its markdown through `createStreamReveal` and renders it itself. The cursor is a `0.55em × 1em` `currentColor` block at `--radius-050` blinking on a stepped opacity keyframe at `--motion-duration-loop-spin`, shown while `streaming` or while the reveal is behind. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the JS reveal is skipped (each chunk appears whole) and the token collapse stills the cursor. `onRevealComplete` fires once when the reveal catches up after streaming ends. Announcement is the container's job — pair with `aria-live` when the surrounding UI does not already announce the message.

### ThreadPanel

**`ds-thread-panel`** — The session-history rail of a chat or agent product: brand header, a new-thread action with an optional shortcut hint, standing control rows, the grouped thread history, and a footer for the profile row and host furniture. It began as the agent-workbench template's threads panel and graduated once the shape proved out. Deliberately **surface-less**: the host paints the band it sits on (the workbench template lays it on its own zone tones), so the component fills its container's height and scrolls exactly one region — the history `<nav>`, behind a tucked-away thin scrollbar (`scrollbar-width: thin`, no track fill) that only shows its `--color-divider` thumb under the pointer. A project row with no `icon` wears its generated PixelAvatar, the default project mark, seeded from the label (or from `pixelAvatar`, a stable id, so a renamed project keeps its character); the panel spreads the inks across its projects with `distinctPixelInks`, so neighbours differ in colour as well as shape. A pinned row reads `pinnedLabel` (default "Pinned") after its title and any status, never on a `pending` row, since the pin mark itself is decorative. Each row's options name, `threadMenuLabel`, takes a string (read as "label: title") or a function of the title, for languages that put the title elsewhere.

The rows are AppSidebar's shared pill shape with the inset moved up a level: the panel itself carries `--padding-300` inline padding in both states, and every 40px `--radius-pill` row fills the padded column at `width: 100%` — one hover shape for the whole surface, every fill stopping at the same 12px breathing room. Rows hold `--padding-200` inline padding in both states: the 20px seat (12 + 8) is what the collapsed circle's math needs (12 + 8 + 24 + 8 + 12 = the 64px rail) and what the expanded panel's marks keep — the logo and the pen icon's box share it — so no mark shifts as the rail sweeps; the 32px avatar sits on the same axis from a `--padding-100` inset. On the trailing edge the meta captions, the profile caption and the collapse toggle end on one rail, the toggle pulled out by `--gap-100` so its glyph's ink, not its box, meets it; on the left the correction runs the other way: the glyphs stay in their boxes, centred under the logo, and the text-led content (group labels, thread rows with their dots, the more row, the rename field) takes `--padding-300` at its leading edge, a bearing deeper than the icon seat, so labels and dots sit under the pen's visible edge. The toggle washes a `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` circle on hover and carries a Tooltip of its label, opening leftward because the rail clips its overflow (collapsed, the native title stands in). Rows sit one `--gap-100` beat apart, hover washing to `--color-action-passive-bg-hover` over `--motion-duration-fast` `--motion-ease-standard`, focus drawing the house `--color-action-primary-bg` ring inset. The new-thread row is deliberately as quiet as the control rows — the leading position and the glyph carry the affordance, not a fill — wearing `edit_square` by default, the same pen-in-a-box chat headers use for New chat (`newThreadIcon` overrides), with compact Kbds for the decorative shortcut hint (the host owns the actual key binding). Control rows carry `--color-icon-primary` icons and `--font-paragraph-sm-*` labels. Thread rows share the action rows' 40px shape exactly, stacked a hairline (1px) apart so adjacent fills never merge into a slab: a one-line title and an optional trailing cluster — a `--color-icon-secondary` session glyph (`icon`, e.g. `cloud`), a `keep` pin mark (`pinned` — a marker only; the host groups pinned threads and offers pin/unpin through the row menu), and the `meta` caption in `tabular-nums` — with an optional status dot leading the title: a `decorative` `xs` StatusDot drawn from the thread's `status`. The five statuses follow StatusDot's reading — `idle` a hollow neutral ring (nothing new, nothing running), `working` a pulsing neutral dot (an agent is running now; monochrome like AgentStatus's working states, colour reserved for the states that ask something), and `unread`, `waiting` and `error` solid dots in the info, warning and error roles (finished and unseen, blocked on the reader, stopped on a failure) — so no state rests on motion alone: under reduced motion `working` parks as a solid dot, still distinct from the ring. The dot column is all or nothing: once any thread carries a status every row draws a dot, the rest reading `idle`, so titles share one left edge, while a panel with no statuses stays dotless; a `pending` row in a dotted panel reads `working`. The dot is decorative, so every status but `idle` is also read aloud after the title through `statusLabels` — except on a `pending` row, whose accessible name is its tooltip alone (the boolean `unread` survives as a deprecated alias). A `description` puts a quiet second line under the title — the `--font-caption-*` face on `--color-text-tertiary`, the row wrapping to `height: auto` at `min-height: 40px` with its gap gone column-only so the lines sit close, the second line indented past the dot's column when the panel has one — under the same trailing mask as the title, so detail rows and plain rows fade out the same way. A long title dissolves under a trailing `--gap-1000` mask (the alpha-keyword gradient, ChatThread's manner) rather than clipping to an ellipsis, with the full text in the row's native tooltip — the fade is also where the overflow trigger lands, so revealed text and control never fight. The active row (matched by the controlled `activeThreadId`) fills the column with `--color-bg-container-tertiary` and announces `aria-current`. Group labels (and the projects header) are the `--font-overline-*` face set one step down at `--font-caption-size`, keeping the overline's line-height so the rhythm holds, aligned to the text seat a bearing inside the rows' icon seat, carrying the rows' own `--padding-200` vertical inset — as if the label sat inside a row — so the overline keeps the pills' rhythm instead of hugging its neighbours; the more row is a quiet `--font-caption-*` reveal that brightens one text step on hover. The brand header takes a `--gap-300` bottom margin, seating the logo evenly between the panel's top edge and the new-thread row.

Thread rows carry a real history's lifecycle, all of it controlled from outside. A `pending` thread is one whose name is still being generated: a full-width `--radius-pill` shimmer bar at 0.75em height, inset only by the row's padding and softened at its trailing end by the title mask (`--color-bg-container-secondary` under Skeleton's sweep at `--motion-duration-loop-shimmer`; the component restates the sweep rather than composing Skeleton, whose `role="status"` label would hijack the row's accessible name) holds the title's place, the given title — typically "New chat" — stays as the row's tooltip and accessible name, and the row shows no menu until the name lands. `threadActions` (with `onThreadAction`; a thread's own `actions` overrides the set) hangs an overflow menu on every row: a 28px circular `more_horiz` trigger absolutely positioned over the faded trailing edge — the `<li>` is the positioning frame, since a control cannot nest inside the row button — opening the compact DropdownMenu aligned end, destructive rows in the menu's own error styling. The trigger rests at opacity 0 with `pointer-events: none` and reveals only on row hover, on `:focus-within`, and while its menu is open (held via `:has([aria-expanded='true'])` — the value is load-bearing, since the closed trigger carries `aria-expanded="false"` — which also keeps the row's hover wash on) — never standing on a resting row; the trailing cluster (glyph, pin, meta) fades out as the trigger fades in, since they share the trailing edge. `renamingThreadId` swaps that row to the inline rename field: an uncontrolled input wearing the row's exact shape and `--font-paragraph-sm-*` face with a `--border-025` `--color-action-primary-bg` border (the active-input colour), prefilled and selected on mount. Enter commits the trimmed value through `onThreadRename`, Escape cancels through `onRenameCancel` (both hand focus back to the row), focus leaving the field commits too, and an empty or unchanged value cancels rather than committing — half a title is not a title.

Between the standing controls and the history sits the optional projects section: rows sharing the controls' exact anatomy (the project's generated PixelAvatar by default, any Material Symbol via `icon`, an optional `meta` caption that fades with the labels) under their own `--font-overline-*` header carrying the group labels' seat — its vertical inset stepped down to `--padding-150` so the 24px new-project button (`add`, rendered only when `onProjectCreate` is wired) doesn't grow the header past the label-only group rows below. The active project (matched by the controlled `activeProjectId`) takes the same `--color-bg-container-tertiary` fill and `aria-current` as an active thread. Collapsed, the header fades out whole (opacity plus `visibility`, the history's manner) while the project rows fold to icon circles like the controls, keeping the section present on the rail.

Fully controlled and stateless — the host owns selection, navigation, the expanded state, the pending flags, and the rename state. Every row follows Button's element pattern (`<button>`, or `<a>` when an `href` is given), each section renders only when its props do, and the component carries no `'use client'`, so a link-driven history renders from a Server Component; the menu and rename affordances take callbacks, so they belong to client hosts by nature. The footer composes Avatar for the profile row; `footerSlot` seats host furniture like a theme toggle above it.

Collapse is AppSidebar's choreography wholesale: `expanded` (default true) with `onExpandedChange`, whose presence renders the header's toggle (`left_panel_close` / `left_panel_open`, announcing `aria-expanded`). The panel owns its width — 280px expanded, the 64px rail collapsed, AppSidebar's own constants — transitioning on `--motion-duration-slow` `--motion-ease-emphasized` while every label stays mounted and fades on `--motion-duration-base` `--motion-ease-standard` under the sweeping clip, so inner layout never snaps. Collapsed, the toggle folds to zero width and the logo takes over as the expand button (AppSidebar's `role="button"` pattern; with no logo the toggle stays standing and centres on the rail's axis, the empty brand slot standing down) — and the rail's dead space is an expand target too, a click anywhere that is not a row reopening the panel — the rows read as circular icon buttons, the `footerSlot` fades out, and the history — whose thread rows have no icons to collapse to — fades and leaves the accessibility tree and tab order via `visibility`. The host pairs the state with its own band, which simply follows the panel's animating width.

### ThreadTabs

**`ds-thread-tabs`** — The strip of open chat sessions across a conversation's top edge: 32px `--radius-pill` pill tabs with an optional leading glyph (`--icon-size-500`, `--color-icon-secondary`), ThreadPanel's status dot (the same `xs` StatusDot and the same statuses, except that an `idle` tab draws none: a strip has no left edge to keep, so only a state worth a glance takes the room), a `--font-paragraph-sm-*` label, a hover-revealed close button, and a trailing 28px new-tab action. Deliberately **surface-less** like its sibling rail — the host paints the band it sits on. Labels sit on `--color-text-secondary` and step up to `--color-text-primary` on the active tab, whose pill fills with `--color-bg-container-tertiary` — the same "on stage" mark as ThreadPanel's active row, so the strip and the rail speak one language. Hover washes `--color-action-passive-bg-hover`; a long label caps the tab at 220px and dissolves under a trailing `--gap-400` mask with the full text in the tab's tooltip (the panel's manner); a crowded strip scrolls sideways with no scrollbar. Not an ARIA tablist: like the panel's rows these are a labelled list of buttons selecting sessions, the active one announcing `aria-current`. Arrow keys, Home and End move focus along the strip; Delete closes the focused tab when `onTabClose` is wired. Each tab's close name, `closeLabel`, also takes a function of the tab label, for languages that put the label elsewhere.

The close button is geometry-stable — a 24px circle that always holds its seat and only fades in (`--motion-duration-fast`) on tab hover, keyboard focus within, or the active tab, so revealing it never shifts the label; closing from the keyboard hands focus to a neighbour before the tab leaves. Enter and exit are the component's own choreography, the grid-track idiom turned sideways: each item is a one-column grid animating between `0fr` and `1fr` (with its own leading `--gap-100` margin folding in and out), so an added tab glides open in place on `--motion-duration-slow` `--motion-ease-entrance` and a removed one — held on stage in a leaving list until its animation ends — folds away on `--motion-duration-base` `--motion-ease-emphasized`, the neighbours sliding over as the column collapses. No measurement, no FLIP; the initial set renders calm, only tabs that arrive later animate. Fully controlled: the host owns which tabs exist and which is active.

### ToolCall

**`ds-tool-call`** — The record of one tool invocation. A skimmable header row — status indicator, monospace tool name (`--font-family-code`, the same sanctioned monospace context CodeBlock uses), summary, status word, `tabular-nums` duration, chevron — over a collapsible body holding the arguments and result. Container is `--color-bg-container-primary` at the chat furniture's shared `--radius-600` shell with a `--border-025` `--color-bg-container-border`, header and body inset `--padding-500` from the sides; a closed row rounds to a capsule the way a one-line bubble does, echoing the system's pill buttons. The panel uses the same `0fr → 1fr` collapse and `visibility` handling as Reasoning.

Four statuses: `pending`, `running`, `success`, `error`, mapping to the warning, info, positive and error roles through two channels — the status word in the `--color-status-*-text` tokens, the indicator glyph in the `--color-status-*-icon` tokens (the plain-surface step; see the Status section). The container stays neutral so a long run reads as a list rather than a wall of tinted cards — the border is the standard container hairline in every status, and the status word and icon carry the state. `running` renders a `Spinner` at `tone="inherit"`, the rest a Material Symbol (`pause_circle`, `check_circle`, `error`) at `--icon-size-500`.

The `actions` slot — allow, deny, always allow — renders in a single-tone footer on the card's own background, separated by whitespace alone, *outside* the collapsible panel, so answering an approval request never requires expanding the call first. With no children the header renders as a plain `div` rather than a button, so a row with nothing to disclose does not look pressable.

### UsageCard

**`ds-usage-card`** — An agent's budgets at a glance: the context window plus any number of plan or rate limits, one compact Meter row each, with reset captions. It wears the chart family's shared card chrome — the root composes `ds-chart` (and `ds-chart--bare` via `bare`), the header is `ds-chart__header` with the family's title and subtitle styles — the Gauge/FunnelChart precedent of chart chrome outside the `Chart/` folder, though the component registers under the ai category because its vocabulary is context windows and agent budgets. Rows stack at `--gap-500`; each pairs a `size="compact"` Meter (label left, readout right, `valueText` overriding the percentage for token counts and spends) with an optional `resetLabel` caption on the bar's trailing edge in `--font-caption-*` `--color-text-secondary`.

Fills recolour through Meter's status variants as a level crosses the card's thresholds — `info` until `warning` at 0.8 of max, `error` from 0.95, both fractions tunable via `thresholds`, mirroring Gauge's threshold idea — and an item's own `variant` pins the derivation aside (a frozen quota can stay `neutral` at 100%). Everything visual belongs to Meter and the chart chrome, so the component adds no tokens and no motion of its own; the a11y contract is Meter's `role="meter"` per row. Deliberately server-renderable: no `'use client'`.

### Waveform

**`ds-waveform`** — Voice made visible: a row of `--radius-pill` bars that dance while audio is happening. The animated states are pure CSS choreography on the shared twelve-slot cycle (`--motion-duration-loop-matrix`, the same loop AgentStatus's dot patterns run on): each bar carries a centre-weighted peak (`--ds-waveform-amp`, outer bars reaching lower so the wave reads as one shape) and a cycle slot (`--ds-waveform-step`, adjacent bars two slots apart, applied as a negative delay so the peak travels the row and every bar is mid-wave on mount). `state`: `idle` parks the bars low in `--color-text-tertiary`; `listening` runs the wave at roughly half height; `speaking` at full — one keyframe set, scaled per state through `--ds-waveform-peak`. `levels` switches to controlled mode for a real analyser: the CSS wave stops and each bar eases to its 0–1 level over `--motion-duration-fast`. Deliberately monochrome like the agent working states (`--color-text-secondary`), tintable through `--ds-waveform-color`. Height rides the icon scale (`--icon-size-600` at `default`, `--icon-size-500` at `compact`), and each bar's width is a fifth of `--icon-size-500` at both sizes. Root is `role="status"` with a visually hidden state label (defaults per state, `label` overrides); the bars are `aria-hidden`. Under reduced motion the wave parks on its resting contour rather than strobing. Purely presentational — no `'use client'`.

### ChatWidget

The chat is the package's fourth kind of thing, between the components and the templates, and its only widget: a complete AI chat a consumer mounts end to end and configures with props, shipped as `rift-ds/chat` (the composition and the engine), `rift-ds/chat/server` (the handlers), `rift-ds/chat/upstash` (the guardrails) and `rift-ds/chat/demo` (a scripted conversation for a preview that needs no key). `src/chat/registry.json` is its registry; the Chat widget row in CLAUDE.md's Registries table owns the fields and the validator. Three components make the widget: `ChatProvider` holds the conversation (the transcript, a stream in flight, the draft, the open state, the placement and the thread lifecycle) and is mounted once, high in the tree, so client-side navigation never remounts it; `ChatWidget` is the surface, filling whatever hosts it; `ChatLauncher` is the floating button and the fixed panel in its docked, overlay, floating and takeover forms. Under them sits the engine, importable on its own by a host that wants the behaviour under its own markup: `useChat` (the state machine), `useThreads` (the thread and tab lifecycle), `createFetchTransport` and `scriptChat` (the two transports), and the `ChatEvent` protocol in `src/chat/types.ts`, which the server handler and both transports are type-checked against.

The library components the widget composes are listed under `parts` in `src/chat/registry.json`, the one home of that list, which `scripts/validate-chat-widget.mjs` holds to the widget's own imports in both directions. Two of them have roles worth naming: `AiButton` is the launcher, and `PixelAvatar` is the agent's mark on the header toggle and the pane's portrait. Its class block is `ds-chat-widget` (`ds-chat-widget__*` for its elements, `ds-chat-launcher__*` for the launcher's chrome), deliberately not `ds-chat-*`, which the library's chat components already own. One stylesheet, `ChatWidget.css`, published as `rift-ds/chat/chat.css`; every value in it is a semantic token or a sanctioned restatement (the sub-display step it restates for its container width carries the `typography` directive). Every control name the widget writes is a `*Label` prop with an English default (the header and rail controls, the scrims, the model picker, send, the reply row's copy and thumbs, and the follow-up row), so a consumer can localise everything it announces; ChatLauncher passes them through `widget`. Every word it writes on screen is a prop too, with its English text as the default: the composer's context note (`contextNote`, given the `contextLabel` title), the drop overlay's limits line (`attachHint`), the welcome greetings (`greetings`), the Chat position menu's rows (`placementLabels`), the agent pane's default status (`agentStatus`), and the artifact panel's empty state (`artifactPanelTitle`, `artifactEmptyTitle`, `artifactEmptyDescription`). Interpolated copy is a function prop, fixed copy a string, and a keyed set takes a partial object, so a key left out keeps its English. A host that seats the built-in parts itself gives each its words directly: `ChatArtifactPane` takes `emptyPanelTitle`, `emptyTitle` and `emptyDescription` for its empty state; `ChatAgentPane` takes `profile` (whose `status` is the line the widget fills from `agentStatus`) and `closeLabel`; `ChatTabs` takes `ariaLabel`, `closeLabel` and `statusLabels`. The words its composed parts write are props too, one per component, named `<component>Labels` and typed from that component's own props (`composerLabels`, `attachmentDropZoneLabels`, `attachmentGroupLabels`, `chatThreadLabels`, `modelPickerLabels`, `artifactPanelLabels`, `agentRailLabels`, `questionCardLabels`, `questionSummaryLabels`, `codeBlockLabels`, `promptSuggestionsLabels`): each key replaces one string and a key left out keeps the component's English. The threads rail takes its words through `threadsProps` (`threadPanelLabels`, `actionLabels`), the tab strip through `tabsProps`, the reasoning summary through `reasoningLabel`, and the launcher's summary panel through ChatLauncher's `aiButtonLabels`. The headless `useThreads` takes `labels` for the names it writes into its state (the row actions, and a new project's name and meta), so a host on its own UI localises them too.

**A transport is the whole integration.** `(messages, signal) => AsyncIterable<ChatEvent>`: the fetch transport parses the NDJSON a `createChatHandler` route streams, a script plays a conversation written in `scriptChat()`, and a host's own transport yields whatever it likes. The events are `status` (a label and an optional reasoning trace point), `model`, `models` (the server's default and locked wire values, which the picker follows), `delta`, `exchange` (the server's log id, whose presence is what shows the thumbs), `followups`, `content` (a React node, for in-browser transports), `card` (serialisable rich content a server can send, drawn by the provider's `renderers` by kind), `question` (questions the agent asks with answers to pick), `notice`, `error` and `done`, which always comes last. A `content` or `card` event draws on the live reply the moment it arrives, after the reasoning and before the prose, so a tool's result shows while the answer still streams, and stays with the turn when it commits. The hook paces deltas through `createStreamReveal` and renders notices and errors whole; an `error` replaces streamed text, so the handler only ever sends one before the first delta. `CHAT_EVENT_KINDS` enumerates the protocol for documentation that lists it.

**Rich content inside a turn.** A reply may carry a card: the server handler forwards one a tool returns, a script attaches one to a reply, and `injectTurn` stages one directly. `ChatProvider`'s `renderers` map a card's `kind` to the component that draws its `data`, so a chart, a plan, a diff or an interrupt card renders where an agent's structured output belongs: after the reasoning, before the prose that reads off what it shows. The demo's `DEMO_RENDERERS` draw every kind its story uses; a kind with no renderer draws nothing, because a card enriches an answer that already stands in text.

The composer's leading action is a `ModelPicker` over the host's `models` list. The pick is a request, not a command: it rides on each send as a wire value, and the handler resolves it through the day's budget tier before calling a model. The picker shows the visitor's pick, or the server's current default when they have not made one (which is how a budget step-down moves the label with no click), and an entry the budget has locked stays listed but disabled, its description swapped for `lockedModelDescription`, so the lineup reads as "paused", never "gone".

**The rails.** `threads` seats a session-history rail: `true` for the built-in `ChatThreads`, a `ThreadPanel` over the provider's `useThreads` (a thread is born from the first utterance and lands at the top of the home group already shimmering, named a beat later; the row menu pins into the leading pinned group, renames inline and deletes; selecting a thread moves the selection, never the transcript), or a render prop handed `{ overlay, close }` for a host-shaped panel. The widget measures its own width and seats the rail by what it finds: at `THREADS_RAIL_MIN_WIDTH` (720px) and up an inline rail, below that a header `menu` button opening a scrim-backed slide-over sheet, so the mode follows the container, not the viewport. Growing into rail territory retires the sheet; Escape settles the sheet before the panel, one layer per press; with the inline rail showing, the header's New chat stands down because the rail carries its own. `tabs` seats a `ThreadTabs` strip under the header (`true` for the built-in `ChatTabs`, synced to the same lifecycle: a fresh thread arrives with a tab, closing the last returns to the greeting). `aside` is the trailing edge's mirror: a render prop for a host-shaped `AgentRail`, or `agent={{ tabs, profile }}` for the built-in `ChatAgentPane`, inline once the widget measures `ASIDE_RAIL_MIN_WIDTH` (760px) and otherwise raised from the header's agent toggle as a bottom sheet capped at 82% of the widget's height, with a decorative grabber. The floating card's move handle and size grips are pointer-only by decision, like the dock's widen grip: the Chat position menu is the keyboard route to every seat, so the grips are a convenience over it, never the only way, and an audit does not raise them. The inline pane glides open and closed rather than appearing, in the artifact panel's manner: the rail's width runs from nothing to the pane's own on the slow duration with the layout easing the threads rail uses, the card is the rail at every frame (a whole rounded card growing from the trailing edge, the pane revealed behind its leading border) and fades in behind the growth, and closed it stays mounted but inert and invisible, so the toggle reads as one motion in either direction. A rail holds a width of its own, so the widget measures the pane and hands the stylesheet the target as `--ds-chat-aside-width`. The panel is one piece of state, resting closed and opened only because someone asked: a click on the toggle, or the host's `agent.defaultOpen`, which opens it at mount and only where the widget is wide enough to seat it inline (a widget that mounts narrower never raises a sheet for it); leaving rail territory closes it rather than handing it to the other seat, and the artifact panel follows the same rule. The header's agent toggle wears the agent's own `PixelAvatar`, seeded by the widget's title and the same mark the built-in pane's portrait defaults to, so the control and the panel it opens read as one character; it holds its seat whether the panel is up or not and says which, wearing the passive wash while on; the whole control cluster sits at the card's right edge, ahead of the rail in the DOM (The controls' one home, below, owns that rule). These three slots are visual staging: switching a thread, a tab or a pane moves the selection, never the transcript. A fourth, `widgets`, belongs to the welcome screen alone: any content under the composer, gone with the first message. `widgetsLayout` says how it takes its room (`fit` keeps the welcome to one viewport, `scroll` turns it into a page) and `widgetsWidth` how wide (`column` on the composer's box, `wide` past it).

**The artifact panel.** `artifacts` seats the trailing edge's other tenant: an ArtifactPanel for something the chat made that wants more room than a turn. An artifact is a `ChatArtifact` (an id, a title, a kind, and whichever of an HTML document, a URL, source and renderer data it carries) and it travels as an ordinary card of kind `artifact`, so any transport that can send a card can send one and the event protocol gains nothing. The thread draws it as an ArtifactCard through the exported `ChatArtifactCard`, which opens its artifact and, pressed again, closes it; `ChatArtifactPane` is the panel half, an ArtifactPanel drawn from the artifact, and both are exported for a host building its own thread. The pane is controlled by design: the widget (or a host seating the pane itself) owns the seat's state, which artifact is on stage, whether the panel is up and whether it is expanded, and passes `artifact` and `expanded` in, hearing back through `onExpandedChange` and `onClose`. The header carries an artifact toggle beside the agent's (`right_panel_open` / `right_panel_close`, the same passive wash while on) that opens the conversation's newest artifact, or an EmptyState when the conversation has not made one yet, and closes whatever is up. A kind named in the provider's `artifactRenderers` is live React content; otherwise `html` is framed as a sandboxed document with its source behind the Code view, `url` is framed only when it is an http(s) URL or a same-site path, and `code` alone shows as source. An artifact that arrives while the visitor is watching opens itself where there is room to seat it inline; in the sheet's territory it waits for its tile, because a sheet nobody asked for covers the answer that announced it. Every road back to an empty thread takes the artifact off stage.

The seat is the agent rail's, exactly, and so are its rules. The trailing seat has one threshold for both tenants: at `ASIDE_RAIL_MIN_WIDTH` and up the artifact is an inset `--radius-600` bordered card beginning under the header row with `--padding-500` to the trailing and bottom edges, as the agent pane is, and below it each rises as a bottom sheet. The artifact's card glides open on the slow duration as its flex share runs from nothing (a little the larger of the two columns at rest), and its sheet stands at 92% of the widget's height rather than the agent pane's 82% cap, because a preview has no natural height to hug. Both trailing panels stand on `--color-bg-page-primary`, solid in either seat, where the widget's own surfaces and the threads sheet are glass: a panel is a place to read and work in, and what shows through glass would sit under its content. The trailing seat holds one panel at a time: an artifact on stage stands in for the agent pane, which returns when the artifact closes if it was up, and asking for the agent swaps them. Under `ASIDE_WITH_THREADS_MIN_WIDTH` (1040px) the inline threads rail yields its room to whichever trailing panel is up, the agent pane as much as the artifact, and goes back behind the header's hamburger; and a widget that narrows out from under either panel closes it rather than raising a sheet nobody asked for. The card's leading edge is a size grip, the dock's widen grip restated: a 20px grab zone straddling the border with a hover-revealed 4×48px pill, dragging the panel's width while both columns keep `ARTIFACT_MIN_COLUMN_WIDTH` (320px); pointer-only by the same decision as the other grips. The panel's own expand toggle gives it the whole widget, the conversation and the history leaving the row and the tab order, and Escape steps it back. Both trailing panels carry a close of their own in the top trailing corner as well as their header toggle.

**The controls' one home.** Wherever the widget is wide enough to seat a trailing panel inline, the header's control cluster lives at the card's own top trailing corner for good, over the header row with no panel up and over the panel's top inset with one, and the chat header keeps a spacer of the cluster's size so the title still stops short of it. The cluster used to move between the header and that corner as a panel opened and closed, which remounted every button and, on the way out, landed them on a column that was still narrow; one home means nothing moves. For the same reason the header's New chat is keyed on the threads rail having room, not on whether an open artifact has borrowed it, so the cluster never gains a button because a panel opened.

**The launcher's panel** is a floating card: `position: fixed` against one viewport edge (the right by default) at `--ds-chat-width` (420px at rest; `DOCK_MIN_WIDTH` mirrors it), inset `--ds-chat-inset` (20px) from the top, bottom and docked-side edges, at `--radius-600` with the container border and `--shadow-floating`, on the `--color-bg-glass` surface behind a `backdrop-filter: blur(24px)`. The three variables are declared on `:root` by the stylesheet and are the one contract a host's own CSS reads (the site aliases its `--layout-chat-*` to them). Docked, a hover-revealed grip on the inner edge drags the width up to `DOCK_MAX_WIDTH` by rewriting `--ds-chat-width` inline on `<html>`, so everything a host sizes from it slides with the edge. Opening fades the card in with a 10px rise on `--motion-duration-slow` / `--motion-ease-entrance`; closing mirrors it on `--motion-duration-base` / `--motion-ease-standard`, the provider holding the panel mounted for `MOTION_EXIT_SYNC_MS` with pointer events off. The move between geometries is a glide on `--motion-duration-slow` with `--motion-ease-emphasized`; fullscreen view drops the inset and radius and raises the blur to 40px.

**The three responsive modes are mandatory for every AI chat surface, not only the site's.** At `DOCK_QUERY` (1440px and up) the panel docks: the launcher sets `data-chat="docked"` (or `docked-left`) on `<html>`, the host's stylesheet pads the page beside it, and the panel is a non-modal `complementary` region with no scrim and no focus trap. Below that it is a modal overlay: `role="dialog"`, `--color-scrim` behind it, focus trapped, body scroll locked through the library's shared counted lock (so a Dialog and the panel can close in either order), Escape closes. Under `TAKEOVER_QUERY` (720px) it is not an overlay at all: the panel leaves the fixed layer for normal flow at `100dvh`, the launcher sets `data-chat="takeover"` and the host hides the page around it, so the chat is the document and the soft keyboard is the browser's problem rather than the panel's (a viewport-sized fixed panel is exactly what iOS Safari renders short under the keyboard). The widget's `phone` layout stacks the welcome there: greeting centred in the empty thread, starters directly above a bottom-pinned composer; short-viewport container queries drop the welcome dressing in order, greeting first, then starters. A host reserves the panel's width only at the dock threshold, never below it, or the page squeezes to a sliver beside the chat. Two exceptions: a panel seated inside a frame of its own answers to that frame, and a screen where the chat is the page holds the takeover form at every width with `closeEnabled` and `fullscreenEnabled` off. `TemplateAssistant` restates the modes over the same thresholds (`src/chat/queries.ts`).

**Placement is an option on top of the pattern, not a fourth threshold.** With `placementEnabled`, the header carries a Chat position menu whose three rows are `CHAT_PLACEMENTS` (dock right, dock left, floating; `src/chat/placement.ts` is the roster): the dock side is the docked mode mirrored, and floating is a non-modal card the visitor moves by its header and sizes from eight hover-revealed grips, between its resting size and `FLOAT_MAX_SCALE` of it, never closer than `FLOAT_EDGE_GAP` to a viewport edge. Full screen has no placement, so the trigger goes inert there; a phone has neither menu nor grips. A host that offers placement implements every seat the menu names; one that does not leaves the prop off.

**The welcome screen** greets by the visitor's clock (`greeting.ts`, read as a store so prerendered HTML never bakes in a build machine's hour) over the host's `tagline`, with `starters` under the composer: a few pre-written questions the host draws from its pools with `pickStarters` and the provider's seed (rolled per load and on New chat, never between), staged with `PromptSuggestions`' pending shimmer for `MOTION_SUGGESTIONS_THINK_MS` the first time a `startersKey` shows and standing still on every later open. Every suggestion the chat shows, starter, follow-up or launcher chip, is held to `SUGGESTION_MAX_CHARS`: a chip never wraps, and one that will not fit is dropped rather than clipped.

Answers render as markdown through `Prose`, fenced code as `CodeBlock`. Model output is untrusted, so only an href the provider's `isInternalHref` accepts (same-site slug paths by default) becomes a link, drawn by `renderLink` (by default the nearest `LinkProvider`'s link component, else a plain anchor; a host may pass its own), and following one closes a panel that covers the page. Each committed assistant turn carries a copy action and, when the server logged the exchange and the provider has a `feedback` sink, thumbs up and down that post the exchange id and the verdict optimistically. Under that row sit the follow-ups: the same `PromptSuggestions` row the welcome uses, arriving a beat after the answer commits from the provider's `followups` source (the built-in fetcher against the follow-ups handler, or a host's own), the pending shimmer shown only under the newest settled turn, and a result that comes back empty substituted with `fallbackFollowups`, because a shimmer that resolves to nothing reads as a failure. Once a chip is sent the row keeps the choice, through `PromptSuggestions`' `value`, with QuestionCard's own treatment: the sent chip outlined solid in `--color-text-primary`, the rest faded to 0.4 behind a dashed `--color-text-tertiary` line, all inert. No indicator is added, so a chip keeps the shape of the line of text it is; a picked option differs by its indicator and its input surface, so the transcript shows which way the conversation went in one visual language.

`flow` passes straight to the thread: `top-down` (the default, the send choreography) or `bottom-up` (the classic thread), offered as the Flow lever in the playground's Chat setup.

**Quick replies: suggestions and questions.** A reply ends in one of two things, never both. Follow-up chips are the visitor's voice, guessed after the answer by a small model. A question is the agent's, asked by the model that wrote the answer because the answer stopped for want of it: it arrives as a `question` event carrying a `ChatQuestionSet`, and a turn that carries one is never asked about for follow-ups. The set renders as a `QuestionCard` straight under the prose, above the actions row, live only while its turn is the newest and nothing streams; passed by without an answer it stays, inert, as a record of what was asked. Answering records the answers on the asking turn and sends them as the next message: one question reads as the plain bubble, several picks joined with commas, and a set as a `QuestionSummary` in its `plain` variant inside the bubble, the same record the asking card folds to. The message the model receives is plain text (`serializeReply`), so the transport and the server are unchanged. A set of more than one question holds the composer, disabled under `questionsPlaceholder`, with focus moved to the card's first option and Skip as the way out; one question never does, because typing instead is a fine answer to it. Every set passes `sanitizeQuestionSet` (`src/chat/questions.ts`) in the handler and again in the hook: option labels are held to `SUGGESTION_MAX_CHARS`, two to five options, at most four questions, and anything malformed is dropped, never repaired. The reply text asks in words too, so a dropped set still reads correctly.

The `disclaimer` line pins to the widget's floor in `--font-caption-*` on `--color-text-tertiary`: text a visitor can find but never has to read.

**The server half** mirrors the client's contract. `createChatHandler` takes the host's system blocks (with `cache_control` on the corpus block), models and budget roles, tools (`{ definition, execute, tracePoint }`, where `execute` may return a card), the opt-in `askUser` (the built-in `ask_user` tool, whose guidance lives in its description so the cached system prefix is untouched; a well-formed call streams a `question` and ends the exchange with no further model call, a malformed one goes back to the model as an error), limits and copy, and returns a web-standard handler that streams the protocol above; guardrail outcomes are 200 plus a `notice`, non-2xx is reserved for malformed bodies, the tool loop is bounded by `maxModelCalls` with tools withheld on the last pass, and the `exchange` id streams only when a `ChatGuardrails.onExchange` logged the line. `createFollowupsHandler` and `createFeedbackHandler` are its siblings; `createUpstashGuardrails` implements the `ChatGuardrails` hooks over Redis (per-visitor rate limits, a daily breaker with model tiers, the exchange log and the feedback store), failing open when unconfigured. The Next route files a host writes, or `npx rift-ds init chat` writes for it, own the segment config and the environment reads; the package imports nothing from a framework.

### Site header (pattern)

The header is website chrome, not a library component: `MegaNav`, mounted by each page rather than from the root layout (the footer and the chat are the layout-mounted chrome), and absent from the chromeless routes. It is two bars sharing one markup pattern: the in-flow header at the top of every page, and a `position: fixed` sticky overlay that slides in on `--motion-duration-slow` / `--motion-ease-standard` once the in-flow bar has scrolled out, so nothing in the page shifts. Both carry the brand, the primary links, the Design system mega panel, the input-dressed search button that opens the site palette (⌘K's visible twin, labelled with the palette's own "Search or ask anything" placeholder), the brand switcher and the theme toggle. The bar folds twice as it narrows, measured on its own width rather than the viewport's (both bars are a `site-header` size container), so a docked chat panel folds it exactly as a narrower window would: at a 1440px bar the search pill collapses to its icon circle, and at 1184px the pills, switcher and toggle hand over to the hamburger drawer rather than clipping. Those are the old 1536px and 1280px viewport steps less the body gutters and a classic scrollbar, so an undocked bar folds where it always did. When the chat docks, the sticky bar's padding grows by the panel's width so its nav items stay over the shifted page, and that padding is what narrows the container. The links sit left-aligned beside the brand — a `--primitive-gap-1000` margin plus the first pill's own padding sets the gap — rather than centred on the page, and the wordmark carries the system's one weight-700 setting so the name reads a step heavier than the pills. The current section's pill takes the action selection convention — `--color-action-primary-bg` fill with `--color-action-primary-text`, hovering to `--color-action-primary-bg-hover`, the same treatment as SegmentedControl's active segment — because the earlier neutral fill was the hover colour too, and vanished into the bars' glassy backdrops. Desktop pills only: the drawer — the sole nav for everything at 1279px and under — keeps NavList's own selected styling, and its floor row carries the brand switcher beside the theme toggle.

**The sticky bar's backdrop is a progressive blur, not a single sheet of glass.** A `backdrop-filter` is uniform across the box it sits on, so a tint gradient fading to transparent over one 20px blur still ends in a hard line where the blur stops, which is the glass-edge look the first version had. A `mask-image` on the same element multiplies both the tint and the filtered backdrop by its alpha, and that is what lets the blur itself dissolve. So the bar carries a `.stickyBackdrop` stack of four absolutely positioned layers reaching `--sticky-backdrop-reach` (64px) below the bar: blur radii rising 2, 6, 12, 24px from the bottom layer to the top, each masked over a band that shrinks as the blur grows (the 2px layer fades across the whole reach; the 24px one is gone by 40% of it), so the page is sharp at the foot of the veil and fully frosted behind the links. The tint rides on the bottom layer alone, `--color-bg-page-primary` at the top stepping through `color-mix` to transparent, in both themes: dark's page surface is the near-black floor, so the bar reads as one with the viewport edge rather than as a lighter panel over the page. The mask keywords are the one sanctioned off-token colour in the file.

The stack is child layers at `z-index: -1` inside the bar's own stacking context, never a filter on the bar itself: a backdrop-filter on the bar would make it a backdrop root, and the mega panel inside it (its own `blur(32px)` glass over an 88% container surface, denser than the chat's because it lands on display type) would then sample only the bar's subtree instead of the page, and its blur would silently die. The phone drawer keeps a single uniform `blur(24px)`, since a full-height sheet has no edge to dissolve.

### Site footer (pattern)

The sitemap footer is website chrome, not a library component: `SiteFooter`, mounted once from the root layout inside a pathname gate (`SiteFooterMount`), so every page carries it without a per-page import. It skips the chromeless routes (`website/src/config/chromeless.ts`), the same set the chat panel denies.

A `FadeDivider` on top — the shared 1px hairline that fades to `--color-bg-container-primary-transparent` at both ends, the same line the home hero and the DS landing draw — then a brand block beside four columns holding five link groups under a `<nav aria-label="Footer">` (Site, Design system, Docs, Foundations, Elsewhere), then a quiet bottom row. Four groups derive from the nav config (`getSectionItems`, `docsSidebarLinks`, `foundationsSidebarLinks`) and `website/src/config/social.ts`. The Site group is hand-listed in `SiteFooter.tsx`, because the top-level nav links live in MegaNav's own markup rather than in the nav config — the one duplication in the footer. Group names sit in `--font-title-body-weight` at `--font-paragraph-sm-size` on `--color-text-primary`; links are `--font-paragraph-sm-*` on `--color-text-tertiary`, hovering to `--color-text-primary` on `--motion-duration-base`. External links carry a trailing `open_in_new` at `--icon-size-500`. The brand block holds the same brand mark and wordmark the header wears, at the same 24px — with one deliberate divergence: the header wordmark runs the system's lone weight 700 (the Site header pattern above), while the footer's keeps `--font-title-body-*`, and it is opacity that sets the footer's back (0.5, full on hover and focus), not a smaller or greyer variant — plus the social icon row, which renders whatever `SOCIAL_PROFILES` in `website/src/config/social.ts` holds (36px targets, `currentColor` at `--color-text-tertiary`) and disappears entirely while that list is empty, as it is today. The bottom row carries two items: the copyright and the author credit, both `--font-paragraph-sm-*` on `--color-text-tertiary`, left-clustered so the fixed chat button never covers either, and wrapping to two lines on phones where the same button takes the corner (extra bottom clearance there for the same reason). The credited name and its URL are `AUTHOR_NAME`/`AUTHOR_URL` in `scripts/brand.mjs`, the one home for both. Its link keeps a permanent underline where the column links carry none: a link in a column reads as a link from its position, a name inside a sentence does not.

**The brand block is what makes the columns line up, and that is its structural job as much as its brand one.** It is exactly `--layout-sidebar-width` wide, separated from the links by `--layout-column-gap` — the docs shell's own rail and gutter — so the link grid begins on the same vertical line the page's content does. On a docs page its four columns land pixel-for-pixel on the four content cards above them; on a page with no rail it reads as an ordinary brand block with the columns starting after it. Five equal columns stretched across the whole shell was the version that never sat right: the pages divide that same width differently (three-up on the home page, four-up on the docs pages), so the footer's column edges landed a few pixels off theirs — 2px off the last card, 30–50px off the middle ones, and near-misses read as mistakes where a clear offset reads as a decision. Anchoring the grid to the content line means it either matches exactly or is offset by a whole rail, and the footer stays **identical on every page**: nothing in it reacts to which shell a page uses.

Five groups fit four columns because Site and Design system share the first one — the pairing that keeps the four columns closest to even. (A link flagged `desktopOnly` in the nav config hides below 960px, so a column's length can differ by viewport; the footer still reacts to nothing page-specific.) Below 960px the rail is gone from the pages too, so the brand block stops reserving its width and sits above the links, the stacked pair unstacks (`display: contents`) and all five groups flow as columns — 3 + 2 there, 2 + 2 + 1 on phones. As a body flex child the footer inherits the docked-chat inset and pins to the viewport bottom on short pages via `margin-top: auto`. Focus rings are the sanctioned `--color-action-primary-bg` outline; the action colour appears nowhere else in the footer.

---

## Light / Dark Theme Contract

The theme is activated by `data-theme="dark"` on the HTML root element. The `tokens-light.css` applies under `[data-theme="light"], :root`. The `tokens-dark.css` applies under `[data-theme="dark"]`.

**Tokens that invert:**
- `--color-bg-page-primary` (light floor → near-black floor)
- `--color-bg-container-*` (light neutral steps → dark neutral steps)
- `--color-text-primary` (near-black → near-white)
- `--color-text-secondary`, `--color-text-tertiary` (dark grays → light grays)
- `--color-divider` (light semi → dark semi)
- `--color-input-bg-primary`, `--color-input-border-primary`, `--color-input-text-primary`
- `--color-action-passive-*` (near-transparent fills swap to dark semi-transparent)
- `--color-bg-container-inverse` / `--color-text-on-inverse` (the inverse surface — ImageCompare's handle label, the "today" markers — flips from near-black to near-white)
- `--color-scrim` (0.5 → 0.7 black); `--color-control-thumb` chains to the on-action ink in both themes, so its per-theme value follows the action plan
- `--shadow-floating`, `--shadow-modal` (shadow opacity increases in dark mode)

- `--color-action-primary-*` — the action family is split by mode by design: each mode pairs its own fill with its own label, because one colour rarely clears contrast against both page floors (see Action / Brand)

**Tokens that stay stable:**
- Status border colors (`--color-status-*-border`) — the same key in both modes (`neutral` steps one notch lighter in dark)
- Status background/text invert to their dark counterparts for contrast

**Rule:** Never hardcode `color` or `background-color` with a hex value in a component. Always use a semantic token — the theme swap is the only mechanism for dark mode, no manual `prefers-color-scheme` queries in components. One sanctioned exception: ColorPicker's colour-mixing constants (the white/black overlay gradients, the hue spectrum, the picker-handle white) are colour-space physics no theme token can represent — they are written as `hsl()` literals, commented in place, and documented in the ColorPicker spec.

---

## Do's and Don'ts

### Do
- Use semantic tokens (every `CATEGORY_PREFIXES` category — `--color-*`, `--radius-*`, `--gap-*`, `--padding-*`, `--border-*`, `--font-*`, `--motion-*`, `--icon-size-*`, `--shadow-*`) in every component. Never use `--primitive-*` tokens directly.
- Reserve `--color-action-primary-bg` for primary CTA buttons, focus rings, active input states, the checked or on state of a form control, the selected item of a mutually exclusive set, and the hover signal of a surface that is itself a control. The one other sanctioned use is as the lead series colour in charts; nowhere else.
- Take heading weights from the type tokens: the display tier for marketing text (a light 300 in the base theme), the heading tier for in-app headings (600). The weights are the theme's; never set one on a page.
- Apply `--radius-pill` to all buttons, `--radius-300` to all inputs and standard containers, and `--radius-600` to Card/EntityCard navigation tiles. The assignment is the system's shape signature; the values are the theme's.
- Map all feedback UI to the five-variant status system (`info`/`positive`/`warning`/`error`/`neutral`) — Badge, Alert, Toast, ProgressBar all share the same semantic tokens.
- Prefer `--color-divider` for rule lines over custom border colors.
- Use Material Symbols Rounded for icons, sized with `--icon-size` set to a scale step (`sm` 20 / `md` 24 / `lg` 32 / `xl` 48). Never set `font-size` on an icon directly.
- Always wrap your app in `ToastProvider` before calling `useToast()`.

### Don't
- Don't use primitive tokens (`--primitive-neutral-05`, `--primitive-blue-07`, etc.) in components or page styles.
- Don't write a `prefers-reduced-motion` query in component CSS — the duration tokens already collapse under it. The one exception runs the other way: that guard is CSS and cannot see a JS-driven loop or timer, so a JS-driven surface has to check the query itself (ShaderField's render loop and Carousel's autoplay are the worked examples) — or ride a library default that does, as the recharts charts do through `isAnimationActive: 'auto'` (the chart-set spec owns that contract).
- Don't use the action colour decoratively. Using it on text or illustrations dilutes its CTA signal. (Chart series are the one sanctioned exception: the action colour leads the palette.)
- Don't set `display` size typography (Mega/Display tokens) inside app UI pages — reserve them for the marketing homepage hero only.
- Don't bold display sizes at the use site. The display weight is the theme's call (300 in the base theme), and the use site never overrides it.
- Don't invent a fourth surface tone outside page / container-primary / container-secondary / container-tertiary. The neutral ramp is intentionally short.
- Don't add `box-shadow` to standard cards and containers. Depth comes from background steps, not shadows. The one exception: interactive Card tiles lift with `0 8px 24px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)` on hover — a deliberate navigational affordance, not an elevation pattern to copy elsewhere. Floating surfaces and modals use `--shadow-floating`/`--shadow-modal`.
- Don't hardcode colors. Every color must be a semantic token so dark mode works without extra code.
- Don't break the five-status system by adding a sixth variant (e.g. "brand") to Badge or Alert. Use `neutral` and customize within the component page if needed.
- Don't disable the `data-theme` swap on any subtree — all components must participate in theme switching.

---

## Responsive Behavior

### Breakpoints
Breakpoints have one home: `src/tokens/breakpoints.ts`, published as `rift-ds/tokens/breakpoints`, with the site's own families in `website/src/config/breakpoints.ts`. Each value is the last width of the narrower side, so `max-width: N` and `min-width: N + 1` both name it, and JavaScript builds its queries from the module (`mediaBelow`, `mediaAbove`) rather than restating a number. Each module also describes its steps as data (`BREAKPOINTS`, `SITE_BREAKPOINTS`), which `/foundations/layout` renders as its Breakpoints section. The canonical layout set is **1279 / 1151 / 959 / 768 / 600**, all `max-width`. The rail widths and column gap live as `--layout-*` custom properties in `globals.css`, so the mid-breakpoint narrowing applies to every page at once (`/foundations/layout` draws the shell from the declared values and lists every other `--layout-*` property):
- **≥ 1280px** — full shell: 291px left nav (`--layout-sidebar-width`), 320px right rail (`--layout-rail-width`), 60px column gaps (`--layout-column-gap`).
- **≤ 1279px (mid)** — both rails narrow (nav 240px, rail 280px) and the column gap tightens to 40px, keeping the center column at a readable measure.
- **≤ 1151px** — the right details rail stacks below the main content (per-page media query on the `resumeLayout`/`updatesLayout` flex row); the left nav stays.
- **≤ 959px (tablet)** — the left nav hides (its links move into the header drawer's section accordions) and pages collapse to a single column.
- **≤ 768px (mobile)** — **the token layer's only breakpoint**: display-tier typography and section-rhythm spacing collapse (see Typography → Hierarchy and Spacing → Gap Scale).
- **≤ 600px (phone)** — body side gutters tighten; dense grids switch to horizontal scroll inside their own blocks.

Beside the layout set, the module names the steps components switch at: **480px (sheet)**, where the modal overlays drop their floating margin, and the chat widget's three thresholds: the takeover at 719px, the dock above 1439px, and the short-viewport height at 619px (see ChatWidget). The site's own families are the template screens' 1080px and 760px steps and the immersive stages' 1099px compact step.

A threshold outside these sets is allowed only when it is content-driven (a bespoke grid that breaks at its own natural width) or deliberately sits on a step's other edge, and it says so in place with a `/* ds-allow(breakpoint): <reason> */` directive directly above the query. `scripts/validate-breakpoints.mjs` fails the build on any `@media` width, or media-query string in TypeScript, that is neither in the set nor sanctioned, and holds the layout set stated above to the module.

The thresholds stay raw numbers in the stylesheets, and that is settled rather than pending: CSS custom properties cannot drive `@media` conditions, and a preprocessor dependency is not worth it for a handful of documented literals. The module is the number's home and the validator is what keeps the literals honest; the rail widths and gutters they switch are tokenized (`--layout-*`).

### Typography Collapse
- Handled by the token layer at ≤768px — Display 2 64px → 40px, Sub Display 30px → 24px, Mega/Display 1 proportional. No per-page overrides.
- In-app headings (H1–H3, 30–22px) hold at every viewport; never reduce H3 below 20px.

### Layout Collapse
- `AppLayout` sidebar collapses to off-canvas/drawer on mobile.
- Component index grids reflow from 3-up (desktop) → 2-up (tablet) → 1-up (mobile).
- `SelectionCard` groups reflow from side-by-side to stacked on narrow viewports.
- Table components allow horizontal scroll on mobile rather than reflowing columns.

### Touch Targets
- All interactive elements minimum 44 × 44px effective touch area (WCAG 2.5.5).
- Button default height: ~36px from padding + line-height. Add `min-height: 44px` if used as standalone tappable button on mobile.

---

## Iteration Guide

1. **Adding a new component variant** — Add a modifier class (e.g. `.ds-badge--brand`) and reference only `--color-*` semantic tokens. Add corresponding light and dark values to `tokens-light.css` and `tokens-dark.css` if a new semantic meaning is needed.
2. **Adding a new status** — Add `--color-status-{name}-bg`, `--color-status-{name}-border`, `--color-status-{name}-text` to both theme files. Then add the variant to Badge, Alert, Toast, and any other status-bearing components.
3. **Adding a new spacing step** — Add to `tokens-primitives.css` first, then alias in `tokens-light.css` only: spacing is not theme-split, and `tokens-dark.css` carries nothing but colour and shadow.
4. **New typography style** — Add to `tokens-typography.css` following the `--font-{name}-{property}` naming pattern.
5. **Reference a component token** — Always use the full CSS variable, e.g. `var(--font-heading-1-size)`. Never inline the resolved value.
6. **Storybook stories** — Each component must have a `.stories.tsx` file with a Default story and one story per meaningful variant. Use `data-theme` Storybook theme toggle to verify dark mode.
7. **Before shipping** — Run `npm run verify`, the full local quality gate — CLAUDE.md's CI & Local Verify section owns the step list; every Storybook story renders in headless Chromium as a smoke test, with any story `play` function asserting behavior on top. The same checks run automatically in CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) on every push and PR, including a drift guard that fails if generated documentation is stale.

---

## Known Gaps

- **Figma parity** — The system originates in Figma ([robr0-ds26](https://www.figma.com/design/8NzqDS8iRsBTFPbNGj3Woj/robr0-ds26)), and foundation/component pages carry `figmaUrl` deep links to specific frames — currently hidden site-wide behind `SHOW_FIGMA_LINKS` in `website/src/config/social.ts`, because the file lags the coded system. Keeping the Figma file and the coded tokens in sync is still a manual process — there is no automated export pipeline.

Three former entries left this list as decisions rather than work: JS-driven timings now share one home (`src/tokens/motion.ts` — see Motion → Migration status), the raw breakpoint literals are settled as raw (see Responsive Behavior → Breakpoints), and form-level validation is permanently the consumer's form layer, not the system's (see the Input spec).
